Surface Orders, Cyclic Time and the Arithmetic Spectral Bridge
Dessins, resolutions, Auslander–Reiten theory, quantum models and verifiable research

Amelie Schreiber
Research development edition

November 3, 2026

Reader’s guide and mathematical
status
This book develops the surface-order and cyclic-time constructions of the supplied Emergent cyclic
time manuscripts together with the subsequent projective-resolution, arithmetic and spectral work.
The originals remain immutable. Their final unconditional GRH claims do not survive the identified
arithmetic and operator-theoretic gaps. Here every construction has a specified category, coefficient
field and determinant target. Established results, conditional implications, computed experiments
and open comparisons play different roles.
The first two parts give the shared article constructions and their expanded proofs. The next
part develops entire AR components, corrects the face-unfolding and Hom-control claims, and relates
valid meshes to derived triangles and determinantal semi-invariants. The physics part retains the
original topics while replacing invalid positivity, hardware and continuum inferences by proved
finite statements or explicit research models. Computational chapters supply trained finite neural
architectures, higher-genus dessins and a local interactive companion. The final comparison problem
remains an independent arithmetic theorem, not a consequence of the pictures or learned parameters.
A reader may start with cellular dessins and the node, then projective resolutions, then the
AR atlas. The more advanced arithmetic chapters use character sums and Fredholm determinants.
The physics chapters can be read with finite-dimensional linear algebra and elementary quantum
mechanics. Each computational claim has a script, seed and stated backend. The genus-two and
genus-three sheet views are projections; the torus views are topological embeddings, not asserted
uniformizing metrics.
The provenance manifest records the source hashes and chapter inventory. Its location is
book/source-provenance.json. The review records state the audit coverage. The supplied 2053
file is byte-identical to the pinned source called COMPLETED in the source clone; the other
supplied COMPLETED file is a shorter, different version. This naming discrepancy is retained in
the provenance record. No filename certifies a theorem.
The companion is served on this machine at http://127.0.0.1:8765. It contains adjustable
dessins, category-labelled AR diagrams, quantum carrier circuits, and saved training curves. The
private repository remains the authoritative source. Selected lemmas are checked in Lean4; a full
formalization of this entire book has not yet been completed.

Part I

Constructions and statements

1

Chapter 1

Cellular dessins and their infinite
orders
We start with the surface-order constructions of Schreiber [1, 2], retaining the original sources
unchanged. The generic-resolution source [3] is background, not a substitute for exactness of a
particular complex. All primary algebra examples below come from explicit clean Belyi maps.
Finite-dimensional objects occur as modules or specified quotient strata of those infinite algebras,
not as replacements for them. The initial arithmetic target is a primitive nonprincipal Dirichlet
L-function. Finite-order Hecke characters, Dedekind zeta functions and general Artin representations
require separate comparison theorems; Artin holomorphy is not assumed.
A clean dessin on a compact oriented surface is specified by a nonempty finite dart set H, a
fixed-point-free involution α, and a permutation σ such that ⟨α, σ⟩ is transitive. Contract the white
vertices of degree two. The resulting cellular graph has vertices the σ-orbits, edges the α-orbits,
and faces the ϕ-orbits, where
τ = σ −1 ,

ϕ = ασ −1 ,

2 − 2g = # cyc(σ) − |H|/2 + # cyc(ϕ).

Write [h] = {h, αh}. Our quiver has vertices these edges and arrows ah : [h] → [τ h]. Paths are
composed in travel order: ab traverses a first. Set
A = kQ/(ah aϕh : h ∈ H),

J = A>0 ,

Λ = lim A/J m .
←−

Each vertex has two incoming and two outgoing arrows. Each arrow has precisely one permitted
successor aτ h and one forbidden successor aϕh ; these are distinct because α has no fixed points.
The same holds for predecessors. This is the locally gentle condition. Every permitted cycle repeats
indefinitely, so A is infinite dimensional. We reserve “gentle” without qualification for the convention
additionally imposing finite dimension.
Proposition 1.1 (The actual split order). For a permitted cycle c of length ℓ, let Bc be the hereditary
cycle order in Mℓ (k[[uc ]]): diagonal and forward matrix entries are in k[[uc ]], and entries crossing
Q
the chosen cycle cut are in uc k[[uc ]]. Then Λ is the suborder of B = c Bc whose two diagonal
constant terms labelled h, αh agree. With R = k[[u]] acting by u 7→ (uc I)c , it is finite free of rank
P
2
c ℓ(c) ; the conductor contains uB.
Proof. In a cycle order a path of length less than ℓ is the corresponding matrix unit, multiplied
by uc exactly when it crosses the cut; another full turn multiplies it by uc . Thus completed
paths in each permitted branch give all entries of Bc . An arrow belongs to only that branch,
2

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

1

whereas e[h] has diagonal entries at both occurrences h, αh. Hence the only condition on branchwise
formal coefficients is the displayed equality of diagonal constants. This proves both injectivity and
surjectivity of the path-normal-form identification. Multiplication respects it: switching branches
across a paired edge is precisely a forbidden pair. The quotient B/Λ is killed by u, so the submodule
has the same generic rank as B. It is torsion free and finite over the DVR R, hence free of that rank.
The path and u-adic topologies agree: uA ⊂ J, and if L is the longest permitted cycle, any path of
length at least L + 1 is u times a shorter nonempty path. Thus J L+1 ⊂ uA. This also justifies the
completion used in the identification.
The path-degree variable q below is not u: one full turn has path degree ℓ(c) but u-degree one.
Formal, Laurent and operator-norm completions will likewise remain distinct.
Here are five explicit global inputs. Permutations list darts starting at zero; E = |H|/2.
Surface and Belyi map β

σ

α

(g, rankR Λ)

The first is the nodal interval: A = k[x, y]/(xy) and Λ = k[[x, y]]/(xy). The second has A =

Projective resolutions, dual dessins
and determinants
Use right modules Pi = ei A, Si = Pi /Pi J. Internal shift M ⟨n⟩ places its old generator in degree n.
Let Rn (i) be the indexing words of length n from i with every adjacent pair forbidden, including ei
for n = 0. Such a word is usually zero in A; it still labels a summand.
Theorem 2.1 (Exact resolutions). There is a minimal graded projective resolution, with P0 (i) = ei A
and for n ≥ 1,
Pn (i) =

M

et(an ) A⟨n⟩,

dn (x)p = an x in the summand a1 · · · an−1 .

p=a1 ···an ∈Rn (i)

The augmentation P0 (i) → Si is the quotient. For the completed order the identical finite-rank
projective terms and formal-series maps resolve the residue simple. Ext and Tor against residue
simples count forbidden words with the prescribed endpoints. In the graded case Ext has matching
internal shift n.
Proof. The maps are right linear and degree zero. Adjacent differentials multiply by a forbidden pair.
The augmentation kernel is the direct sum of aA over initial arrows. For any arrow a, multiplication
et(a) A → es(a) A kills exactly the permitted paths beginning with its forbidden successor f (a). All
other basis paths survive to distinct basis paths. Thus its kernel is f (a)A. For a fixed prefix,
different last arrows have disjoint surviving supports; there is no cancellation between summands.
This proves exactness inductively. For formal series the same assertion holds coefficient by coefficient
and identifies the entire closed kernel, not just its dense polynomial subspace. Terms are idempotent
projectives and all entries are arrows. Consequently applying Hom or tensor with a residue simple
kills every differential, proving minimality and the Ext/Tor count. In A minimality is graded; it
does not require that J be its ordinary Jacobson radical.
The two arms never terminate in this closed class. Their labelled patterns follow ϕ-cycles. An
ungraded positive syzygy tail can have a shorter period by permutation of summands: the nodal
interval has labelled period two but unordered tail period one; the elliptic theta has labelled period
six and unordered tail period three. A simple need not be isomorphic to a positive syzygy; for the
node it is R-torsion, whereas the positive syzygies are lattices.

homological degree n starts in internal degree n. Constant terms are I, so the inverse is two-sided.
Already signed E(q) must not be evaluated at −q again.

another closed dessin algebra, with α# = α, σ # = ϕ−1 , permitted successor ϕ, and forbidden

Proof. α(σ # )−1 = αϕ = τ , so the claimed relations and face data follow directly; the connectedness
group is unchanged. To prove the determinant, put Sv,a = wa when s(a) = v, and Ta,v = 1 when
t(a) = v, zero otherwise. On arrow states let Ua,b = wb for permitted adjacency and Va,b = wb for
forbidden adjacency. Then T S = U + V and the path sum is C = I + S(I − U )−1 T . Sylvester’s
identity gives det C = det(I + V )/ det(I − U ). Each transition is a weighted permutation, whose
cycle determinant is respectively 1 − (−1)ℓ w(c) and 1 − w(c). Everything holds in formal power
series, hence as rational functions. This supplies a direct proof of the Bessenrodt–Holm formula in
the closed dessin class [4].

The last cancellation does not imply finite global dimension: its forbidden resolution is infinite.
These rational functions have cycle poles/zeros, not arithmetic prime Euler factors.
For clarity, a complex is a sequence with d2 = 0, its homotopy category identifies maps differing
by dh + hd, and D(A) inverts maps inducing cohomology isomorphisms. A perfect complex is
represented by a bounded complex of finite projectives. Our infinite simple resolutions compute
derived Hom and tensor but are generally not perfect. The two-term presentation [P1 → P0 ]
is perfect even if its kernel is nonzero. Quadratic linearity is a Koszul statement; it is not by
itself an equivalence between all completed derived categories. Likewise a finite-dimensional gentle
graded-curve theorem [6] cannot simply be transferred to these closed infinite orders. Here the
surface walks explicitly compute the resolution endpoints: τ turns round a graph vertex and ϕ
follows a face. For longer string objects all maps can be computed from the occurrence matrices; a
universal geodesic-intersection classification for this completed category remains a separate theorem.

For I = (x2 , xy, y 2 ) the underlying sequence is also kernel checked in Lean 4 over the actual

image, surjective quotient augmentation, projective free terms, and vanishing of both induced

modules, distinct from the residue action on k. General staircases, graded minimality and the
surface-order coefficient cone remain separately scoped prose arguments.
At grade ν ∈ N2 , Mν has dimension one exactly when no generator degree is componentwise at
most ν. Its comparable-grade map has rank one exactly when both endpoint grades satisfy this
condition. A CW realization takes one loop and attaches a disk once along it at each generator degree.
The active indices form an interval: the x inequality selects a final interval, the y inequality an initial
interval. With c active disks, the cellular map k c → k has every entry one. Thus dim H1 = 1c=0 and
dim H2 = max(c − 1, 0), the latter counting active adjacent syzygies. This auxiliary CW complex is
not the dessin surface.
For the actual nodal order Λ = k[[ξ, η]]/(ξη) put u = ξ + η and E = Λ/u2 Λ. The basis (1, ξ, u, uξ)
u2

of E satisfies ξ 2 = uξ, u2 = 0, η = u − ξ. Tensoring the resolution above with 0 → Λ −→ Λ → E → 0
gives, over B = Λ[x, y], free ranks (1, m + 1, 2m − 1, m − 1). Indeed the scalar resolution stays exact
after tensoring over the field; its degree-zero module is free over Λ on the standard monomials.
Multiplication by u2 is injective there and on all free terms, so the long exact homology sequence of
its mapping cone gives positive-degree homology zero and degree-zero module E ⊗k M . In contrast,
as an R-module this object has ranks 4(1, m, m − 1) in its minimal resolution. Neither finite-length
E nor these parameter rings replace the infinite parent dessin algebra or its lattice category.

2.2

A monotone quantum comparison with exact finite ranks

Proposition 2.4 (One common channel). Let T be a positive trace-preserving linear map between
finite complex matrix algebras. For density matrices ρ, σ, their trace distance d(ρ, σ) = 12 ∥ρ − σ∥1
satisfies d(T ρ, T σ) ≤ d(ρ, σ). For finitely many labelled states, keep fixed vertex birth grades ei . At
(r, e) include vertices with ei ≤ e and edges with distance at most r, and take the flag complex. Then
T gives an identity-on-labels inclusion from the input to the output bifiltration, natural in (r, e).
Proof. Write the Hermitian difference as A = A+ − A− with A± ≥ 0. Positivity, the trace norm
triangle inequality and trace preservation give ∥T A∥1 ≤ ∥T A+ ∥1 + ∥T A− ∥1 = tr A+ + tr A− = ∥A∥1 .
Thus every input edge is an output edge; the fixed births preserve vertices, and all cliques are
6

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

2

preserved. Increasing either parameter preserves the simplices, and every map is the same label
inclusion, so the squares commute. Complete positivity is sufficient but is not needed for this norm
argument.
For a finite inclusion K ⊂ L, work over a field and embed a basis ZK of source one-cycles into
the target edge space. If BL is the matrix of target two-boundaries, the induced H1 rank is
ρ1 (K, L) = rank[BL ZK ] − rank BL .
Its image is (ZK + im BL )/ im BL ; source boundaries already map into target boundaries. This
proves the formula, including empty complexes. Computing a flag two-skeleton suffices for H0 , H1 ,
but does not compute the full flag complex’s H2 . Integer ceilings of scaled exact distances preserve
the inclusion. For numerical distances, frozen integer labels define an exact combinatorial diagram
only after their inequality is checked; this is not an interval certificate of the underlying quantum
distances. None of these finite ranks identifies a completed arithmetic determinant or proves GRH.

2.3

A correct DG model of the infinite node

Use cohomological degrees and an uncurved differential of degree +1. An odd element ε with
dε = 1 contracts the underlying k-complex of every unital left DG module: h(m) = εm satisfies
dh + hd = 1 by the graded Leibniz rule. Thus this example has zero cohomology, contrary to the
original ground-field-resolution claim. An isomorphism on H 0 alone is also insufficient for a DG
quasi-isomorphism; all degrees must be checked.
Proposition 2.5 (Nodal Koszul model). Let k be a field, R = k[ξ, η] and f = ξη and K = R ⊕ Rε,
with |ε| = −1, ε2 = 0, dR = 0 and dε = f . The augmentation K → A = R/(ξη), a + bε 7→ [a], is
a DG-algebra quasi-isomorphism onto the actual infinite nodal dessin algebra. Branch rescaling
ξ 7→ qξ, η 7→ q −1 η, ε 7→ ε for q ∈ k × lifts to a DG automorphism.
Proof. Multiplication is (a + bε)(c + eε) = ac + (ae + bc)ε. The differential kills degree zero and
sends bε to f b. Its square is zero. For two odd inputs the Leibniz right side is f bcε − bf cε = 0; the
other homogeneous cases follow by R-linearity. Multiplication by f is injective in the polynomial
domain. Hence H −1 (K) = 0, H 0 (K) = R/(f ), and all other groups vanish. The augmentation
preserves products and kills boundaries. Rescaling fixes f , so its coefficientwise extension commutes
with d and has inverse obtained by replacing q with q −1 . This is a one-relation Koszul construction
[78, 79].
Replacing R by k[[ξ, η]] gives the completed order: setting each variable separately to zero maps
onto the branch pullback, and the kernel consists precisely of mixed monomials, hence equals (ξη).
Rescaling is generally semilinear over the coefficient ring, rather than linear over the fixed diagonal
DVR k[[u]], u = ξ + η. It preserves that DVR as a subring exactly when q 2 = 1, and fixes it
pointwise exactly when q = 1. No Hilbert norm or arithmetic spectrum follows.
The corresponding Lean file checks the actual exterior-line algebra, differential, sign-Leibniz,
homotopy and polynomial ambient-resolution identities. Full graded/category/completion objects
remain separately scoped. The two theta dessins likewise require endpoint-sensitive Ext: self Ext1 is
zero in both; for the elliptic theta self Ext3 has dimension two, while for the spherical theta positive
even self Ext has dimension two. Outdegree alone does not count self extensions. Minimal A∞
models use additional graded data [77, §3.3]; m3 is not automatically a physical curvature.

Proposition 3.2 (What completion does to invariants). A continuous d-dimensional representation
of Λ kills J d . On the reduced nilpotent representation variety of fixed dimension vector every
invariant regular function is constant. Nonzero-weight semi-invariants need not be constant.
Proof. Continuity with discrete finite-dimensional target kills some J m ; its Loewy filtration has at
most d nonzero strict steps. Choose vertexwise complements to this filtration. An arrow sends a
layer p into layers q ≥ p + 1. Scaling layer p by z p conjugates each component by z q−p . At z = 0 the
representation tends to the zero-arrow representation. Every invariant therefore has the same value
at all points; reducedness and the Nullstellensatz give the ring assertion. In the square example the
scalar N (a) varies on a nilpotent locus and has weight g1 /g0 , so semi-invariants survive. Formal
entries in a projective map are evaluated on this locus by truncating modulo J d , a legitimate finite
polynomial operation.
On the node, u = x+y is regular and A is free of rank two over k[u]. The polynomial presentation
×(u−λ) for λ ̸= 0 is injective and its determinant on N is det(uN −λI), the characteristic polynomial
of uN up to the sign (−1)d . But u−λ is a unit in Λ, with its geometric-series inverse, so those modules
do not extend continuously to the completion. An R-free lattice and its u-nilpotent quotients are
different categories. On a lattice a separately specified endomorphism F has a meaningful relative
characteristic polynomial over R; the scalar base action uI alone does not create Frobenius.

3.1

Young tableaux and a cyclic representation stratum

For a nonempty partition λ ⊢ m, choose a numbering T of its boxes. On V ⊗m let YT = RT CT , the
unnormalized row sum followed by the signed column sum. The classical identity YT2 = hλ YT , where
hλ is the hook product, makes YT /hλ an idempotent in characteristic zero. Its image is Sλ (V ), one
Schur copy, rather than the whole symmetric-group isotypic component [31, 32]. Its character is sλ ,
computed by semistandard tableaux (weak rows, strict columns), and

monomial dessin algebra: Sλ (N (b))Sλ (N (a)) = Sλ (0) = 0 for a forbidden pair. The empty partition
does not have this property: it sends zero arrow maps to the identity of k.

additive or exact; they cannot simply be applied degreewise to a projective resolution. They also
need not preserve the fixed R-action on lattices.

For the interval dessin let X = (x), Y = (y) in Λ = k[[x, y]]/(xy), R = k[[u]], u = x + y. A lattice is
a finitely generated Λ-module free over R.
Theorem 4.1 (The nodal lattice calculation). Every lattice is Λr ⊕ X a ⊕ Y b . The almost-split
sequences in this lattice category are
×y

×x

0 → Y ,−→ Λ −−→ X → 0,

0 → X ,−→ Λ −−→ Y → 0.

In particular τ X = Y , τ Y = X. Multiplication by u between two copies of either row, followed by
reduction modulo u, gives an actual nonzero exact three-by-three diagram.
Proof. Normalize M inside its total quotient module: M = BM , B = k[[x]] × k[[y]]. The conductor
gives uM ⊂ M ⊂ M . Both branch projections of W = M/uM ⊂ k p ⊕ k q are onto. Intersect W
with the two factors; its remaining quotient identifies their two residual quotients. Separate bases
for these pure factors and the common quotient put W into pure first lines, pure second lines and
diagonal pairs. Lift branch bases over the DVRs. These are respectively the summands X, Y, Λ.
Their endomorphism rings are local.
Exactness of the first sequence is ann x = Y . It does not split: a map X → Λ lands in ann y = X,
and its composite with ×x is a nonunit in End X = k[[x]]. Every map Λ → X factors through
this epimorphism, Hom(Y, X) = 0, and every nonunit endomorphism of X is divisible by x and
factors through X ,→ Λ → X. Classification handles all finite sums; a unit X component would
be a retraction. This is right almost splitness. The dual argument for nonsections out of Y proves
left almost splitness. Endomorphisms fixing either structure map have constant term one, hence
are units, proving minimality. Swap x, y for the other sequence. Finally all three terms are R-free.
Multiplication by u is injective and reduction preserves exactness, proving the diagram assertion.
The lattice AR quiver has arrows Y → Λ → X and X → Λ → Y ; the mesh composites are zero.
Its projective vertex Λ is not the endpoint of another nonsplit AR sequence. AR meshes do not
mean that all rows and columns of any drawn grid are short exact.
For a second, explicit family fix n ≥ 3 and the rational Belyi map

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

1, . . . , n, endpoint loops l, r, opposite arrows ai : i → i + 1, bi : i + 1 → i, and precisely the relations
l2 , r2 , ai bi , bi ai . Its single permitted cycle is la1 · · · an−1 rbn−1 · · · b1 ; the order rank is (2n)2 .
Retain one arrow at each edge in an orientation Ω, and kill its opposite. The quotient is
exactly the string algebra Hn (Ω) of Huang–Lin–Su [46]; the killed-arrow ideal contains J n+2 , so
it is also an actual quotient of Λn . Inflation identifies its module category with the full abelian
quotient-annihilator subcategory CΩ of Λn -modules. This is generally not extension closed in the
whole order category. The imported AR property is intrinsic to CΩ , not automatically an ambient
lattice or finite-length AR property. The extended edition gives nine explicit string modules, all
twelve maps and the four exact meshes from the supplied diamond resource, now situated in this
actual dessin quotient.
The other supplied resources also have a precise home. For the interval order the subcategory
annihilated by J 2 is Mod B0 , B0 = Λ/J 2 . This is a domestic string quotient, not itself a gentle
algebra. Inflation is full and preserves purity, and Zg B0 is a closed subspace of Zg Λ. Ringel’s infiniteword/pure-injective program and its proof by Puninski–Prest apply there [43, 44]; the isolated band
xy −1 gives Krull–Gabriel and relative Cantor–Bendixson dimension two by [45, Theorem10.19]. This
is not an ambient rank calculation for all Λ-modules. The parameter in x = J2 , y = λJ2 survives
even though all cycle traces vanish; the pp condition ∃v (z = xv ∧ (y − λx)v = 0) detects it.
These modules are u-torsion and are not nonzero order lattices. Infinite-string and locally finite AR
theorems must retain their distinct finiteness hypotheses; a surviving permitted cycle has infinitely
many paths at a vertex.

4.1

Polynomial strings, completed lattices and the Fukaya category

Crawley–Boevey’s theorem [38, Theorems1.1–1.4] applies directly to the infinite polynomial dessin
algebra: its finitely controlled modules decompose into strings and finite-dimensional bands; its
pointwise artinian modules additionally allow primitive injective bands. For our finite quiver, these
conditions mean finitely generated and artinian respectively. The words may be infinite, but their
modules have algebraic direct-sum bases. This is not a classification of every module or of arbitrary
formal completions.
In fact no nonzero completed-order lattice, restricted to A, meets either finiteness condition. The
polynomial A is finite over k[u]: remove full turns from sufficiently long paths as in Proposition 1.1.
If an R-free lattice were finitely generated over A, it would be finite torsion free, hence free, over the
PID k[u]. Multiplication by 1 − u would then fail to be onto, whereas it is a unit over R = k[[u]].
Also M ⊋ uM ⊋ u2 M ⊋ · · · excludes artinianity. For a concrete indecomposable outside the
ordinary string/band list take k[[x]] with y = 0: its endomorphisms are multiplication by formal
series, so its endomorphism ring is local. Its x is injective and nonsurjective, while 1 − x is invertible;
these properties exclude every pure-x ordinary string or band. The extended edition gives the full
word argument.
The Fukaya connection requires equally precise categories. Lekili–Polishchuk explicitly allow
infinite-dimensional locally gentle algebras, but their equivalence is for perfect dg modules of a
homologically smooth algebra, with specified grading, line field and stops [66, Definition3.1.1,
Lemma3.1.3, Theorem3.2.2]. Every nonempty closed dessin here has both permitted and forbidden
cycles, so its polynomial algebra is neither proper nor smooth by their criterion. This does not
exclude appropriate singular or completed variants; it prevents a direct use of that particular theorem.
Infinite word, graded curve and geodesic are not interchangeable without a surface realization and
boundary/grading data.
There is a genuine positive example inside our setting: the polynomial interval quotient A/(y) =
12

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

4

k[x] is still infinite dimensional, is smooth, and has no forbidden cycle. Its perfect category has the
partially wrapped annulus model with one stop total. In degree zero Perf(k[x]) = Db (modfg k[x]).
Inflation of this quotient is fully faithful on ordinary modules, but not on derived categories:
Ext2A (k[x], k[x]) = k, whereas Ext2k[x] (k[x], k[x]) = 0. The former follows from the alternating y, x
resolution. Thus this quotient bridge does not identify the whole closed dessin’s perfect category
with the annulus category.
Finally, Leavitt path algebras can fail invariant basis number, but need not. The permitted-state
Laurent algebras here have finite-dimensional matrix quotients, and therefore invariant basis number.
The polynomial and completed dessin algebras have their vertex-simple quotients, giving the same
conclusion. The complex Leavitt algebra of Gα below is a dense *-subalgebra of its graph C*-algebra,
on that specified graph; it is not the original gentle algebra with ordinary CK1 imposed.

Work now over C. Ordinary Leavitt and graph C* constructions [47, 48] cannot simply impose our
original forbidden products: if ab = 0 and CK1 says a∗ a = pt(a) , then 0 = a∗ ab = b. Every arrow is
a forbidden successor, hence every arrow and then every vertex is zero. CK2 is not needed. The
ordinary graph-Toeplitz CK1 setting has the same collapse.

is the ordinary Toeplitz algebra of a cycle of length ℓ > 1 equal to Mℓ (T ): their K0 ranks are ℓ and

A graph presentation and an actual pullback

The Fock algebra itself has a graph presentation, on a different graph from the original gentle quiver.

Proof. The two edges at qh have orthogonal initial pieces and orthogonal ranges summing to Qah ,

For the nodal interval, Gα has two loop vertices, each with an exit to the common vacuum sink.

Proof. The section consists of the constant paired diagonal projections. The kernel is the displayed

For an explicit embedding set g(z) = |1 − z|2 /4, choose one cut arrow per permitted cycle, and
send ah to g(z)z ϵh Eh,τ h in its branch, where ϵh = 1 at the cut and zero otherwise. Send ee to the

at one its argument recovers z. Continuous functional calculus therefore supplies every f (z)Ehh
with f (1) = 0. Multiplying by path images and approximating division away from one supplies
every vanishing off-diagonal entry. Together with the constant paired diagonals these generate DH .
Path normal forms in a fixed entry are a nonzero prefactor times a polynomial in wc ; its infinite
range proves independence. Evaluation at one first separates the zero-length coefficients. Finally for

unital map preserves units, proving nonextension.
This is the C* algebra generated by a faithful polynomial representation and its adjoints, distinct
from the completed order and from TA . The norm closure of the polynomial image alone need
not contain those adjoints. Their K groups already differ. The pairing belongs to the labelled
presentation; an unmarked isomorphism class need not recover the ribbon ordering. No arithmetic
descent, Frobenius action or completed-L positive determinant is inferred from this additional valid
gluing model.

For a nontrivial additive character ψ, write G(χ) = x χ(x)ψ(x). Character orthogonality gives

one, whereas Dirichlet L-functions have center 1/2. Local weight-one purity does not determine
either global zero set. The elementary local bound proves absolute convergence of the elliptic Euler
product on Re s > 3/2; the new local identities give no determinant-preserving continuation with
the desired global positive spectrum.

Arbitrary projective actions: an obstruction and a genuine

The preceding tensor test has a stronger replacement. Keep the characteristic-zero completed elliptic
theta order Λ over R = Q[[u]], and write K = Q((u)). All determinants in the next theorem are
ordinary underlying-R determinants; they are not reduced norms.

Theorem 6.3 (Projective determinant multiplicity). Let P be any finitely generated projective right
L
Λ-module and f ∈ EndΛ (P ). Then P ≃ 2i=0 (ei Λ)ni . Let Ai ∈ Mni (Q) be the induced maps on its
three top multiplicity spaces. Then
det(1 − T f )

For any bounded complex of these projectives and any Λ-linear chain endomorphism, its alternating
determinant ratio specializes to a sixth power in Q(T ). Generically, the ordinary ratio is a cube in
K(T ).
Proof. The arrow ideal is the Jacobson radical: its elements admit geometric-series inverses after
subtracting from one, and the quotient is Q3 . A projective’s top lifts to a map from the displayed
sum; Nakayama gives surjectivity, projectivity splits it, and another application of Nakayama kills
the kernel.
Put B = Λ/uΛ. Each ei B has path layers of dimensions 1, 2, 2, 1: the identity, two length-one
paths, two length-two paths, and one length-three path. The two full cycles based at i add to
uei , so become negatives; all length-four paths vanish. A positive-length entry of a projective
homomorphism strictly raises path length. Consequently the associated graded map has six copies
of A = diag(A0 , A1 , A2 ), giving the formula by triangular block determinants. Base change of
determinants justifies specialization. Multiplicativity along filtrations is ordinary linear algebra,
consistent with [69, Tags0FJ9,0GSX]; no purity assumption enters.
Each term’s determinant has constant term one in T , so every denominator has nonzero
specialization. Taking the finite alternating product therefore preserves the sixth power, even
after cancellation. Finally ΛK = M3 (K) × M3 (K); a projective’s branch modules are sums of
three-dimensional simples. A commuting endomorphism acts on each multiplicity space, repeating
every factor three times. This proves the generic assertion.
Thus no such ordinary perfect-complex ratio can equal the constant-in-u quadratic Pd (T ), or
the full local curve zeta Pd (T )/((1 − T )(1 − qT )). For Pd , the valuation at infinity is −2, not a
√
multiple of six. For the zeta ratio, Pd (1) = q + 1 − aq > 0 by the local bound |aq | ≤ 2 q < q + 1, so
its pole at T = 1 is simple. This also covers square prime powers where the numerator may have a
double root. It is an obstruction for the specified ordinary determinant and linear category, not all
spectral or determinant theories.
There is nevertheless a genuine integral functor removing this multiplicity. Choose a permitted
branch and its cycle cut. Its projection Λ → B+ ⊂ M3 (R) makes the column lattice W+ = R3 a left
Λ-module. Define
Φ+ : Perf(Λ) −→ Perf(R),
P • 7−→ P • ⊗Λ W+ .
Bounded projectives compute this derived tensor functor; flatness of W+ on all modules is unnecessary.
Since ei W+ ≃ R, its determinant on ei Λ ⊗Q V with external action 1 ⊗ Md is exactly Pd , whereas
on the regular module it is Pd3 . This is a defined change of determinant functor, not an arbitrary
polynomial root. It does not construct the arithmetic action independently: the already computed
Md is still the input.
The price of this repair is precise. Branch swap α exchanges W+ and W− ; pairing them yields

the stipulated base/lattice data. A descent-compatible arithmetic determinant retaining the required
symmetry and a global positive realization remains open.

Tame descent: a surviving order and a sharper obstruction

We now test the proposed ramified repair rather than assume that its rotation descends. Extend

theorem is needed for the displayed calculation.

The natural arithmetic attempt fails: aI + bC does not commute with Θ when b ̸= 0, as in both
prime-seven Jacobi examples. The original cellular sum-zero plane is not Θ-stable either. Replacing
C by vC does give the descended operator A on L, but
det(1 − T (aI + bA)) = (1 − aT )3 − ub3 T 3 ,
whose special fiber is (1 − aT )3 , not the required local quadratic. These carrier maps are not
automatically Λ-linear arithmetic endomorphisms.
There is a stronger category-level conclusion.

Theorem 6.4 (Full-deck coefficient kernels). Let W0 be a finite R-free left ΛR -module on which
the central u acts by the given scalar. Suppose it has invertible R-linear intertwiners lifting the
order automorphisms σ and α. Then, for some m ≥ 0, every ei W0 has rank 6m. For any finite
projective P and ΛR -linear f : P → P , let Ai be its top blocks. The genuine coefficient functor
ΦW0 = − ⊗L
Λ W0 satisfies
!6m

det(1 − T ΦW0 (f ))

u=0

Y
i

det(1 − T Ai )
k

.

Bounded perfect ratios retain this exponent. Thus this whole class of full-deck, fixed-center finite
free coefficient kernels cannot supply either the single local quadratic or the full local curve zeta.
Proof. In the generic fiber intersect W0 with its two central branch summands to obtain finite free
integral lattices W± ; an integral direct-sum decomposition is not assumed. Rotation preserves them
and branch swap exchanges them. On a branch let the common vertex rank be r. The three arrow
matrices have cyclic product uIr . Their determinant valuations are nonnegative, sum to r, and
rotation intertwiners make them equal. Therefore 3 | r. Branch swap makes the two ranks equal, so
the full vertex rank is 6m.
For the determinant formula, filter W0 /uW0 by the arrow radical, whose fourth power vanishes.
A positive-length entry of f raises the filtration; a top block Ai repeats across its i-th graded
pieces a total of dimk ei (W0 /uW0 ) = 6m times. Triangular determinants give the assertion. Finite
alternating products preserve it. Quadratic degree and the uncancelled simple pole of the full local
zeta exclude the targets as before.
This theorem is sharp: W0 = ΛR has vertex rank six. It requires neither left projectivity
nor left flatness of a general kernel. It excludes this coefficient category, not arbitrary semilinear
correspondences, twisted determinant lines or infinite spectral constructions. The extended edition
identifies the full character-orbit repair with the hereditary normalization and explains why Schur
commutator cancellation is insufficient. All determinant multiplicities here are relative to the stated
k-valued base; restricting a rational quadratic factor to the former Q-base squares it. No arithmetic
descent or global positive determinant has been recovered by this ramification.
The recent NCG literature supplies concrete comparison targets, with strong hypotheses. Connes–
Consani construct finite abelian covers of the adelic Riemann sector with prime-orbit length log p
and arithmetic Frobenius monodromy [53, Theorem3.12]. The prolate/local-factor and moment constructions [54, 55] give particular finite-prime analytic models, not a uniform positive comparison for
all completed L-functions. The zeta-spectral-triple determinant theorem [56, Theorem5.10] assumes
a specified restricted Weil form, simple even bottom eigenfunction and boundary normalization. Its
infinite comparison is still an additional step. The distributional real-zero theorem [57, Theorem6.1]
concerns a particular interval quadratic form, essential self-adjointness on its trigonometric core and
an even simple ground state; a Fock vacuum alone verifies none of those arithmetic identifications.
Deninger’s periodic-packet construction and real-cohomology obstruction [60, 62] further specify,
rather than remove, these issues. Full theorem-level reading coverage and exact versions are recorded
alongside this draft.
A complete approach must construct arithmetic descent and prime actions on actual order/derived
or analytic objects, prove determinant-line and local-factor compatibility, control regularization
and conductor/gamma normalization, and establish a positive realization for that same function.
Finite-field cohomological trace formulas and Deligne purity supply the classical model [20, 21];
alternating duality alone is not positivity or purity. In a number-field construction these are
obligations, not imported conclusions.
23

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

6

For a sharply testable final analytic target, put Fχ (z) = Λ(1/2 + iz, χ). Suppose entire functions
hm (z) det(zI − Hm ), with Hm finite self-adjoint and hm entire nowhere zero, converge locally
uniformly on C to this nonzero completed function. Hurwitz on every disk disjoint from R then
proves all its zeros real, hence the Dirichlet critical-line assertion. Establishing this convergence
with independently constructed Hm is exactly the missing load-bearing claim; a rank-one encoding
or a reformulation does not establish it. Hecke, Dedekind and Artin extensions must state their
analytic scope anew.

6.5

Whole components, derived endpoints and determinant relations

The full node lattice category has precisely the three indecomposables X, Λ, Y and arrows X →
Λ → Y , Y → Λ → X. Its stable category has only X, Y , no irreducible arrows, and suspension
exchanging them; its AR triangles have zero middle term. For the polynomial node at a smooth
support x = λ + t, y = 0, λ ̸= 0, the modules Tr = k[t]/tr form an ambient homogeneous tube:
(π,t)

(t,−π)

0 −→ Tr −−−→ Tr−1 ⊕ Tr+1 −−−−→ Tr −→ 0,

T0 = 0,

τ Tr = Tr .

The shift changes the derived placement, not the quasi-length. These nonzero-central-support
objects do not extend to continuous finite-length modules of the arrow-adic completion. At its
singular point even the candidate pure-branch tube fails ambient almost-splitness: T2y → k cannot
factor through T2x → k, since every intervening map lands in the x-socle, killed by the last map.
The nine HLS strings above sit in an entire intrinsic quotient component Tp,q , (p, q) ∈ Z2 , with
arrows increasing one coordinate and τ Tp,q = Tp−1,q−1 [46]. Its central object E = M (ab) has infinite
projective dimension: restriction to the first corner gives the dual-number simple with its infinite
loop resolution, while restricted projectives are free or zero. Its module AR sequence therefore yields
a distinguished derived triangle but not a derived AR triangle ending at E. Ringel’s four-quarter
wind-wheel tile [43], with infinite-word diagonals and mixed center, is a different category’s quilt; it
is not silently identified with that HLS component or the Belyi surface.
There is no general equation “AR mesh equals polynomial semi-invariant syzygy.” The intrinsic
k(1 → 2) quotient of the infinite Chebyshev algebra has AR row 0 → S2 → E → S1 → 0 and a
free presentation determinant z on dimension (1, 1), so its semi-invariant ring k[z] has no generator
relation. A genuine framed elliptic-square stratum has Z = [x1 , x2 , y1 , y2 ] ∈ Mat2×4 with six minors
and relation
p12 p34 − p13 p24 + p14 p23 = 0.
These minors have the same determinant character for the target GL2 with framed source; they are
not all semi-invariants for the full unframed vertex group. In contrast, the nodal lattice AR row
gives a genuine relative

and characteristic factor (1 − T a)(1 − T (a + ub)). The endpoints have infinite

Finally, the three-vertex theta algebra has no unital surjection onto a six-vertex radical-squarezero circular algebra: its semisimple image has at most three primitive idempotent images. Duplicating face vertex spaces instead gives an exact faithful unfolding which is not full; it does not
inherit quotient or AR-transfer claims. Zwara Hom-control means that the Hom-dimension defect
is bilinear on K0 , not merely a covering direct-sum formula [72, 73]. The book supplies explicit
fixed-dimension counterexamples, corrected circular-complex incidence resolutions, complete drawn
component windows and all category hypotheses. These additions do not supply the arithmetic
determinant comparison.

Proof. Label its basis by 0, . . . , 8 with vertex residues (0, 1, 0, 1, 2, 1, 2, 0, 2), and use matrix units
Eij acting on columns. Set

r = ⟨E02 + E13 , E15 , E35 + E46 , E48 , E68 + E70 , E72 ⟩,

Solving the arrow commutation equations gives exactly these seven basis elements. The ring is
local: an element cI + n is invertible exactly when c ̸= 0, by the finite geometric series. It has no
nontrivial idempotents, so the representation is indecomposable.
A finite-support lift would define an Euler operator D acting by r on the summand at lift vertex
r. Then [D, X] = X and [D, Y ] = Y . Every displayed nonzero arrow is the unique nonzero entry in
its row and column in its own matrix. Its commutator entry is therefore Dii − Djj . Add the six X
equations and subtract the three Y equations. Each diagonal term cancels, leaving 0 = 6 − 3 = 3,
a contradiction. This argument allows arbitrary D, not just a diagonal matrix in the displayed
basis.

Verification and research frontier
The two nodal/invariant Lean files construct the node as an actual subring of k[[X]] × k[[X]]
with equal constants, prove its annihilators, regular common parameter, augmentation, alternating
projective resolution and a bijective pair of power-series coordinates. A second file proves CK1
collapse under the stated permutation/coverage hypotheses, determinant base change, actual 2 × 2
symmetric-square functoriality and relation preservation, a Schur character identity and the fixedparameter lattice failure. These thirty printed theorem types have only standard Lean foundations.
They do not formalize general dessins, abstract Ext/AR/Schur–Weyl, C* exactness, Fredholm
determinants, arithmetic descent or GRH. Earlier formal layers remain preserved with their literal
scopes.
Exact-rational diagnostics independently check the displayed dessins, path identities, Schur
and Young examples and the nine-object grid. These finite checks supplement the proofs, rather
than prove infinite spectral or arithmetic assertions. The next arithmetic target is an exact
descent/Frobenius-compatible construction on the labelled order/Fock data, with a determinantpreserving positive comparison. The local Schur kernel and the simple degree spectrum alone do
not supply it. All source clones and earlier research are retained; the earlier general focused PDFs
are archived at the preceding commit.
Ten further declarations in DessinPullbackComponents.lean check actual matrix-unit identities, nonmultiplicativity of raw diagonal extraction, impossibility of a unital two-state matrix residue
into a nonzero commutative ring, multiplicativity on diagonal fibers, and genuine equal-image
pullback subring/projection components. The analytic suite then contained187printed declarations
at unchanged pins. The circle-function C* model, functional calculus, generation, graph uniqueness
and K-theory remain prose/external-theorem arguments, not hidden assumptions of these component
checks.
Sixteen additional declarations in ThetaJacobi.lean check genuine finite-field MulChar Jacobi
conjugation, norm and rescaling with explicit nontriviality hypotheses; cubic–quadratic noncancellation; the cyclotomic multiplication determinant and positive Gram identity; and actual theta cellular
boundary, homology-inclusion, restriction and rational chain-map matrices. The full analytic suite
now contains203declarations. The point-count proof, deck/Kummer comparison, cohomological
trace interpretation and global arithmetic comparison are not thereby fully formalized.
The final three ThetaJacobi.lean declarations verify the actual conjugation matrix identities
and the real two-dimensional inert-prime determinant obstruction. They do not assume any unknown
zero location or exclude complex/enlarged spectral realizations.
ThetaProjectiveMultiplicity.lean adds five declarations. Arbitrary upper-block matrices
with six identical diagonal blocks have the sixth-power determinant and characteristic-factor
27

formulas. A literal power-series constant-coefficient map specializes matrix determinants, for each
scalar parameter; the coefficientwise polynomial/order interface remains prose. A nonzero quadratic
cannot be a rational-function sixth power, proved by polynomial degrees. Three genuine power
series with product X cannot have the cyclically equivalent residue-vanishing conditions forced by
invertible rank-one arrow intertwiners. The derivation of the path blocks from the full theta order,

Expanded dessin, resolution and
derived calculations
9.1

The cycle order in coordinates

Number a permitted cycle in travel order 0, . . . , ℓ − 1. Map an arrow i → i + 1 to Ei,i+1 for i < ℓ − 1,
and the final arrow to uc Eℓ−1,0 . A path from i to j then gives urc Eij , where r counts cut crossings.
These are linearly independent path normal forms, and every positive-length permitted path has
exactly one such expression on exactly one branch. The full-cycle sum in a branch is uc I. At a
paired edge its two diagonal occurrences come from a single original idempotent; no positive-length
coefficients are identified. This proves the order description including self-pairings within one cycle.
Although the suborder is generically a product of full matrix algebras, it is not their unglued product
at the special fiber.
For the interval this becomes the literal fiber product
Λ = {(f, g) ∈ k[[X]]2 : f (0) = g(0)},

x = (X, 0), y = (0, X), u = (X, X).

Every element has a unique expression a(u) + b(u)x: take a = g and b = (f − g)/X. Thus it is
R-free on 1, x with x2 = ux and y = u − x. The annihilator of x consists of pairs (0, g) with g(0) = 0,
exactly yΛ; the other branch is symmetric. Multiplication by u is injective, coefficientwise. These
are actual formal power-series statements, not conclusions from finite truncations.

9.2

The two-arm complex and its calculations

For an arrow a write f (a) for its unique forbidden successor. The summand in Pn (i) indexed
by the initial arrow a has endpoint t(f n−1 (a)). Its map multiplies by that last arrow. Thus the
resolution can be computed by iterating a finite permutation, while the projectives themselves
remain infinite-dimensional. The Hom complex against a module N is the direct sum of the endpoint
spaces with differentials given by the same arrow matrices. Tensor against a left module instead
uses the same path entries acting on the left. No rank or exactness claim for an arbitrary N is
inferred by counting endpoints alone.
For the nodal residue field the maps are particularly explicit:
diag(x,y)

(x y)

diag(y,x)

· · · −−−−−→ Λ2 −−−−−→ Λ2 −−−−→ Λ −→ k −→ 0.
If xz1 + yz2 = 0, its first branch says the first branch of z1 is zero, and its second branch says the
second branch of z2 is zero. Hence z1 = yw1 , z2 = xw2 . The same annihilator calculation proves
30

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

9

the alternating kernels in all degrees. Projectivity is genuine: the terms are regular modules or their
finite direct sums. It is not a finite free resolution, despite the period of its positive tail.
For the elliptic theta the quiver vertices are v0 = {0, 3}, v1 = {1, 4}, v2 = {2, 5}. The arrows
a0 , a3 go v0 → v2 , a2 , a5 go v2 → v1 , and a1 , a4 go v1 → v0 . The two forbidden arms from v0 are
a0 , a5 , a1 , a3 , a2 , a4 , . . . ,

a3 , a2 , a4 , a0 , a5 , a1 , . . . .

Their top pairs are (v2 , v2 ), (v1 , v1 ), (v0 , v0 ), repeated. The three steps exchange the arms. Their
distinct top pairs exclude shorter unordered positive periods. The spherical theta instead has
forbidden two-cycles and successive top pairs (v2 , v1 ), (v0 , v0 ), giving period two. Thus even identical
hereditary overorders need not give identical resolutions.
Quadratic Koszul duality here has a directly checkable local meaning: relations complement the
two possible successors. The dual dessin replaces the graph rotation by ϕ−1 . In the Ext algebra
against A/J, concatenating the forbidden-word basis gives the product when the join is forbidden; a
permitted join gives zero. One can see this by lifting a length-r basis class to the resolution: discard
the prescribed initial r letters and identify the remaining summand with its suffix resolution, taking
zero on other prefixes. These prefix-discard comparison maps commute with the suffix differentials,
with the standard shift signs when viewed as shifted complexes. The concatenated indexing word
exists exactly when its join is forbidden; evaluation of the two basis classes on that word gives
one. If the join is permitted there is no such indexing word and the product is zero. Composition
therefore concatenates indexing words, with the chosen right-module/Yoneda opposite convention.
This is a graded algebra statement; it neither makes the unbounded resolution perfect nor constructs
an equivalence of all formal-completion derived categories.
For an arbitrary bounded projective complex P • , its determinant line over a commutative
N
n (−1)n , with determinant duals for negative exponents. A chain
coefficient ring is
n (det P )
automorphism acts on this line; a quasi-isomorphism gives the corresponding determinant-functor
isomorphism. For a general endomorphism F , the expression for 1 − zF uses the locus where it is
invertible and gives a rational determinant-line action/trivialization, not an everywhere invertible
line map at singular parameters. Additivity is compatible with exact triangles under the usual
determinant-functor conventions [17, 18]. In our order context an R-linear determinant uses the finite
free underlying R-modules; it must specify a separate endomorphism. Determinants of a differential
presentation, alternating determinants of a chain action, regularized analytic determinants and the
Cartan path matrix are four distinct constructions. Their equality requires a proof, not their shared
name.
The geometric model literature [6, 8, 9] is useful for identifying which crossings and gradings
would have to be represented by a functor. To apply it here one must specify the category of
polynomial strings, completed lattices or finite-length quotients; construct its surface/curve objects;
and prove that degree-r intersections compute the Hom group into shift r, with local systems and
boundary exceptions included. We have supplied explicit walks and maps in the examples, not
claimed this general completed-order theorem. On a finite word, a map can already be computed
rigorously without such a theorem: assign a coefficient matrix between equal-vertex occurrences and
solve Ft(a) N (a) = N ′ (a)Fs(a) for every arrow. The diamond calculations below implement exactly
this system.

10.1

Derived information retained by a presentation

Let K = im f . The injection Hom(K, N ) → Hom(P1 , N ) descends to the Ext injection used in
Theorem 3.1; its kernel modulo im Hom(P0 , N ) is zero because the map P1 → K is onto. The
missing part of the cokernel detects maps on P1 not descending through K. This explains exactly
why an injective resolution map is needed for its full Euler interpretation.
In the node example the resolution of M = Λ/(x) has maps x, y, x, y, . . .. On N = k[y]/(y 2 )
the Hom cochain maps are 0, y, 0, y, . . .. Thus Hom(M, N ) = N and Ext1 (M, N ) = ker y have
dimensions two and one; the cokernel of the first zero map is two-dimensional. In the elliptic-square
example the first four Hom maps are 1, 0, 0, 0, periodically. Hence degrees zero and one vanish,
degrees two and three are k, and degree four vanishes. This also makes clear why a nonzero scalar
semi-invariant does not give a derived orthogonality theorem.
With coordinate action (gF )(N ) = F (g −1 N ) the Schofield weight is a − b, the negative of the
point-evaluation weight. For a single arrow it is the same elementary distinction as the action on
Hom(V, W ) versus on its coordinate functions V ⊗ W ∗ . The Cauchy decomposition for coordinate
functions is

10.3

Two precise functorial limitations

At any actual vertex simple S of A, the split exact sequence 0 → S → S ⊕ S → S → 0 uses first
inclusion and second projection. Termwise Sym2 has dimensions 1, 3, 1 at that vertex; its middle
kernel contains the mixed monomial as well as the first square, but its first image contains only
the first square. Middle homology is S. Thus even split exactness is not preserved. Genuine
derived Schur constructions instead use mixed tensor degrees, signed total differentials and suitable
flat/projective replacements [32, 33]; their membership in a particular order category must still be
checked.
The R-lattice M = Rex ⊕ Rey with x = diag(u, 0), y = diag(0, u) has x + y = uI. On Sym2R M
the images are diag(u2 , 0, 0) and diag(0, 0, u2 ). Their sum kills the mixed vector, hence equals
33

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

10

neither uI nor u2 I. This is a specific failure to retain the original central action, not a failure of the
polynomial monomial representation functor. Even a common ramified base substitution does not
repair this mixed vector.
Semi-invariants retain useful moduli on nilpotent modules. In the theta quiver keep only
a0 , a3 : v0 → v2 , put their scalar values (x, y) = (1, λ) and all other arrows zero. The v1 simple
splits off. Every length-two path acts by zero, so these are continuous completed modules. The two
scalars have the same weight gv2 /gv0 , and y/x is a rational invariant on x ̸= 0, giving the projective
pencil [x : y]. It is not a regular invariant on the whole affine locus, whose orbit closures meet zero.
For the interval quotient take x = J2 , y = λJ2 . With its top and socle specified, the separated
Kronecker coordinates are again (1, λ); the relation to the same-weight ratio is exact. The pp
formula in the core is stronger than merely noticing a ratio: on Mλ its solution set is im x, whereas
on Mµ , µ =
̸ λ, the equation (µ − λ)xv = 0 forces z = xv = 0. Different parameters are therefore
distinguishable by actual positive-primitive conditions even though every cycle trace is zero.

The nodal exact diagram

The normalization proof of Theorem 4.1 uses BM inside the generic module. Replacing it without

subdirect residue subspace is decomposed, the branch basis changes lift over complete DVRs; taking
inverse images modulo the conductor gives the direct decomposition, not just a decomposition of
residues.

11.2

Chebyshev geometry and the exact quotient

The identity 1 − Tn (z)2 = (1 − z 2 )Un−1 (z)2 follows from z = cos θ and the sine/cosine multiple-angle
formulas, hence as a polynomial identity. Tn′ = nUn−1 . Therefore βn has its only zero at infinity, of
order 2n; its n points over one are double since βn − 1 = Tn2 /(1 − Tn2 ); and its poles are simple at
±1 and double at the n − 1 interior roots of Un−1 . The derivative has no additional critical values.
The total ramification is (2n − 1) + n + (n − 1) = 4n − 2, as required for a degree-2n rational Belyi
map. The dual graph has n + 1 vertices of degrees 1, 1, 2, . . . , 2, and n edges; it is a connected path.
Its planar ribbon embedding is unique, so this identifies the particular nested-loop dessin, not only
its passport.
In the quotient retaining orientation Ω, any surviving path traverses each chain edge at most
once and either endpoint loop at most once. Its length is at most n + 1; thus the killed-arrow ideal
contains J n+2 . The quotient of An and of its completion is the same Hn (Ω). For n = 3 with forward
chain arrows, its path counts in lengths zero through four are 3, 4, 3, 2, 1, total thirteen. These

35

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

11

counts describe a quotient representation category of the infinite order, not a change of primary
object.
Inflation is full because the two algebras have exactly the same actions on these annihilated
objects. It preserves internal exact sequences. It does not preserve arbitrary ambient extension
closure: the two adjacent simples are annihilated by the killed reverse arrow, but the two-dimensional
extension having only that arrow nonzero is not. Likewise endpoint-local freeness over k[l]/l2 or
k[r]/r2 in [46] is not R-freeness. To transfer an almost-split property to a larger category one must
prove the requisite approximation/factorization statements; locally finite quiver criteria [40, 41, 42]
do not apply just because each vertex has two arrows. The permitted cycles violate interval/Hom
finiteness.

A basis vector is assigned to each visited position 0, . . . , ℓ(w) at its vertex. A direct letter sends its
preceding vector to its following vector; an inverse letter sends the following vector to the preceding
vector. Sum these elementary maps for each arrow and put all unmentioned entries zero. This
defines every matrix, including repeated loops, and verifies the relations. Set the killed reverse
arrows to zero to obtain actual Λ3 -modules.

The canonical diagram appears in Chapter 21; the proof and calculation below are retained.
Horizontal projections keep the initial word positions and kill the remaining positions. Horizontal
inclusions append the final r− , keeping old positions. Upper vertical projections delete the initial
l− : old position j ≥ 1 maps to position j − 1, and old position zero maps to zero. Lower vertical
inclusions prepend r− b− a− l: position j maps to j + 4. Multiply these maps by the indicated signs.
For every arrow these formulas commute with the string action, including the two positions adjacent
to a cut or join; those are exactly where the loop inverse and chosen orientation matter.
Every small square has two length-two composites whose sum is zero. The exact sequences are
0 → A → B ⊕ D → E → 0,

0 → B → C ⊕ E → F → 0,

0 → D → E ⊕ G → H → 0,

0 → E → F ⊕ H → I → 0.

For example the maps out of A retain its initial positions 0, 1, 2, 3 in B and delete its first position
in D, so their joint kernel is zero. The maps to E are onto, and the dimension vectors give
dim A + dim E = dim B + dim D vertexwise. The zero composite plus this equality proves exactness.
36

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

11

The other three use precisely the same keep/delete or append/prepend rules; their vertexwise
dimension equalities and jointly injective/surjective maps prove exactness. The shipped exact
checker constructs all matrices and verifies these assertions independently over Q.
The stronger almost-split property is imported by identifying E = w = ab as the minimal string
of type (2, 2) and these exact hook/cohook words with [46, Proposition3.12], using that paper’s
Propositions2.6/2.9 and Lemma3.10. Opposite-module conventions are reconciled by reversing
travel/composition conventions; the closed doubled quiver is isomorphic to its opposite by exchanging
the two arrows at each chain edge. Thus intrinsically τ E = A, τ F = B, τ H = D, τ I = E. The
rows and columns of this grid are not generally short exact, unlike the nodal diagram. Two small
cautions in that source are not needed here: a tube-rank label in Example3.9(3) is inconsistent
with its listed mouth, and a general homogeneous-tube statement must not be read as a converse
classifying every such object as a band. Neither is used to import these four meshes.

11.4

The pure-injective quotient and what its dimension means

For B0 = Λ/J 2 = k⟨x, y⟩/(x, y)2 , words alternate direct and inverse letters because every direct/direct and inverse/inverse adjacent pair is forbidden. Up to rotation and inverse its only
primitive band is xy −1 ; the bridge quiver has two oriented band vertices with no connecting nontrivial bridge. The longest path has length zero. The domestic string results [44, 45] therefore give
relative dimension 0 + 2 = 2, not an automatic dimension three for every one-domestic algebra.
Finite-dimensional points have relative CB rank zero; the appropriate Prüfer/adic and one-sided
periodic string points have rank one; the unique generic point has rank two. These statements
concern the quotient category with its inherited pp structure.
Indeed a pp formula is a finite system of linear equations with existentially quantified unknowns.
On a module annihilated by J 2 every coefficient reduces to B0 , so purity and pure-injectivity are
preserved in both directions under inflation. The condition M J 2 = 0 is a definable closed condition,
giving the closed Ziegler subspace. Its relative ranks need not be ranks in the entire Λ spectrum.
This subcategory is not extension closed: Λ/(x3 , y) gives an extension of objects killed by J 2 that is
not itself killed by J 2 . Since u2 annihilates every object of the subcategory, it contains no nonzero
R-free lattice. Ringel’s representation-theoretic node construction is also not a Morita equivalence
identifying the geometric nodal order with the Kronecker algebra. Infinite-dimensional polynomial
string classifications [38] retain finitely-controlled or pointwise-artinian assumptions; an arbitrary
complete lattice does not automatically satisfy them as an algebraic A-module.

Chapter 12

The operator extension in detail
12.1

A corrected cycle lift and the integer boundary map

In the permitted-state graph each length-ℓ cycle has a full matrix-corner Laurent/circle coordinate.
Its chosen K1 generator is the full cycle at one state plus the complementary identity. In Fock space
L∗c Lc = Qa + vs(a) . Thus the tempting lift Lc + (1 − Qa ) has overlapping initial pieces and is not an
isometry. The corrected lift in Theorem 5.1 first restricts to Qa :
W = Lc Qa + (1 − Qa ),

W ∗ W = I,

I − W W ∗ = Qa − Lc Qa L∗c .

A path starting with a and length greater than ℓ has uniquely the full prefix c followed by another
path starting with a. The missing range consists exactly of lengths 1, . . . , ℓ. Its terminal multiset
is the original graph’s incident-edge multiset at that cycle vertex. These finite defect vectors all
lie in terminal compact ideals; their classes give the claimed unsigned incidence entries. The sign
convention affects only the overall sign of the boundary map.
Here is the Smith calculation without spectral assumptions. Choose a spanning tree of the
connected underlying graph and one root. By changing signs on one bipartition of the tree, its
E × V incidence submatrix becomes the ordinary oriented tree incidence, with V − 1 unit pivots.
After eliminating these pivots, a remaining column represents the common root coordinate, and
each nontree edge evaluates on it as zero when its fundamental cycle is even, or ±2 when odd. A
loop is the latter case. If all are even the graph is bipartite and this column is zero; otherwise
its entries have gcd two, giving the final pivot two. Remaining rows are free. Consequently the
kernel/cokernel, then the six-term sequence, give the K groups stated in the core. No theorem
reconstructing a cyclic embedding from those groups is asserted.
The compact ideal is block diagonal, not K(H): prefixes never move terminal vertices, so
cross-terminal rank-one maps do not belong to the displayed ideal. This is why the arithmetic
insertion must choose one terminal sector. A graph/Cuntz boundary quotient kills the vacua and
hence this ideal, so that insertion need not survive passage to the quotient.

12.2

Spectral domain and arithmetic trace

A vector belongs to the adjoint domain of the diagonal N precisely when its coefficientwise product
by the real sequence |p| is square summable; this proves self-adjointness on the given domain. The
truncations by length prove finite supports are a core. Resolvent coefficients (|p| − i)−1 tend to zero
and each length space is finite dimensional, giving compactness. Prefix and deletion shift the length
38

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

12

by at most one, so preserve the domain and prove the commutator formulas there. Leibniz then
handles the dense polynomial *-algebra.
Every terminal sector has one vacuum and two paths at each length n ≥ 1, even when some
graph endpoints coincide: distinct formal paths remain distinct basis vectors. Thus restriction to a
sector still has a simple vacuum, but it need not be a faithful representation of the whole concrete
P

algebra. The trace of e−tN is E + 2E n≥1 e−tn , distinct from the semigroup trace of N in the
core. The trace identity with χ(N ) is only on Re s > 1, where the product is trace class. Analytic
continuation of the scalar L-function does not make that same operator product trace class on
Re s > 0.

13.1

Precisely how classical and recent sources enter

Over a finite field the Grothendieck trace-formula architecture writes a zeta function as alternating
determinants on finite-dimensional cohomology; Deligne’s purity supplies the weights of eigenvalues
[22, 20, 21]. If a weight-w Frobenius is q w/2 times a positive-metric unitary, each eigenvalue has
modulus q w/2 . The norm conclusion is immediate; constructing that form or proving purity is the
deep step. Symplectic/alternating duality F t JF = q w J alone only pairs reciprocal eigenvalues and
does not give their individual moduli. An order resolution or Schur-positive character expansion
alone cannot replace this step in number-field cohomology.
The recent primary comparisons used here are specific:
• [53, Theorem3.12, pp.15–17] gives finite abelian covers with local orbit length log p and genuine
arithmetic monodromy. It suggests what a descent-equipped order functor should match; it
does not identify our u or a face cycle with a prime without such a functor.
• [54, Propositions4.1–4.3, Theorem4.6] builds finite-prime local models with archimedean Fourier
factors. Its Hilbertian isomorphisms are not an asserted uniformly controlled common isometry
for our arithmetic approximants. [55, Proposition3.1] gives determinacy of a particular local
moment problem; positive finite Hankel matrices are not the missing global Weil inequality.
• [56, Theorem3.6] gives discreteness/lower boundedness for the stated restricted Weil form.
Theorem5.10, pp.23–24, additionally assumes a finite truncation, λ > 1, a simple even bottom
eigenfunction ξ and δN ξ = 1. Its quotient metric is positive and its regularized determinant
b
is −iλ−iz ξ(z).
Passing to the infinite arithmetic comparison, including the ground-state and
convergence conditions in its §8, is not automatic.
41

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

13

• [57, Theorem6.1, p.13] assumes a specified real distribution on an interval, the corresponding
trigonometric-core form, essential self-adjointness, and an isolated simple lowest eigenvalue
with reflection-even eigenfunction. Its Fourier transform then has only real zeros. Our degree
operator’s vacuum and length parity are not those data.
• [60, Theorem6.1] gives prime-indexed periodic packets in its dynamical construction, not one
proved orbit per prime with the full desired cohomological trace formula. [62] isolates an
obstruction to a particular real-coefficient cohomology with full functorial Artin multiplicities; it
is not a theorem excluding every Hilbert–Pólya operator. The current survey [58] distinguishes
remaining steps. The recent Picard-monoid construction [59] is not already a polarized abelian
variety with a number-field purity theorem. [63] uses RH as an input in the cited spectral
identification, so cannot be used circularly as its proof.
These are targeted theorem/proof readings, not claims that every page of every cited work has
been audited. The preserved review records specify actual page coverage and hashes, including
inaccessible original PDFs where the modern primary proof was used instead.

13.2

A concrete sequence of remaining constructions

First choose a number-field model of a Belyi pair, its finite covers and conductor/ramification data.
Construct actions or exact correspondences on the labelled order/module or analytic categories that
recover the local prime Frobenius polynomials. The elliptic twists show why omitting this datum
loses information. Test at more than one arithmetic model of the same complex dessin, not just its
topological passport.
Second specify a finite projective or otherwise determinant-admissible complex and its independent
arithmetic endomorphism. Prove exact-sequence/determinant-line compatibility. Use semi-invariants
to encode presentation degeneracies, and Schur/exterior characters to calculate local factors, retaining
every central-action and nilpotence distinction above. General derived Schur replacements require
their own construction; termwise Schur on the resolution fails the exhibited split sequence test.
Third prove the same local factors, gamma terms and conductor give a globally convergent
or correctly regularized determinant. Trace-norm convergence must be established for the chosen
analytic perturbations; strong convergence is insufficient in general. The inserted Abel family
supplies this topology for an elementary scalar encoding, but has neither the independent arithmetic
spectrum nor the positive metric needed for zero location.
Finally produce an independently proved positive form or self-adjoint determinant-preserving
realization. One acceptance criterion is the Hurwitz target in the core; another is equality with the
precise arithmetic quadratic form in the recent spectral theorems, with all their domain, ground-state,
normalization and limit hypotheses verified. The local Cauchy kernel, K-group computation and
graph spectral dimension alone meet none of these final comparison hypotheses. No remaining
condition is silently asserted true here. Dirichlet, Hecke, Dedekind and Artin scopes must remain
individually recorded.

specified fibers
14.1

The complete Fock graph presentation

For graph C* conventions the edge operator has initial projection at its range vertex and final
projection under its source vertex. Put
Sh = Lah Qaτ h ,

Th = Lah v[τ h] .

The two right-hand projections are orthogonal subprojections of L∗ah Lah = Qaτ h + v[τ h] . Thus
Sh∗ Sh = Qaτ h , Th∗ Th = v[τ h] , and Sh∗ Th = 0. For distinct h the prefix ranges are orthogonal, so
every other cross-edge CK1 product is zero. Their final projections sum to Qah . The vacua have
no outgoing edges. The resulting graph has |H| + |Q0 | vertices and 2|H| edges, with cycles only
among its state vertices and exits to the paired sinks. Gauge covariance and nonzero vertices prove
faithfulness as well as surjectivity. This explains precisely how a graph C* algebra is obtained
without imposing the incompatible original-quiver CK1 relations.
The graph integer map has one column for each regular vertex h and no sink columns:
d : ZH −→ ZH ⊕ ZQ0 ,

d(eh ) = eh − eτ h − b[τ h] .

The state part of d(x) = 0 says xh = xτ −1 h ; the sink part then says the constant cycle coefficients
are in ker BΓ . For the cokernel choose a base state on each cycle and successively use qh = qτ h + b[τ h]
P
to eliminate all other states. Closing the cycle leaves h∈c b[h] = 0, while its base state remains free.
Hence

= Z# cyc σ ⊕ coker BΓ .
This gives an independent integral check of the previous compact-extension computation. The full
labelled graph, diagonal projections and edge families carry more information than those coarse
groups.

14.2

A genuine application of the supplied pullback theorem

For the interval dessin let E2 = Gα have loop vertices qx , qy , common sink b, loops sx , sy and exits
tx , ty . Remove b to obtain F2 , two disjoint loop graphs. Its quotient is exactly C(T)2 . Let E1 have
the same three vertices and edges ex,n : qx → b, ey,n : qy → b, n ≥ 0, and no other edges. Remove b
to obtain the edgeless F1 , whose algebra is C2 .
43

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

14

The inclusions Fi ⊂ Ei are admissible in the precise sense of [65, Definition3.1]: the removed
sink set is hereditary/saturated; all edges ending at retained vertices are retained; no vertex emits
infinitely many edges into the removed set and a positive finite number outside it. For E1 the latter
outside count is zero, which is allowed. Define a path functor identity on objects by
ex,n 7−→ snx tx ,

ey,n 7−→ sny ty .

Images of distinct positive edges are prefix incomparable: at the first different length one has an
exit and the other a loop. Source vertices and identities cause no exception. The image consists of
all positive pointed paths in E2 , whose last edge is not a short loop; vertices are included separately.
The first graph is acyclic and the removed sink has no short loop in the second. All hypotheses of
Theorem3.4 are now verified, giving
C ∗ (E1 ) ∼
= TA × C2 .
C(T)2

The quotient of C ∗ (E1 ) by its sink ideal is C2 , and its sink ideal is compact operators on the paths
ending at b. The graph is acyclic, hence its algebra is AF. There are no regular vertices in E1 : the
two sources are infinite emitters and b is a sink. The graph K matrix has no columns and three
rows, yielding K0 = Z3 and K1 = 0.
This is a positive instance of the new source inside our infinite dessin framework. It is a
constants-symbol subalgebra of the Fock model, not the entire Fock algebra or the formal order.
For a permitted cycle of length greater than one, its full closed path is itself pointed in this
paper’s convention, so an object-preserving acyclic path functor cannot cover all such paths. That
simple obstruction prevents copying this example to every cycle by analogy. The sink-extension
Theorem4.3 also requires a specified original pullback, injective gluing maps, annihilation of glued
vertex projections and coverage of all paths ending at the gluing sinks. A two-to-one identification of
sink occurrences within one branch does not satisfy these assumptions without another construction.

14.3

Diagonal residue obstruction and the corrected pullback

Suppose one tries to glue full matrix-circle blocks by their diagonal values. In M2 , E12 E21 = E11 ,
although both factors have zero diagonal. Thus the proposed boundary map is not a homomorphism.
For ℓ > 1, more generally any *-homomorphism from the full matrix-circle algebra to a commutative
C*-algebra has equal images of Eii = Eij Eji and Ejj = Eji Eij . These images are both orthogonal
and equal, hence zero; summing the diagonal units kills the image of one and then the whole
homomorphism. This works for nonunital *-maps as well: a nonzero image of one would give a
nonzero unital corner map. The failure concerns full matrix-circle blocks, not the already-restricted
fiber algebra Dℓ .
Evaluation Dℓ → Cℓ is a genuine surjective *-homomorphism since at one both factors are
diagonal. Its kernel is C0 (T \ {1}, Mℓ ). Pulling it back along the repeated-coordinate embedding
∆α : CQ0 → CH gives exactly the fiber conditions of DH . The paired constant diagonal matrices
give a unital *-section. Hence this is a split C*-extension, not a claim deduced from a merely
commutative diagram of nonmultiplicative maps. Since T \ {1} ∼
= R, each kernel block is a matrix
suspension of C, with K0 = 0, K1 = Z. Split exactness now proves Theorem 5.3.
The pairing is retained in this specified presentation, not recovered from the unmarked isomorphism class. In particular arbitrary constant permutation matrices can relabel a branch’s
diagonal positions, so the spherical and elliptic theta examples have isomorphic unmarked DH
despite different ribbon orderings. Keep the labelled arrows, cycle cuts and distinguished fiber as
44

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

14

additional data when using it as a dessin model. Also the central branch unitary zIℓ represents ℓ
times the primitive corner generator in K1 , not a new independent generator per matrix entry.

14.4

Generation, faithfulness and the exact failure of extension

Let ρ denote the polynomial representation in Theorem 5.3. Its vertex images are orthogonal
projections summing to one. Every arrow image lies under the correct source and range vertex
projections. A forbidden product vanishes because the intermediate state indices are τ h and ατ h,
which are distinct. Thus ρ is an actual algebra map, not a fitted characteristic polynomial.
For a full turn starting at h, ρ(ch ) = wc Ehh with wc (z) = g(z)|c| z. It is a normal element of DH .
The function wc vanishes exactly at one; away from that point wc /|wc | = z. Therefore it is injective
on all of T, and a homeomorphism to its image. Nonunital continuous functional calculus at ρ(ch ),
using functions zero at zero, gives every f Ehh with f ∈ C(T), f (1) = 0. The zero condition makes
the result vanish in all other matrix entries and branches.
To obtain an off-diagonal entry choose the unique shortest permitted path from state h to j
within its cycle, of length r > 0 and cut-crossing count κ ∈ {0, 1}. Its image is g r z κ Ehj . Given
f (1) = 0, multiply it on the left by the already available diagonal element
f (z)z −κ

ε > 0.

The product is f g r /(g r + ε)Ehj and converges uniformly to f Ehj . Indeed near one |f | is small, and
on the complement of that neighborhood g r has a positive minimum. These two bounds prove
uniform convergence without assuming division by a function vanishing at the fiber. Thus the
generated algebra contains the whole kernel ideal. Its quotient contains the entire paired diagonal
algebra, so it is DH .

vanish, forcing the coefficients of the original vertex idempotents to vanish. Fix a state-matrix entry

with a possible initial zero coefficient if r = 0. On T \ {1} the prefactor is nonzero, and wc has infinite
range, so the polynomial has infinitely many distinct zeros. All coefficients are zero. Repeating by
entries proves faithfulness.
No boundedness improvement repairs full formal extension. Pick any z0 ̸= 1, let λ = wc (z0 )−1 ,
P
and consider 1 − λch . Its inverse m≥0 λm cm
h exists in the arrow-adic order. The matrix image at
z0 has a zero eigenvalue on state h, so cannot be invertible. A unital algebra homomorphism would
preserve the inverse identity, an immediate contradiction. This proves nonextension without any
continuity assumption. It does not rely on the false claim that a norm greater than one alone forces
a geometric-series denominator to be singular. Scaling g to have norm at most one still does not
allow arbitrary formal series.
The two valid operator completions are structurally different. In the bipartite case K0 (TA ) =
ZE+1 differs from K0 (DH ) = ZE ; in the nonbipartite case the former has the extra Z/2 torsion.
Neither is automatically the norm completion of the arrow-adic topology. No invariant constructed
solely from these complex objects distinguishes the rational twists already exhibited; arithmetic
descent remains a separate input.
