Invariant basis number and the Leavitt comparison

If a unital k-algebra R1 has a unital representation on a nonzero finite-dimensional vector space V ,
an isomorphism R1m ∼
= R1n induces an isomorphism V m ∼
= V n by tensoring. Comparing dimensions
gives m = n. This proves invariant basis number without requiring the representation to be faithful.
Every original dessin algebra and completed order has a vertex-simple representation, and each
permitted-state Laurent algebra has evaluation onto a nonzero matrix block. The Leavitt algebra of
Gα has the same finite-dimensional representation after killing the vacuum sinks and evaluating
one boundary cycle. Thus all of these examples have invariant basis number. Leavitt’s free-rank
phenomena are important elsewhere but cannot be assumed in this class simply from the name LPA.
For the specified finite graph Gα , the usual complex Leavitt path algebra embeds in C ∗ (Gα )
with norm-dense image, using its canonical generators and involution [67, Theorem7.3]. Those
generators are the split Sh , Th , not the unsplit original gentle arrows equipped with ordinary CK1.
This is the exact algebraic/analytic twin relationship applicable here.

46

Chapter 15

String finiteness and the smooth
quotient surface
For a finite quiver, finitely controlled implies finitely generated: choose one finitely generated
submodule containing each vertex space and sum the finitely many choices. Conversely the module
itself works. Stabilization at each of finitely many vertices likewise gives ordinary artinianity.
Crawley–Boevey’s polynomial theorem therefore applies directly to our finitely generated and
artinian polynomial modules, with its primitive injective bands in the latter case. It does not assert
a classification of every possible algebraic module.
The formal branch M = k[[x]], y = 0, gives a precise counterexample to that stronger assertion.
An A-endomorphism F preserves each xn M . For the polynomial truncation fn of f , one has
F (f ) − fn F (1) ∈ xn M . Separatedness and all n imply F (f ) = f F (1). Hence EndA (M ) = k[[x]] is
local, so M is indecomposable. If an ordinary word module has y = 0, its word cannot contain
y or y −1 , since such a letter would create a nonzero basis map. A reduced word using only x±1
cannot change direction. Its nonperiodic choices are finite strings, the free ray k[x], and the opposite
locally nilpotent ray. The first and last fail injectivity of x, and the free ray fails invertibility of
1 − x. A two-sided pure-x word is periodic, and in every associated band x acts invertibly. On M ,
x is injective but not onto. None of these possibilities equals M . This includes arbitrary nonzero
indecomposable Laurent coefficient modules, not just finite bands.
For a general nonzero completed lattice, finite generation over A would imply finite generation
over k[u]. Every nonzero polynomial in u is a power of u times a unit of k[[u]], so it acts injectively.
The lattice would be a nonzero finite free k[u]-module. Multiplication by 1 − u on such a free module
has a nonzero cokernel, but the same map is invertible over k[[u]]. This proves the contradiction
in the core. The strictly descending u-adic chain separately excludes artinianity. These are actual
failed hypotheses, not merely unverified applicability.
Lekili–Polishchuk’s precise convention takes D(A) to mean perfect dg modules. Their locally
gentle definition allows infinite dimension, and their smoothness criterion says no forbidden cycle;
properness says no permitted cycle. Both fail for every nonempty closed dessin here. Their
Theorem3.2.2 therefore does not apply to the full polynomial dessin algebra, even though it
applies to suitable infinite-dimensional quotients. The OPS finite-dimensional geometric-intersection
theorem has its own grading, local-system, boundary and exceptional coincident-curve conditions.
An unrestricted statement that every gentle/string derived object is an ungraded geodesic crossing
is not justified by either source.
For the interval quotient B = A/(y) = k[x], global dimension one follows from its PID structure:
every submodule of a finite free module is free, giving finite projective resolutions of all finitely
47

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

15

generated modules. Thus Perf(B) = Db (modfg B) in grading zero. Its permitted loop gives one
unmarked boundary component and its length-one forbidden thread x gives one stop on the other
boundary, the annulus of the smooth partially wrapped model. This construction uses the infinite
polynomial quotient, not the completed k[[x]] category.
Finally the quotient does not give a derived fully faithful embedding by ordinary inflation. Resolve
B over A by the alternating maps y, x, y, x, . . .. Hom into B gives 0, x, 0, x, . . ., so Ext2r
A (B, B) =
B/xB = k for every r ≥ 1, whereas all positive self-Ext over B vanish. Consequently its inflation is
not derived fully faithful, and the perfect B-module B becomes an infinite-projective-dimension
A-module. A surface functor transferring these categories must account for that difference explicitly.

16.2

Deck descent and the actual cellular basis

The CM cellular construction in this and the next subsection assumes q ≡ 1 (mod 3). The cover
β = x3 /d has deck transformations (x, y) 7→ (ζ3 x, y) and (x, y) 7→ (x, −y), generating C3 × C2 = C6 .
Label their actions on darts by σ = (012)(345) and α = (03)(14)(25); they commute. Their product
is (0 4 2 3 1 5). They preserve the edge pairing and both successor permutations. Consequently
they define algebra automorphisms, preserve J and its completion and fix the common u. The
projective-resolution maps are compatible since θg (ah x) = agh θg (x). Twisting module actions gives
an exact functor and the corresponding relabelled resolution, without an assumption of arithmetic
Frobenius.
Over an algebraic closure choose a3 = d, b2 = d and φ(x, y) = (ax, by) from E1 to Ed . For the
q-power Frobenius morphisms,
φ−1 ◦ Fd ◦ φ = hd ◦ F1 ,

hd (x, y) = (aq−1 x, bq−1 y).

When q ≡ 1 (mod 6), the first coordinate is the cubic residue d(q−1)/3 and the second η(d). Choose
the embedding of cube roots defining ρ consistently. On the deck eigenspace labelled by dx/y, the
deck character is their ratio, ρ(d)/η(d) = λd . This labels the twist, not a nonzero Frobenius action
on differentials in characteristic p: that pullback is zero. An inverse convention for arithmetic Galois
Frobenius changes the action convention; our displayed formula uses the geometric curve morphism
explicitly. The character-sum polynomial fixes its trace without an unstated convention.
Orient the three geometric edges as in the core. Each occurs twice with opposite orientation in
the single face, so ∂2 = 0; ∂1 has columns (−1, 1)t . The homology basis b0 = e0 − e1 , b1 = e1 − e2
has Gram matrix G inherited from the orthonormal cellular edges. Rotation sends b0 7→ b1 ,
b1 7→ −b0 − b1 , exactly C. The involution swaps vertices and reverses each edge, hence acts as −I on
this plane. Thus multiplication by A + Bω is literally AI + BC, not merely an abstract matrix with
the correct trace. Expanding its determinant gives the norm A2 − AB + B 2 , and multiplication of
this norm proves M t GM = qG. The displayed positive quadratic decomposition proves the metric
independently of unknown zeros.

16.5

The remaining order and global spectral obligations

The deck automorphism has finite order, so over characteristic zero its eigenvalues are roots of
√
unity. Its permutation action alone cannot supply a weight-one norm q > 1. The independently
computed Jacobi coefficient is essential. The above construction closes the specified local cellular
benchmark, not a reconstruction of arithmetic from bare complex dessin data.
For these order-insertion tests take Λ = ΛQ over R = Q[[u]], with rational residue simples and
Vd = Q2 . This is distinct from reducing the matrix in characteristic p, where its determinant p
becomes zero. There are two concrete failures of a naive order lift. First, a residue simple Se
is annihilated by all arrows; Se ⊗ Vd with independent endomorphism I ⊗ Md has the desired
underlying finite-Q determinant. But its projective dimension is infinite by the nonterminating
forbidden resolution, and σ permutes all three vertex labels, so choosing Se does not preserve full
deck symmetry. This is not a perfect-order determinant realization.
Second, Λ ⊗ Vd is free of rank two over Λ, but the underlying R-rank of Λ is eighteen. In
an R-basis, the Λ-linear I ⊗ Md is eighteen copies of Md , hence its determinant is Pd18 . On eΛ
the rank is six: each of its two hereditary branches contributes a row of length three, with finite
conductor gluing changing no rank. This gives Pd6 . Restoring all three residue vertices similarly
gives Pd3 for this untwisted tensor action. A genuine deck-semilinear map permutes factors as well
and need not have these powers; it requires its own comparison. No justified determinant-line root
or multiplicity-removing functor is supplied here.
Finally fix a nonzero rational d when forming its global Euler product from good reductions.
Local purity cannot be multiplied into a global zero-location proof. Since each reciprocal root has
√
modulus p, the product of good elliptic factors is absolutely convergent on Re s > 3/2. The local
zeros at Re s = 1/2 are outside that initial product argument. The finite-field curve zeta’s center
is 1/2; the global elliptic L-function’s unshifted center is one, or 1/2 only after a half-unit shift.
Neither is an identification with the primitive Dirichlet function targeted earlier. Ramified factors,
archimedean normalization, adelic descent and the global determinant-preserving positive spectral
limit remain separate obligations.

17.3

An actual integral determinant functor of multiplicity one

Project to one hereditary cycle order B+ ⊂ M3 (R). Its standard column W+ = R3 is a left
wi
Λ-module with ei W+ = Rϵi . More explicitly a+
i acts by u Ei,i−1 , with (w0 , w1 , w2 ) = (1, 0, 0)
−
for one cut, while every ai acts by zero. These matrices respect travel-order multiplication as
written: Eij Ejk = Eik . Each plus full cycle is uEii , so the common central u acts by uI. Nonempty
paths tend to zero u-adically, giving the action of the full completed order, not just its polynomial
subalgebra.
This (Λ, R)-bimodule defines − ⊗L
Λ W+ . On a bounded complex of finite projectives it is
computed termwise, and its terms are finite free over R. An acyclic bounded projective complex is
split contractible, and tensor preserves that contraction. Thus it descends to a genuine triangulated
functor Φ+ : Perf(Λ) → Perf(R). No assertion of exactness on all un-derived modules or flatness of
W+ is needed. In fact W+ has infinite projective dimension as a left Λ-module: Λ is noetherian,
being finite over the central noetherian ring R, and W+ is finitely generated. A finite projective
resolution could thus be chosen with finite terms. Every left projective term has R-rank divisible
by six by the same top argument, whereas W+ has rank three, contradicting the alternating rank
identity. This does not prevent tensoring bounded right-projective complexes.
The isomorphism ei Λ ⊗Λ W+ → ei W+ sends ei λ ⊗ w 7→ ei λw. Its inverse is ei w 7→ ei ⊗ ei w,
proving rank one directly. Consequently ei Λ ⊗Q V , with action 1 ⊗ M , maps to R ⊗Q V with action
M . Its characteristic determinant is det(1 − T M ) once. On the regular input, the three vertex lines
survive and the factor appears three times. A full symmetric vertex orbit therefore is not silently
replaced by a chosen vertex.
Different cycle cuts give different integral column lattices. Their generic modules are isomorphic,
and branch endomorphism characteristic determinants agree by generic conjugation; equality over
K implies equality over R. Nevertheless an equivariant lattice is stronger data. Branch selection
also changes the functor: for the unit f = ei + c+
i on ei Λ, the plus determinant is 1 + u and its
−
characteristic factor is 1 − (1 + u)T . Under α, c+
i becomes ci , whose plus action is zero; the
determinant becomes one and the characteristic factor 1 − T . Since these scalar automorphisms
differ, no natural isomorphism of determinant lines can make this chosen branch functor α-invariant
for all endomorphisms. Pairing both branch functors makes this branch-swap data available but
doubles the local factor on the basic-projective tensor input.

17.4

The exact rotation obstruction and its allowed escape

Consider any left Λ-lattice supported on the plus branch, free of rank one at every ei , and with
central u acting as multiplication by u. Write its plus arrow scalars as a0 , a1 , a2 ∈ R. Their product
is u, so they are nonzero and their nonnegative valuations sum to one. A strict R-linear lift of σ
that intertwines the order rotation permutes the three vertex lines by units. Its arrow-intertwining

55

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

17

identities make the three arrow valuations equal. Thus 3m = 1 for an integer m ≥ 0, impossible.
The argument already excludes an invertible intertwiner; imposing that its cube be one cannot help.
There is an elementary coefficient proof of the same obstruction. Intertwining by units makes
vanishing of the three arrow constant coefficients equivalent. Their product has constant coefficient
zero, so all three vanish. Factoring u from two of them would make a0 a1 a2 divisible by u2 ,
contradicting that it equals u. This is the literal power-series component checked in Lean.
After the explicit ramified change R′ = Q[[v]], u = v 3 , choose a new column lattice with all three
plus arrow maps vEi,i−1 . Cyclic permutation of the basis is an order-three intertwiner fixing v. The
full-cycle relation is now v 3 = u as required. Merely base-changing the old cut lattice gives arrow
valuations (3, 0, 0) and does not produce that linearization. This construction changes the base and
lattice and does not establish an unramified descent comparison. It also still needs both branches to
address α.
The rotation obstruction is about actual central-fixed rank-one branch lattices, not every
possible determinant-level natural transformation. The branch characteristic determinants may be
rotation-invariant through their generic reduced interpretation even without a lattice lift. Neither
this theorem nor the ordinary-determinant obstruction excludes semilinear correspondences, other
determinant categories or infinite analytic models.
The next arithmetic task is therefore narrower and more concrete: choose a branch functor,
a ramified lattice or a different equivariant replacement only after proving the required descent
comparison, and construct its prime action from arithmetic data rather than feed in the already
known Md . Bad primes, integral/Tate comparison, local-to-global conductor and gamma factors,
and a positive spectral limit for the same completed function remain unresolved.

Explicit tame descent and the full-deck

The preceding ramified column is a precisely specified object of the original infinite theta-order
framework. We compute its descent instead of treating a semilinear group action as automatically
effective integral descent. All constructions below are over the new mathematical coefficient field
k = Q(ω); they do not replace the original rational model or its source history.

A projective symmetry survives on the endomorphism order

18.4

Why the local Jacobi plane does not descend by this repair

On constant vectors Θ acts by D. It sends the sum-zero cycle ϵ0 − ϵ1 to ϵ0 − ω 2 ϵ1 , whose coordinate
sum is nonzero. It also does not preserve the edge-sum line. Thus this order column is not already
a tame descent of the earlier cellular chain complex or its H1 .
For the split local coefficient π = a + bω, the natural rotation polynomial on the balanced carrier
is F = aI + bC. Since g fixes a, b ∈ k, its commutator is
ΘF − F Θ = b(ω 2 − 1)CΘ.
It is nonzero when b ̸= 0. In particular both prime-seven twists (a, b) = (3, 2), (−1, −3) fail this
test. Prime powers with b = 0 are not excluded by this particular calculation; that exceptional
commutation does not prove a global comparison.
Replacing C by vC gives an invariant carrier map, whose matrix on L is aI + bA. Direct
determinant expansion gives
det(1 − T (aI + bA)) = (1 − aT )3 − ub3 T 3 .
At u = 0 this is (1 − aT )3 , not Pd (T ), which has degree two. The weighted replacement therefore
does not recover the established local factor. Neither C nor vC is generally Λ-linear: they intertwine
its rotation. Their sums with the identity have not thereby become endomorphisms in the ordinary
order-module category. They are legitimate carrier tests, with exactly the failures just exhibited.

The remaining arithmetic problem therefore requires a different hypothesis: a genuinely twisted

infinite analytic realization. One cannot continue searching inside this closed finite-free, fixed-center,
full-deck kernel class for a missing small estimate. The local purity and descent computations supply
no global positive spectral limit. Moreover k/Q has degree two: ordinary restriction of scalars sends
a rational local factor Pd to Pd2 . A relative k-determinant must not silently be identified with the
former rational determinant. Finite-field curve zeta, global elliptic L-functions and Dirichlet GRH
keep their distinct scopes and centers.

Here are the linear details behind Proposition 7.2. Write xi = Fii for diagonal entries of an
endomorphism F preserving the three vertex spaces. Solving F X = XF , F Y = Y F leaves a
common diagonal c and six free entries. More explicitly its only off-diagonal entries are
F02 = F13 = t0 ,

F15 = t1 ,

F35 = F46 = t2 ,

F48 = t3 ,

F68 = F70 = t4 ,

F72 = t5 .

All others vanish. This can be checked column by column using the displayed partial permutation
matrices X, Y : their nonzero columns specify all constraints, and the zero columns force the
remaining entries to zero. If r0 , . . . , r5 are the six radical generators in the displayed order, the only
nonzero products are
r0 r2 = r1 ,
r2 r4 = r3 ,
r4 r0 = r5 .
Every product of three radical generators vanishes. Thus the listed space is a local algebra, not
merely a seven-dimensional vector-space count.
For clarity, the Euler equations used in the obstruction are

where di = Dii . Subtract the last row from the first two rows after summing: the left side is zero
and the right side is three. This is an identity in every characteristic-zero coefficient field. In

closed walk

Part III

AR components, derived categories
and Hom-control

66

Chapter 21

Almost-split geometry, Hom control
and determinant relations
The infinite locally gentle algebra and its completed order are the primary objects in this chapter.
Finite algebras enter through specified quotients or special fibers, with their categories named. This
distinction makes the pictures more useful: a mesh is an exact sequence in an actual category, a
tube is an entire component, and a diagram of limiting words records additional objects rather than
suggesting an unproved equivalence.
We work over an algebraically closed field k of characteristic zero unless stated otherwise.
Paths are written in travel order, so ab traverses a first. We use right projectives ei A with this
convention. Column representations may instead be read over the opposite algebra; all displayed
arrow-intertwining identities use the usual column convention. This change of handedness does not
identify different categories or change any of the explicit Hom dimensions.

21.1

What an AR picture specifies

For an infinite polynomial dessin algebra A = kQ/I, write modfd A for finite-dimensional modules.
Its arrow-adic completion is Λ = lim A/J n . A continuous finite-dimensional Λ-module kills some
←−
J n . A lattice over an order finite over R = k[[u]] is instead a finitely generated module free over R.
A nonzero lattice has infinite dimension over k and is not a continuous finite-length module. These
distinctions persist even when the same quiver describes the arrow actions.
The polynomial string classification has its own finiteness boundary. For a finite quiver, finitely
controlled modules are precisely finitely generated modules: sum the finitely many finitely generated
submodules containing their vertex spaces. Pointwise artinian is likewise equivalent to artinian, by
taking a common stabilization index over the finitely many vertices. Thus [111, Theorems 1.2–1.4]
genuinely applies to finitely generated and artinian modules over our infinite polynomial string
algebras, with its specified strings, finite-dimensional bands and primitive injective bands. It does
not say that every module, or every infinite word, satisfies these finiteness conditions.
A nonzero completed node lattice is outside both classes after restriction to A = k[x, y]/xy.
Indeed A is finite over k[u], u = x + y. If the lattice were finitely generated over A, it would be
finite torsion free over the PID k[u], hence finite free of positive rank. Multiplication by 1 − u would
then have a nonzero cokernel. On a k[[u]]-lattice it is a unit, a contradiction. The strictly decreasing
chain M ⊃ uM ⊃ u2 M ⊃ · · · also excludes artinianness. The lattice classification below is proved
independently.

67

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

21

An almost-split sequence in an exact category is a nonsplit conflation
p

i

0 −→ L →
− E→
− M −→ 0
whose first map factors every nonsection out of L and whose second map factors every nonretraction
into M . Minimality removes redundant summands. The Auslander–Reiten translate is τ M = L.
Irreducible maps give the arrows of the AR quiver; projective vertices do not have nonsplit almostsplit sequences ending there. The mesh relation says that the two component composites, with the
appropriate signs, sum to zero. It is an identity between module maps. It is not, without a further
construction, a polynomial relation between functions on a representation variety.
A quotient A ↠ B gives a fully faithful exact inflation mod B → mod A. Its image is the full
subcategory annihilated by the kernel. That image need not be extension closed. Even when it is
closed under the relevant exact operations, an almost-split factorization in the smaller category
need not factor every map from the larger category. Throughout, the word intrinsic records this
boundary.

The corrected elliptic-theta face example

21.2.1

Permutations, genus and the actual algebra

The six-dart data

σ = (1 2 3)(4 5 6),

α = (1 4)(2 5)(3 6)

are transitive and commute. With composition acting rightmost first,
σα = (1 5 3 4 2 6),

ϕ = (σα)−1 = (1 6 2 4 3 5).

The uncontracted dessin has two black vertices, three white vertices, six edges and one face. Thus
2 − 2g = 2 + 3 − 6 + 1 = 0, and g = 1. After contracting the clean white vertices the counts are two
vertices, three edges and one face. A global Belyi realization is y 2 = x3 − 1, β = x3 .
Its algebra has vertices x1 , x2 , x3 and arrows
a1 : x1 → x2 ,

a2 : x2 → x3 ,

a3 : x3 → x1 ,

b3 : x1 → x2 ,

b1 : x2 → x3 ,

b2 : x3 → x1 .

The forbidden travel pairs are
a1 b1 , b1 a3 , a3 b3 , b3 a2 , a2 b2 , b2 a1 .
This is the same presentation as the corresponding right-to-left list after reversing each product.
For an explicit dart identification use
1 7→ a1 ,

2 7→ a3 ,

3 7→ a2 ,

4 7→ b3 ,

5 7→ b2 ,

6 7→ b1 .

Then the face is the forbidden indexing cycle a1 b1 a3 b3 a2 b2 . It repeats vertices; it does not create
six distinct primitive idempotents. The algebra is infinite dimensional because its two permitted
three-cycles can be traversed indefinitely. Its completed order has R-rank 18.
Proposition 21.1 (A face unfolds to a complex, not a six-vertex quotient). Put B6 = k Ae5 /J 2 ,
with the cyclic orientation. Every representation of the theta algebra gives a B6 -representation by
unfolding the face:
(V1 , V2 , V3 , V1 , V2 , V3 ),

(D1 , . . . , D6 ) = (a1 , b1 , a3 , b3 , a2 , b2 ).

The resulting functor is additive, exact and faithful, but is not full. There is no unital surjection
from this theta algebra to B6 .
68

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

21

Proof. Every consecutive product Di+1 Di is zero by the displayed relation list. A theta morphism
repeats its three vertex maps at the two corresponding face positions, so it gives a complex morphism
and is recovered from those repeated maps. Kernels and cokernels are computed vertexwise, proving
exactness and faithfulness. On the vertex simple at x1 its image is the sum of the two B6 -simples at
positions 1, 4. The original endomorphism space has dimension one and the image endomorphism
space dimension two. Thus fullness fails.
If a unital surjection to B6 existed, compose it with B6 ↠ k 6 . Every theta arrow joins distinct
idempotents. Its image in this commutative algebra is therefore zero, since those two idempotent
images are orthogonal. The image is generated by the three vertex idempotents and has dimension
at most three, whereas a surjection to k 6 would have image dimension six. This is impossible.
For a fixed theta dimension vector (d1 , d2 , d3 ), the arrays of six matrices satisfying the equations
are indeed the same affine variety as the circular complex variety of dimensions (d1 , d2 , d3 , d1 , d2 , d3 ),
after identifying the repeated vector spaces and bases. The acting groups differ:

21.2.2

Correct rank strata and the incidence resolution

The standalone circular-complex model remains valuable. Let Vi = k di , with indices modulo six,
and set
X (d) = {(Di ) : Di : Vi → Vi+1 , Di+1 Di = 0}.
A feasible rank vector satisfies

0 ≤ ri ,

ri−1 + ri ≤ di .

At each vertex choose a decomposition into the incoming image, a homology space and an outgoing
complement. Each outgoing complement maps isomorphically onto the next incoming image. This
proves that ranks classify orbits for the six-factor group. It also decomposes a complex into simple
vertex modules and length-two arrow modules. Thus B6 is representation finite, with six simples
and six indecomposable length-two projectives; it is not the category of the HLS diamond or of
band tubes.
Theorem 21.2 (Kernel incidence resolution). For a feasible r, use subspaces of dimension di − ri
and define
Z(r) = {(D, R) : dim Ri = di − ri , im Di−1 ⊂ Ri ⊂ ker Di }.
This is smooth, and its projection is proper and birational onto the closure of the rank-r stratum.
The maximal feasible vectors, in componentwise order, give the irreducible components of X (d).
Proof. Over

i Gr(di − ri , Vi ) the allowed maps are precisely

Q

Di ∈ Hom(Vi /Ri , Ri+1 ).
They form a vector bundle, so its total space is smooth and irreducible. The incidence conditions
are closed, and projection from the Grassmannian product is proper. Its image is the locus of
69

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

21

complexes with ranks at most r. To see surjectivity onto that locus, extend the incoming image to
a (di − ri )-dimensional subspace inside the kernel; feasibility gives both inequalities needed for that
extension. Full ranks ri occur in the bundle, since ri ≤ di+1 − ri+1 . On this open locus Ri = ker Di
is forced and varies regularly, giving an inverse there. The image is consequently the closure of this
stratum. Every complex has a feasible rank vector and these closures are ordered by componentwise
rank, proving the last assertion.
The index in di − ri matters. With all di = 1 and the maximal vector (1, 0, 1, 0, 1, 0), the
alternative di − ri−1 asks for a one-dimensional R1 inside the zero kernel of D1 . It has no fiber on
the intended dense stratum. This elementary counterexample rules out that incorrect resolution.
The theorem above describes the circular variety and its own group, not a nonexistent quotient map
of Proposition 21.1.

21.3

Zwara’s Hom control and covering Hom decompositions

For finite-dimensional algebras B, A, an exact k-linear functor F : mod B → mod A is Hom-controlled
in the sense used by Zwara if there is a bilinear form β : K0 (B) × K0 (B) → Z with
dimk HomA (F U, F V ) − dimk HomB (U, V ) = β([U ], [V ])

(21.1)

for all U, V . This is the explicit definition in [108, Section 6, p.71], following [107]. A fully faithful
exact functor satisfies it with β = 0, when these finite-dimensional hypotheses apply. The definition
does not consist of a sum indexed by deck translates.
The face unfolding fails even this weaker numerical test on a finite radical-square-zero quotient.
Take dimension (1, 1, 0), let M0 have a1 = 1, b3 = 0, and M1 have a1 = b3 = 1, all other maps
zero. Their original endomorphism dimensions are both one. After unfolding, M0 has one edge
summand and two isolated simples, so its endomorphism dimension is three; M1 has two disjoint
edge summands, so its endomorphism dimension is two. The defects are two and one at the same
K0 class. No bilinear form in (21.1) can describe them.
Under suitable covering and support hypotheses a Galois push-down has the separate identity
Hom(Fλ M, Fλ N ) ∼

The support hypotheses ensure the asserted direct sum is the appropriate space; an arbitrary
infinite-support category may require other finiteness conditions. Even when this formula holds, the
nonidentity summands can depend on the module structures rather than only on their K0 classes.
Proposition 21.3 (A finite covering counterexample). A Galois push-down need not be Homcontrolled in Zwara’s sense, even for a finite group and finite-dimensional algebras.

The geometric conclusion must also be stated accurately. The module-scheme result of [107]
gives the specified smooth-then-immersion factorization on connected components. The smoothness
results for orbit closures and rank schemes in [108, Theorems 6.1–6.2] have their own source, image
and singularity-type hypotheses. They do not say that every induced map into the entire module
variety is smooth. For example the quotient k[t]/t2 ↠ k has fully faithful inflation, but the point
with t = 0 is not a smooth immersion into the positive-dimensional square-zero matrix variety in
dimension two.

The same construction is available on each branch of the polynomial theta algebra at a nonzero

factorization would fail in the larger node finite-length category.

21.7

Derived AR triangles and an explicit infinite-pd endpoint

A short exact sequence gives a triangle in a bounded derived category, but almost splitness is
tested against all objects there, including shifts. For a finite-dimensional algebra an indecomposable
endpoint admits a derived AR triangle precisely when it is perfect; its translate is the derived
75

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

21

Nakayama functor followed by [−1]. In the finite-dimensional gentle setting, [109, Section 5.1]
applies this criterion to finite homotopy strings and bands, and describes the graded endpoint
rotations. The finite-dimensional, grading, boundary and local-system hypotheses remain part of
that theorem.
For our E = M (ab) the failure of perfectness can be seen directly. In the travel/right convention
the cover P1 → E has the eight-element basis
e1 , l, a, la, ab, lab, abr, labr,
and retains e1 , a, ab. Its kernel is lP1 ⊕ abrP3 . Multiplication by l : P1 → P1 has kernel lP1 , since
exactly the paths already starting with l are killed. The terminal simple repeats through r : P3 → P3
in the same way. Thus its minimal projective resolution has nonzero terms in arbitrarily high
degrees. In particular the module AR sequence ending in E in Figure 21.4 does not become an AR
triangle ending in E in Db (mod H3 ).
By contrast, the smooth-support block of Proposition 21.4 is hereditary. The operator (x − λ)r
is a nonzerodivisor of the polynomial node algebra and its cokernel is Tr , so it gives a length-one
free resolution. The indecomposables in that block’s bounded derived category are Tr [n]; each fixed
shift has the same homogeneous tube and the AR triangles are induced by its exact sequences. The
nodal stable lattice triangles above give another actual triangulated model. These two positive
descriptions do not identify all of Db , Perf and the stable lattice category.

21.8

Ringel’s wind-wheel tile: limits are part of the object

Ringel’s T (45) is the tile of the five-bar wind wheel specified in [106, Section 12.5, p.30], drawn in
Section 13.3, pp.38–40. It is part of an AR quilt, not just a finite-module mesh. Four finite-word
quarters surround a nonperiodic two-sided word. The diagonals consist of one-sided limiting words;
contracting ones use ordinary strings, expanding ones need completion, and the central mixed
word needs partial completion. The five-tile example glues to a torus with five holes. Deleting the
infinite-word points recovers its finite string components. This is a category construction, not an
assertion that its quilt genus equals the genus of our original Belyi pair.

Both sides of (21.3) are (1 − T a)(1 − T (a + ub)). This is a determinant-functor statement after the

On the reduced continuous finite-length representation variety of the one-vertex node, the
ordinary semi-invariant ring is in fact just k. Scalar matrices act trivially by conjugation, forcing a
nonzero character detm to have m = 0. The nilpotent filtration gives a one-parameter conjugation
degeneration to zero arrows, so all invariant regular functions are constant. This coexists with the
nontrivial lattice AR sequences and the relative determinant identity; the target data differ.

21.9.3

An inherited relation and a framed Plücker relation

A genuine unframed relation is obtained by retaining both arrows a : 1 → 2, b : 2 → 1 in a
Chebyshev quotient and killing all other vertices and arrows. At dimension (1, 1, 0) its coordinate
ring is k[a, b]/(ab). The two coordinates have opposite torus weights and are semi-invariants. Their
relation ab = 0 comes from the inherited zero relation; it was not deduced merely from a mesh
picture.
For a richer example use the actual elliptic-square order. Its dessin has σ = (1 2 3 4) and
α = (1 3)(2 4); there is one face, so 1 + 2 − 4 + 1 = 0 gives genus one. The two quiver vertices are
the two clean edges. The four arrows form one permitted alternating four-cycle, with two arrows in
each direction; every mixed consecutive choice is forbidden. Put V0 = k 2 , V1 = k 2 , set both reverse
arrows to zero, and let the two forward arrows have arbitrary matrices X, Y . These modules kill J 2
and therefore extend continuously to the completed order. Frame the source V0 : the acting group in
the next statement is only GL(V1 ). Write Z = [x1 , x2 , y1 , y2 ] and pij = det(zi , zj ).
Theorem 21.8 (A genuine framed generator relation). All six pij have target character det, and
their subalgebra is

k[p12 , p13 , p14 , p23 , p24 , p34 ] (p12 p34 − p13 p24 + p14 p23 ).
The stated quadratic is the full ideal of relations of these six functions.
78

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

21

Proof. On the dense open p12 ̸= 0, express the other columns in the basis x1 , x2 and expand
determinants. This gives the quadratic; polynomial continuation proves it everywhere. Conversely
any nonzero six-tuple satisfying it is realized by four columns. After choosing a nonzero p12 by
permutation, one may take
z1 = (1, 0)t ,

z2 = (0, p12 )t ,

z3 = (−p23 /p12 , p13 )t ,

z4 = (−p24 /p12 , p14 )t .

Their last minor has the required value by the equation. The zero tuple is also realized. The
quadratic is irreducible: its nondegenerate quadratic form has rank six, whereas a product of linear
forms has rank at most two. Its ideal is prime, so this image description proves it is the kernel of
the polynomial map on coordinate rings.
Z

The exact diagram behind decomposability is 0 → ker Z → k 4 −
→ k 2 → 0 on the rank-two locus.
Its exterior comparison relates complementary kernel minors. The relation extends to the lower-rank
boundary; the diagram is not an almost-split sequence.
The framing is indispensable. With X = I and Y = ( 01 00 ), p13 = 1, but swapping the source
basis makes it zero. Thus p13 is not a semi-invariant for the full original source-and-target group.
The coefficients of
det(X + tY ) = p12 + t(p14 − p23 ) + t2 p34
are full-group semi-invariants. The framed six-generator relation must not be misidentified as their
unframed presentation.

21.10

What this supplies to the physical and arithmetic program

The chapter supplies exact categories, explicit maps, parameter spaces and some genuine determinant
identities. These can organize an effective model’s states and couplings, but τ is a categorical
translation, not by itself a unitary time evolution or a self-adjoint Hamiltonian. A physical realization
must supply its Hilbert space, domain, positive metric and dynamics independently. Likewise an
arbitrary tube parameter is not a prime norm or a Frobenius eigenvalue.
A valid route from a mesh to polynomial functions requires a universal family, the correct group
action, determinant-admissible complexes and rank hypotheses. A route from those functions to an
arithmetic L-function additionally requires descent, local factors, conductor and archimedean data,
convergence or regularization, and a determinant-preserving positive comparison. The relative nodal
identity and framed Plücker model give usable intermediate constructions. They do not discharge
those arithmetic or analytic obligations, and no full GRH conclusion is asserted.

79

Chapter 22

Coverings, equivariant AR theory and
graded surfaces
The new covering calculation in Section 7 is useful for computations, precisely because its limitations
are explicit. The parent algebra is our infinite elliptic theta algebra, its truncation is a finite
quotient, and its cover has vertices indexed by integers. They are three different categories. The
nine-dimensional module in Proposition 7.2 and the higher-genus family in Corollary 7.3 show that
even this genuine covering does not reach every module or bounded derived object.

22.1

Reading the three new sources together

The lecture [74] supplies the covering language: locally bounded categories, exact push-down/pull-up
and graded adjunctions. Its Theorems 4.23 and 4.28 give precoverings on the specified module
and bounded derived categories. The AR-quiver covering theorem 4.18 assumes an actual Galois
covering between Hom-finite Krull–Schmidt categories; density cannot be dropped. A universal
bound-quiver cover also depends on its relations and presentation. It is not automatically a covering
of the compact surface.
The GLS paper [75] works over an algebraically closed characteristic-zero field. Its finitedimensional GLS algebras are not automatically our surface orders. Equivariantization transports
string/band information to certain non-string algebras. Theorem B includes ZD∞ , ZA∞
∞ and tube
components, with its stated indexing. Theorem C corrects a naive root-only prediction by the
additional rank vectors βi + (2r + 1)δ in the named tame affine family. Here vertexwise local freeness
and its rank vector concern the GLS vertex algebras; they are not freeness over the central DVR of
a surface order.
Schroll’s survey [76], particularly Sections 4–5, organizes graded gentle categories through
curves, local systems, crossings and twisted complexes, with grading encoded by a line field. Its
framework distinguishes perfect objects from finite-total-cohomology objects, and properness from
homological smoothness. These distinctions matter for our infinite algebras and for any completed
or operator-algebra version.

22.2

A cover that really preserves the relations

At finite L the cover is locally bounded: only lengths below L survive. Without the cutoff, each
vertex has infinitely many nonzero outgoing Hom spaces, so the locally bounded hypothesis used
above fails. Locally finite dimensional Hom spaces alone do not repair that failure. Conversely the

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

truncated algebra has extra length relations and is not entitled to all quadratic-gentle surface-model
theorems.

22.3

Equivariant modules, not an unproved folding rule

The branch swap preserves J L . Thus it acts on BL and defines the skew-group algebra BL #k[Z/2].
An equivariant object needs an isomorphism c : M → σM satisfying the cocycle equation σ(c)c = 1.
A fixed isomorphism class alone does not choose c. For the nine-dimensional witness, the unequal
branch ranks rule out even M ≃ σM ; the induced pair M ⊕ σM instead has a canonical swapping
action. This is a concrete use of equivariant machinery without asserting a GLS identification or
drawing a folded AR component whose almost-split property has not been checked.

22.4

Derived and symplectic data needed for a determinant

Let Perf(A) be the compact objects of D(A) and write Dfd (A) for finite-dimensional total cohomology.
For our infinite zero-graded A, its regular projective is perfect but has infinite k-dimension; hence
it does not belong to Dfd (A). This is already a counterexample to silently embedding the former
category in the latter. In proper smooth finite-dimensional situations the familiar equivalences have
stronger conclusions; these hypotheses cannot be omitted here.
A curve model must retain the dissection, stops or punctures, line field, intersection grading,
local systems and dg or A∞ enhancement. For perfect complexes, the finite deck decomposition of
the Hom complex is proved in Section 20. For a matrix of paths representing a differential one must
still check d2 = 0; intersections without degrees and composition data do not prove this identity.
A marked surface used for the Fukaya model is not automatically the original compact Riemann
surface with its analytic metric and all boundary data forgotten.
Completion to Λ requires continuous modules and the chosen topology. The finite quotient
modules extend continuously because a power of J annihilates them. They are not central-DVR-free
lattices: in the nine-dimensional example the central length-three parameter already acts by zero.
The cutoffs A/J L must not be renamed Λ/uL .
For a useful arithmetic determinant, these categorical constructions still need a prime correspondence, a perfect or analytically controlled target, a determinant-line comparison, conductor and
81

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

22

archimedean normalization, and positivity for that same arithmetic function. A derived equivalence
or an AR translation does not supply a Hilbert-space adjoint, self-adjoint domain or trace-class
convergence. These remain the spectral program’s independent load-bearing obligations.
The executable calculation is examples/coverings/theta_cover.py. The exact matrix obstruction is checked in lean-analytic/CoveringBand.lean. The latter verifies selected actual
matrices, not every category or equivalence discussed here.

Linear relations, filtrations and actual
branch modules
This chapter develops original 2053 lines 1097–1307 on the genuine infinite dessin algebra, correcting
the graph and category conventions before using them in a determinant calculation. The finite

algebra. The companion exposes every kernel/image dimension, the branch monodromy and its
polynomial-versus-completed-order status.

23.1

Linear relations and functorial branch filtrations

The original linear-relations chapter mixes graphs, partial relations and stable kernels. We keep these
different objects separate. For a field k, a linear relation is a subspace C ⊆ V ⊕ V ; its inverse swaps
coordinates and DC = {(x, z) : ∃y, (x, y) ∈ C, (y, z) ∈ D}. The full graph ΓT = {(x, T x) : x ∈ V }
always has dimension dim V , including pairs with zero output. In particular ΓnT (0) = 0, whereas

For odd m ≥ 3, use the actual clean dessin of y 2 = xm − 1, β = xm , on the compact smooth
curve, in characteristic zero. Its genus is (m − 1)/2; its infinite locally gentle algebra Am has vertices
i ∈ Z/m, arrows ai , bi : i → i − 1, and mixed consecutive branch products zero. Below paths act
in their direction of travel on left representations over any coefficient field k. Let Em be the full
category of finite-dimensional representations with every bi = 0 and ai invertible for i ̸= 0.
Theorem 23.1 (A concrete relation category on an infinite dessin algebra). The category Em is
equivalent to finite-dimensional pairs (V, T ) with intertwining maps. A normal form Nm (T ) puts V
at each vertex, a0 = T , and all other ai = 1. For n = dim V ,
V = K(T ) ⊕ I(T ),

K(T ) = ker T n ,

I(T ) = im T n .

These summands and their kernel/image filtrations are functorial. The first restriction is nilpotent
and the second invertible. The central full-turn parameter u acts as T . A finite Nm (T ) extends
continuously to the u-adically completed order precisely when T is nilpotent. Such modules are
torsion, not nonzero order lattices.
Proof. Transport all vertex spaces to vertex zero along the invertible arrows; the remaining map is
the full-turn monodromy T . Arrow-compatible maps become exactly hT = T ′ h. Finite kernel/image
chains stabilize by step n. If v = T n w and T n v = 0, then w ∈ ker T 2n = ker T n , hence v = 0;

rank-nullity proves the direct sum. Stability gives the two restrictions, and hT r = T ′r h proves
functoriality. Continuity on a finite-dimensional discrete space means some ur kills it. Conversely
nilpotence makes every formal series act by a finite sum. This proves the completion assertion.
Over an algebraically closed k, the indecomposable branch pairs are Jr (λ). The value λ = 1
is permitted for a polynomial band; it is not a forbidden deformation parameter. The nilpotent
blocks are strings, but are not all rigid: Nm (J2 (0)) is a nonsplit self-extension of Nm (J1 (0)). The
Hom dimension between two such Jordan pairs is min(r, s) for equal eigenvalues and zero otherwise.
These assertions concern this specified full subcategory, not all infinite modules.

actual cyclic-quiver invariant; it is not the missing arithmetic positive determinant comparison.

Proof of the dessin-category equivalence

Write ai : Vi → Vi−1 for i = 1, . . . , m − 1 and pi = a1 · · · ai : Vi → V0 , with p0 = 1; the rightmost
map acts first. Every pi is an isomorphism. Conjugating the arrow maps by these identifications
makes ai = 1 for i ̸= 0, and makes T = pm−1 a0 at the remaining arrow. All b-relations vanish. An
intertwiner is transported to maps hi with hi = hi−1 for i ̸= 0 and hm−1 T = T ′ h0 . Hence every
hi = h and hT = T ′ h. This proves full faithfulness and essential surjectivity, with inverse given
by (V, T ) 7→ Nm (T ). It is also an exact equivalence: kernels and cokernels of intertwiners are the
usual invariant kernels/cokernels; the identity arrow maps remain identities. Finite dimension is a
condition on modules, while Am itself is infinite dimensional through its arbitrarily long permitted
branch paths.
The sum of the full permitted turns on both branches is central in this closed dessin algebra.
On the chosen modules the b-turns vanish and each a-turn equals T , so u acts as T at every vertex.
If T n = 0, the action factors through Am /(un ) and extends to the completion. If T has a nonzero
invertible Fitting summand, no power of u kills that summand, precluding continuity. For T = Jr (λ)
with λ =
̸ 0, the obstruction is stronger: u − λ is a unit of k[[u]] but its rth power annihilates
the proposed module, impossible for a nonzero unital module. This rules out even an unqualified
algebraic extension of that band, not just a chosen topology.

23.2.3

Functoriality and the actual filtration

Kernel dimensions are nondecreasing and at most n. Once two successive kernels agree, applying T −1
shows that all later kernels agree. Thus they stabilize by n; rank-nullity gives image stabilization
as well. On K(T ), T n = 0. On I(T ), T (I(T )) = I(T ); finite dimension makes this a bijection. If
hT = T ′ h, induction gives hT r = T ′r h, hence
h(ker T r ) ⊆ ker T ′r ,

h(im T r ) ⊆ im T ′r .

This proves functoriality even when dim V =
̸ dim V ′ : choose a common stabilization exponent
for the two finite objects. The decomposition projection onto K(T ) along I(T ) is canonical and
commutes with all intertwiners; the existence of a Jordan basis is not needed. Finite dimension is
T
essential. On V = k[t], multiplication by t has stable kernel zero and stable image r tr k[t] = 0,
although V ̸= 0. The same construction Nm (V, t) is an actual infinite-dimensional module of Am .
Thus the finite Fitting split cannot be extended to every module of our infinite algebra merely by
repeating its notation.
Let kr = dim ker T r , with k0 = 0, and form Gr = ker T r / ker T r−1 . For a nilpotent Jordan block
of size s, kr = min(r, s), so dim Gr counts the blocks of size at least r, and 2kr − kr−1 − kr+1 counts
blocks of size exactly r. For J3 (0) ⊕ J1 (0) ⊕ J2 (1) the stable dimensions are 4 and 2; the kernel
dimensions begin 0, 2, 3, 4, 4. The Gr are functorial subquotients. Their dimensions do not specify a
natural choice of basis or split each filtration step.

The direction of γ matters: it is into M , contrary to one source diagram. Local finiteness is

kernels, cokernels and the usual exactness axioms; addition is on Hom groups, not on an alleged
group of quiver vertices. The abstract assumptions above do not assert that the family Fi is faithful
on arbitrary morphisms. Our Fitting example is concrete: B0 consists of nilpotent pairs, B× of
invertible pairs, S are inclusions and F0 , F× the two stable summands.

23.2.4

Strings, bands, Hom and one non-rigid string

In this branch category, Jordan normal form classifies indecomposables over an algebraically closed
field. Over a general field replace Jordan eigenvalues by powers of monic irreducible polynomials.
A Jordan block Jr (0) is one directed string with mr basis visits and mr − 1 nonzero successor
arrows; Jr (λ) for λ ̸= 0 is a permitted cycle band. Formal inverse letters are reverse traversals, not
inverse elements of Am . Strings and their reverses describe the same string module under the usual
isomorphism convention. Arbitrary infinite modules are not all classified by this finite calculation;
the actual finiteness hypotheses in [38] remain essential.
Identify a Jordan pair with k[t]/(t − λ)r . An intertwiner from size r to size s is determined
by the image of 1, annihilated by (t − λ)r . For equal eigenvalues this subspace has dimension
min(r, s). For different eigenvalues, Bezout’s identity makes t − λ invertible on the target, so
only zero remains. Full faithfulness above transfers this Hom computation to Am . The sequence
0 → J1 (0) → J2 (0) → J1 (0) → 0 is not split: with J2 (0)e1 = e2 , a lift of the quotient generator has
nonzero first coordinate and is not killed by J2 (0). Its image under Nm is a nonsplit ambient-Am
self-extension. Thus even the string Nm (J1 (0)) is not rigid. No assertion that this exact full
subcategory is derived fully faithful is needed for the nonsplitting argument.
For this particular closed Am , every oriented or inverse letter has exactly two admissible
successors: continue on its branch in the same orientation, or switch branch and reverse orientation.
Cancellation and forbidden pairs, including inverse forbidden pairs, remove the other choices. A fixed
ordering of these choices identifies each first-letter cylinder with {0, 1}N . The whole infinite-word
space is a finite disjoint union of Cantor spaces, hence itself Cantor. There are no terminating
words inside that space to remove. Arbitrary gentle quivers can have isolated or eventually forced
paths, so this conclusion is not universal. Algebraic covers retain the lifted zero-relations and deck
equivariance data; arbitrary periodic cover representations are not automatically bands or a unitary
time evolution.

23.2.5

A determinant comparison with its exact limits

On i Vi , Lm = diag(T, . . . , T ). For a direct determinant proof of (23.1), successively eliminate the
blocks 1 at vertices 1, . . . , m − 1 in 1 − zL. Block row additions preserve determinant and leave
1 − z m T at vertex zero. This works over k[z] without inverting z or T ; the zero-size case gives
one on both sides. The Fitting splitting separates a nilpotent factor 1 and the determinant on the
invertible summand. For instance m = 5, T = J2 (2) gives (1 − 2z 5 )2 , whose complex roots have
modulus 2−1/5 , despite being a genuine genus-two dessin module.
Under conjugation of T this is an invariant polynomial. With independent source/target bases a
presentation determinant is instead a semi-invariant with its determinant-character weight; those
actions must not be conflated. The polynomial equality does not construct a positive Hermitian
L

86

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

23

metric, an arithmetic Euler factor, a regularized determinant or a zero-preserving map to completed
L-functions. Functorial kernel filtrations similarly do not construct an equivalence of derived/Fukaya
categories. A fixed graded surface category and a determinant-compatible arithmetic realization
remain separate obligations.
The exact rational checker evaluates actual genus-two/four quivers, Hom equations, full-turn
matrices, both filtration directions and the nonsplit extension. lean-analytic/LinearRelations.
lean proves full graph identities, semiconjugacy and filtration preservation, actual finite-space
Fitting splitting/stabilization and unit-power annihilation. It does not package the entire dessin
category, Jordan classification, general splitting-functor proposition or determinant proof in Lean.

not supply an arithmetic spectral determinant or a zero-location theorem.
Theorem 23.4 (Unbounded normal-image faithfulness). In M5 (k[t])2 , index the stationary images
by i and the positive images by (b, i, j, q), where b ∈ {a, b}, 0 ≤ i, j < 5, q ≥ 0 and i < j or q > 0.
The positive image is tq Eij in factor b, with zero in the other factor. Its path length is 5q + j − i > 0.
All these images, including the shared stationary images, are linearly independent over every field.
Every finite normal expansion has uniquely recoverable coefficients, without a bound on degree or
path length.

Proof. Read a stationary coefficient from the first factor’s constant diagonal; read a positive
coefficient from the indicated factor, entry and degree. The index restriction ensures that no positive
image contributes to a constant diagonal. These linear selectors are biorthogonal to the images, so
applying each selector to a finite expansion recovers its coefficient.
This theorem is now kernel checked for actual polynomial matrix pairs. The membership and
spanning steps are supplied by the next theorem. Comparison with a separately constructed path
quotient remains a distinct algebraic formalization task.

23.4

Normal forms and the full module action

23.4.1

Why the polynomial matrix algebra is the path algebra

With our convention a path from j to i acts as Eij ; the rightmost arrow acts first. Every nonzero
positive path uses only the a-branch or only the b-branch, because mixed consecutive arrows are the
defining quadratic zero relations. Along either branch the path of length ℓ starting at j is unique.
Write
ℓ = j − i + 5q,
0 ≤ i, j < 5.
Its image is tq Eij in the chosen factor and zero in the other. For i < j, q ≥ 0; for i > j, q ≥ 1; for
i = j and positive length, q ≥ 1. The stationary path is the shared (Eii , Eii ).
These images are linearly independent: off-diagonal matrix units, positive diagonal monomials,
branch factors and degrees are independent; the shared constant diagonal units are independent of
all positive paths. They span H ×k5 H, since upper entries permit all polynomials, lower entries
are divisible by t, and only the constant diagonals of the two factors must agree. Products of
paths agree with matrix products. Thus the arrow assignment induces a bijective algebra map from
88

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

23

kQ/(ai bi+1 , bi ai+1 ) to B. This argument includes arbitrarily long paths; it imposes no nilpotence
relation on a full turn.
The central parameter u, the sum of all full turns on both branches, has image (tI5 , tI5 ).
Completing in this parameter is a separate operation. The polynomial algebra and its completion
are not identified by the normal-form calculation. The finite modules below are annihilated by u2 ,
so their continuous completed actions require only a finite Taylor polynomial.

23.4.2

Coefficient selectors without a truncation

Here is an explicit proof of Theorem 23.4 and the coordinate convention used by the implementation.
Let
S = {(i, j, q) : 0 ≤ i, j < 5, q ∈ N, i < j or q > 0},

K = {0, . . . , 4} ⊔ ({a, b} × S).

For a stationary index set vi = (Eii , Eii ); for a positive index set va,i,j,q = (tq Eij , 0) and vb,i,j,q =
(0, tq Eij ). These are elements of the ambient polynomial matrix-pair space, with no degree cutoff.
For M = (Ma , Mb ) define
λi (M ) = [t0 ](Ma )ii ,

λb,i,j,q (M ) = [tq ](Mb )ij .

Here the subscript b in the second formula denotes either branch. Every λx is k-linear, since
extraction of a fixed matrix entry and polynomial coefficient is linear.
We verify λx (vy ) = δxy , including the cases at degree zero. For two stationary indices this is
the ordinary diagonal matrix-unit identity. A stationary selector vanishes on a positive image in
the other factor. In its own factor it could see a constant diagonal only if i = j and q = 0, which
the definition of S excludes. Conversely, a positive selector applied to a stationary image could be
nonzero only at precisely the same forbidden slot. For two positive indices, different branches have
disjoint supports. In one branch the matrix entry forces both endpoints to agree and the monomial
coefficient forces the degrees to agree. These conditions give equality of the full indices. The witness
of the condition defining S carries no extra algebraic data. This proves biorthogonality in every
characteristic, using no division.
For any finitely supported c : K → k, linearity now gives

If the sum vanishes, every cx vanishes; if two sums agree, their coefficients agree. This proves linear
independence and injectivity of normal expansion on the actual k-module of finitely supported
functions. It does not replace finite support by a convergent or formal infinite sum.
The length formula also contains useful information beyond independence. If i < j then 5q +j > i
even at q = 0; otherwise q > 0 and i ≤ 4 give the same strict inequality. Hence ℓ = 5q + j − i is a
positive integer, and ℓ + i = 5q + j. For fixed starting vertex j and length ℓ, two such equations
imply i − i′ = 5(q − q ′ ). Since i, i′ ∈ {0, . . . , 4}, this forces i = i′ and then q = q ′ . In Lean the
subtraction is natural subtraction; positivity is proved before using the rearranged equation.
For example the a-path of length 12 from vertex 4 ends at vertex 2 and has image (t2 E24 , 0).
The length-4 a-path from 3 to 4 has image (tE43 , 0). Their product, with the rightmost path first, is
(t3 E23 , 0), of length 16. Replacing the second path by its b-branch version makes the product zero.
Degree-zero upper entries such as (E24 , 0) are permitted, but (E22 , 0) is not a stationary image: the
stationary element is the shared pair (E22 , E22 ). This last distinction prevents a spurious extra
idempotent in the proposed basis.

The selector theorem alone gives the faithfulness part of the intended quotient comparison.

properties require their own subsequent constructions. None of these finite-support identities supplies
an arithmetic self-adjoint determinant or controls zeta zeros.

Cb (i) = Pb (i, i).

The integer Lij lies between one and five. In particular Pb (i, i) is a positive full turn, whereas
Wb (0, i) is the shared stationary idempotent. These two elements are not interchangeable.
Directly following the arrows, the path Pb (i, j) crosses the weighted arrow b0 exactly dij times.
Its image is therefore tdij Eij in factor b, with zero in the other factor. The Lean proof checks
all five-by-five endpoint pairs for each of the two branches; this is a finite verification of a finite
combinatorial case split, not a bound on permitted path lengths. For all n ∈ N, induction gives
view Cb (i)n Pb (i, j) = tn+dij Eij


in factor b.

For n = 0 the power is the unit of the whole two-factor algebra, so multiplication by Pb (i, j) still
selects the correct branch. The induction step uses Cb (i) with image tEii and Eii Eij = Eij . No
assertion that a branch-only degree-zero diagonal belongs to the algebra is needed. Since a permitted
slot satisfies q ≥ dij , the choice n = q − dij constructs its preimage in A. This proves membership
at every polynomial degree.
For clarity, if i = 2, j = 4, q = 2, then Pa (2, 4) = a3 a4 and Ca (2)2 Pa (2, 4) realizes (t2 E24 , 0) by
a length-12 arrow word. For i = 4, j = 0, q = 1, we have Pa (4, 0) = a0 and the exponent q − d40 is
zero. The corresponding exponent must never be replaced by a negative power or by an inverse
arrow. The construction works over F2 as well as characteristic zero.

23.4.4

Spanning, multiplication and the inverse coordinate map

Let S be the k-linear span of all normal images in the ambient space. Membership just proved gives
S ⊆ A. We prove the reverse inclusion by showing that S is closed under the operations used to
generate A. The product rules are

The displayed nonzero output is always an admissible positive image. If q + s > 0 this follows from
its degree. If q + s = 0, nonnegativity gives q = s = 0, so the input conditions imply i < j = r < l.
Thus i < l and the output is admissible even at degree zero. This explains why closure cannot be
justified merely by ignoring the lower-triangular constraints.
By bilinearity the product of two finite linear combinations lies in S. Every vertex is one of its
normal images, and each arrow is a permitted image with degree zero except the wrap arrow, which
has degree one. The sum of the shared vertices is the unit. Scalar multiples of this unit belong to S.
Induction on the algebra generated by the arrows and vertices therefore gives A ⊆ S. In Lean this
is actual Algebra.adjoin_induction, not a predicate that assumes the desired normal form for
every element. Consequently A = S as submodules of the polynomial matrix-pair space.
Now lift each vx to an element vex ∈ A using its proved membership. Their images under the
injective inclusion are the already independent ambient images, so the lifts are independent. The
equality A = S gives surjectivity of finite expansion; independence gives injectivity. This constructs
the linear equivalence E on the genuine generated algebra. For any a ∈ A, apply the ambient
coefficient recovery identity to E −1 a to obtain
(E −1 a)x = λx (view(a)).
The formula holds for every element of A, not only on a sampled list of paths or generators. Its
finite support follows from the equivalence with finitely supported functions. No convergence of
an infinite expansion is asserted. Although the product table has been used to prove span closure,
we have not yet supplied the formal universal map from a separately constructed bound-quiver
path quotient. The distinction between a linear coordinate equivalence and that algebraic quotient
interface is retained.

23.4.6

Formal and computational coverage

The file lean-analytic/BranchExtension.lean defines B as the actual generated subalgebra of
polynomial matrix pairs, proves its infinite dimensionality through injective polynomial evaluation,
91

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

23

and constructs i, p as B-linear maps. The original 21 printed theorem types and axiom reports
cover the generators, infinite embedding, actions, exactness and absence of a module section. Eight
additional reports in NormalPath cover slot extensionality, positive length, the length equation,
recovery from start and length, biorthogonality, coefficient recovery, linear independence and injective
normal expansion. Ten further printed reports now establish actual generated-algebra membership,
equality with the normal span, lifted independence, bijective finite expansion and its explicit inverse
coefficients. The normal-form isomorphism with the separately constructed path quotient above is
proved in prose; it is not packaged as a Lean categorical equivalence. Nor is a Yoneda-Ext object or
the universal factorization property of an almost-split sequence formalized by these maps alone.
The exact checker examples/relations/check_extension.py independently uses unbounded
normal paths and polynomial matrix pairs. It checks 405 distinct paths through length 40, 7,225
products, 30 distinct loop powers and 80 whole-polynomial module actions. The full generator
system for a hypothetical section has coefficient rank 49 and augmented rank 50 over Q, F2 , F3 , F5 .
These finite diagnostics complement the symbolic proof; their finite cutoffs do not establish infinite
dimensionality by themselves.
The separate exact checker examples/relations/check_normal_paths.py checks 475 normal
images at degrees up to 1,000,003 (length 5,000,019), all 225,625 selector pairs, 1,030 direct arrow
steps, 15,625 composable-or-zero product comparisons and 200 finite expansions over Q, F2 , F3 , F5 .
Invalid constant slots are rejected. These are finite diagnostics of the code and conventions; the
unbounded assertion is established by the Lean theorem, not by the size of the tests.
A further deterministic checker, examples/relations/check_normal_algebra.py, constructs
all 50 shortest arrow words and 375 actual preimages, including degree 1,000,003, using sparse
binary powers of the full turns. It checks 5,625 product round trips and 200 exact expansions over
the four stated fields. Three ambient matrices with forbidden constant shapes fail reconstruction,
as required. These diagnostics test the implementation independently of finite degree bounds; the
general membership/span/equivalence result is the Lean theorem, not an extrapolation from these
samples.

Chapter 24

DG foundations, clock lifts and
endpoint-sensitive extensions
A derived construction begins with a differential and its cohomology, not with a proposed physical
clock. This chapter develops the DG material of the original 2053 manuscript, lines1308–1478.
Throughout this chapter k is a field. We use the actual infinite nodal and theta dessin algebras.
The source is preserved unchanged. Its unit-boundary example and self-extension count require
corrections; its higher-product and physical interpretations require explicit additional data.

24.1

Degrees, maps and a decisive contracting homotopy

We use cohomological grading. A unital DG algebra is a graded algebra B = n∈Z B n with
d : B n → B n+1 , d2 = 0 and d(ab) = d(a)b + (−1)|a| a d(b) on homogeneous elements. A DG map
preserves multiplication, the unit, degrees and the differential. It is a quasi-isomorphism when it
induces isomorphisms on every cohomology group. A left DG module has the corresponding Leibniz
rule for its action. The exterior generator’s degree must be declared; the notation Λ• (V ) alone does
not specify negative degrees.
L

Proposition 24.1 (A unit boundary is contractible). If ε ∈ B −1 satisfies dε = 1, the underlying
k-complex of every unital left DG B-module is contractible. In particular H ∗ (B) = 0; it is not a
DG resolution of a nonzero field with zero differential.
Proof. Define h : M n → M n−1 by h(m) = εm. Then dM h(m) = m − εdM m, so dM h + hdM = 1.
Every cocycle m is therefore dM h(m). For a right module the correct formula is h(m) = (−1)n mε:
its two compositions have opposite dM mε terms and again sum to m. Taking the regular module
proves the algebra assertion. The displayed homotopy is k-linear; graded B-linearity would require
an additional compatibility, such as graded centrality of ε. A unital chain map to a nonzero field
would send dε both to zero and to one, which is impossible.
The qualifier is essential for a general noncommutative DG algebra. Over our actual nodal
1
algebra A, let C = (A →
− A) in degrees −1, 0 and B = EndA (C) with commutator differential. The
inverse-arrow homotopy ε satisfies dB ε = 1. But a degree-−1 graded B-linear endomorphism of
C must commute with the even projection onto C 0 ; its only possible component C 0 → C −1 is
then zero. Thus C is acyclic and its underlying complex contracts, without being contractible by a
B-linear homotopy. Derived and homotopy categories must not be conflated.

93

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

24

For the source’s two-piece example k ⊕ kε, the differential is the identity kε → k. Both
cohomology groups vanish. The auxiliary identity dh + hd = f for a Koszul relation f is useful
instead of assuming nonzero cohomology [78].

The clean interval dessin on the sphere gives A = k[ξ, η]/(ξη), with basis 1, ξ, ξ 2 , . . . , η, η 2 , . . .. It
remains infinite dimensional. The variables ξ, η here are actual dessin loops; they are distinct from
the auxiliary persistence parameters x, y used later in the book. Set R = k[ξ, η] and consider

24.3

Completion and the diagonal coefficient ring

Write S = k[[ξ, η]]. The same two-term construction over S maps to
∼ {(a(ξ), b(η)) : a(0) = b(0)}.

Here is a coefficientwise proof, avoiding an unjustified interchange of completion and arbitrary
cohomology. The branch-evaluation map sends F to (F (ξ, 0), F (0, η)). Every equal-constant pair is
the image of a(ξ) + b(η) − a(0). Its kernel consists of series whose coefficients on both coordinate axes
are zero. Such a series is uniquely ξηG by shifting both exponents down one. The same shift proves
multiplication by ξη injective. Consequently the completed Koszul model has H −1 = 0, H 0 = Λ and
no other cohomology. All these maps are continuous for the maximal-ideal topologies: evaluation

preserves powers, and the product relation raises order by two. This is the actual completed surface
order already used in the node chapter.
An isomorphism H 0 (B) ∼

comparisons. Equivalences of derived DG-module categories use an external change-of-algebra
theorem with its hypotheses; they do not follow from a single ordinary algebra isomorphism on H 0 .

24.4

A clock that really lifts, and its semilinearity

For q ∈ k × , define σq (ξ) = qξ, σq (η) = q −1 η. Substitution is a polynomial algebra automorphism,
inverse to σq−1 , and fixes ξη. It acts coefficientwise on K, fixing ε, so
σq d(bε) = σq (ξηb) = ξησq (b) = dσq (bε).
Multiplicativity follows from the two-piece product formula. The completed substitution preserves
each maximal-ideal power, so extends continuously, with continuous inverse, to the formal DG
model and branch pullback. It is a semilinear chain automorphism over the ambient coefficient ring:
σq (rp) = σq (r)σq (p), not generally an R-linear map.
The diagonal DVR k[[u]], u = ξ + η, has branches (F (ξ), F (η)). The clock sends u to the pair
(qξ, q −1 η). If this is a diagonal series G(u), its two linear coefficients must agree; hence q = q −1 .
Conversely, if q 2 = 1, substitution sends F (u) to F (qu) and preserves the diagonal subring. It
fixes that subring pointwise exactly when q = 1, as the image of u shows. In characteristic two
these alternatives may coincide. Thus preserving the relation and order topology does not by itself
preserve a chosen fixed coefficient action.
For k = C and q = eiθ , the coefficient of ξ i η j acquires phase q i−j . A selected finite monomial
space with its declared orthonormal basis then has a unitary diagonal action. Choosing that finite
norm does not construct a canonical Hilbert completion of the order, a self-adjoint arithmetic
generator or an L-function comparison. The positive-transfer and unitary-logarithm distinctions
in the physics chapters still apply. Gluing DG clocks further requires actual compatible maps and
coherences; no gluing theorem is inferred here.

24.5

Theta self extensions require endpoints

The source calls its double-three-cycle example spherical. Keep that rational theta distinct from
the elliptic theta. Both have σ = (012)(345), but their dart involutions are respectively α =
(03)(15)(24) and (03)(14)(25). Write vertices as the sorted α-pairs 0, 1, 2; for the elliptic case these
are {0, 3}, {1, 4}, {2, 5}. The simples Si are the residue simples of the actual infinite dessin algebra.
The exact forbidden-word resolution of Theorem 2.1 has two positive-degree arms. After Hom to a
residue simple, every arrow differential vanishes, so Ext dimension counts the terminal vertices of
these arms, not just arrows leaving the initial vertex.
Proposition 24.2 (Two different Ext tables). For n = 0 the dimension matrix is the identity. For
n > 0, using right-module and path-travel conventions of the resolution chapter, the elliptic theta has
dimk Extn (Si , Sj ) = 2 1j=i−n mod 3 .
For the spherical theta, positive even n give 2 on the diagonal and zero elsewhere; odd n give zero
on the diagonal and 1 at each other vertex.

Proof. For the elliptic involution, both initial arrows lower the vertex label by one modulo three;
every next forbidden arrow does the same. Hence both length-n indexing words end at i − n. For
the spherical involution the forbidden dart permutation has cycles (04), (13), (25). Each two-step
arm returns to its initial vertex, while its first step reaches one of the two other vertices, one arm
each. This proves all positive degrees; degree zero is Hom between pairwise distinct residue simples.
Minimality of the exact resolution supplies the Hom differential vanishing already used above.
Thus neither example has two-dimensional self Ext1 . The two self-extension tables are

24.6

Higher products and a usable derived interface

For an uncurved cohomological A∞ algebra, mn has degree 2 − n. With the graded tensor sign
convention its identities are

When m1 = 0, the n = 3 identity makes m2 associative. The operation m3 is therefore not its
ordinary associator. Minimal-model existence over a field and the appropriate derived comparison
are external results of Keller [77, §§3.1,3.3,6.2]; they are not formalized here. For an ordinary algebra
concentrated in degree zero, taking m2 to be its product and all other operations zero already gives
a minimal model. For our nodal DG resolution the cohomology is likewise concentrated in zero, so
every higher mn , n ≥ 3, has zero target by degree. This model therefore supplies no compulsory
nonzero higher operation or physical curvature.
The Ext algebra of a sum of simples is a different graded object. To build its derived composition
data, choose an appropriate projective resolution P and use the DG endomorphisms with ∂f =
dP f − (−1)|f | f dP . Expanding twice cancels the mixed terms and leaves d2P f − f d2P = 0. Boundedabove projective resolutions in the ordinary module category give the familiar derived-Hom model;
completed and topological versions must specify their Hom spaces and admissible maps.
There is also a useful grading test. Suppose an Ext minimal model is diagonal in cohomological/internal degree and its operations preserve internal weight. On inputs of cohomological degrees

a1 , . . . , an , mn has output degree ai + 2 − n but internal weight ai . Diagonality forces n = 2

their Ext has this diagonal grading. A transfer using contractions chosen separately in each internal

Derived traces and genuine filtered DG modules

The graded enveloping algebra is B e = B ⊗k B op with Koszul signs; Hochschild objects are the
derived tensor B ⊗L
B e B and derived Hom R HomB e (B, B), with their homological or cohomological
conventions declared. Derived base tensor or flatness is needed beyond the field setting. Completed
continuous Hochschild theory also requires its completed tensor and continuity conventions. These
objects are not interchangeable with an arbitrary ordinary matrix trace.
For the ordinary commutative nodal algebra, one concrete calculation is HH0 (A) = A/[A, A] = A:
the degree-one Hochschild boundary sends a ⊗ b to ab − ba = 0. This quotient of the infinitedimensional algebra carries universal trace classes but chooses neither a functional nor a positive
state. Over C declare ξ, η self-adjoint and conjugate coefficients under ∗. The origin evaluation
φ(a) = a(0, 0) is a clock-invariant positive state, since φ(a∗ a) = |a(0, 0)|2 . Its negative is still an
invariant trace but is not positive, as its value at one shows. Even the positive choice has GNS null
space (ξ, η), so its GNS Hilbert space is just C. This follows directly from the displayed seminorm,
and shows that trace classes, positivity and a useful spectral representation are three separate
choices. No arithmetic determinant is obtained here.
A strict filtered DG module is a functor from a poset: its maps preserve degrees, actions and
differentials, identities are identities, and every comparable triple has exactly the same composite
map. A homotopy-coherent replacement must supply the higher coherences too; pairwise homotopies
alone do not define it. For an actual example over our nodal DG model,
Fa,b = ξ a η b K,

(a, b) ∈ (N2 )op ,

forms a family of DG K-submodules, with inclusion maps when a ≥ c and b ≥ d. Indeed multiplying
by the central degree-zero monomial commutes with d, and these ideals are closed under the K-action.
Inclusions compose literally; the clock preserves each ideal up to its scalar q a−b . Componentwise
divisibility also gives Fa,b ∩ Fc,d = Fmax(a,c),max(b,d) . This is a decreasing module filtration indexed
with the declared reversed order.
The declared involution also matters. For self-adjoint ξ, η, a nonreal phase q makes σq an algebra
automorphism which is not a ∗-automorphism. If a unitary implements it in a ∗-representation and
X = π(ξ) is self-adjoint, then U XU ∗ = qX is self-adjoint, so (q − q)X = 0 and hence X = 0; the
same holds for π(η). Thus this particular nonreal clock cannot act faithfully by unitary conjugation
with those self-adjoint branch conventions. This does not exclude other involutions or graph-operator
constructions with different adjoint generators; it forbids silently identifying their adjoints with
these declared ones.
The finite monomial windows below instead form increasing subcomplexes of k-vector spaces.
They are not R-submodules or DG K-submodules, because multiplying by a variable leaves the box.
Inverting both variables to manufacture increasing fractional modules would make f = ξη a unit:
then d(ε/f ) = 1 and the localized DG algebra contracts. Such localization loses the node rather
than improving its derived resolution.
The remaining source interfaces need their own constructions: bar/cobar, Ginzburg and Fukaya
enhancements are not selected by merely matching H 0 ; gluing up to homotopy requires coherent
maps. Nor does a symbol m3 identify a Hochschild deformation or a measured curvature. The
existing unitary-logarithm discussion is retained in the physics chapter. Its nonunitary counterpart
needs care: a singular finite matrix cannot be an exponential, since every matrix exponential is
invertible. An invertible unipotent matrix has a finite nilpotent logarithm in characteristic zero,
which is not a self-adjoint generator unless the matrix is the identity. These observations delimit
the operator interface without assuming a physical realization of the algebraic clock.

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

24.8
