Executable windows and exact verification coverage

The canonical laboratory shows a finite subcomplex, not a finite parent algebra. For dε = ξη it
retains source monomials 0 ≤ i, j ≤ N − 1 and target monomials 0 ≤ i, j ≤ N . Every source
maps injectively to the distinct target (i + 1, j + 1). The finite rank is N 2 , negative cohomology
vanishes, and the surviving 2N + 1 target monomials lie on the two axes. These windows exhaust
the polynomial resolution. For dε = 1 both layers instead use the same box and contract; for d = 0
both layers survive. The window is not an R-submodule and is not closed under multiplication, so
it must not be advertised as a finite DG subalgebra. The phase weight i − j is equal at the two
ends of every arrow. The second view displays the exact endpoint-sensitive theta Ext matrices.
NodalDG.lean uses the actual square-zero exterior-line coefficient algebra, with even/odd embeddings, differential and parity sign. It checks the Leibniz, homotopy, augmentation and semilinearclock identities, as well as the exact ambient polynomial sequence for the nodal relation. These
are actual algebra/module components. A categorical Z-graded DG object, completed topology,
A-infinity transfer and derived equivalence are not thereby kernel checked. Exact Python diagnostics
check symbolic identities, normal forms and the two actual dart systems. Browser figures provide
finite windows and phase illustrations; they are not numerical proofs of the infinite statements or of
GRH.

Part IV

Quantum models, trained
renormalization and physical
realizations

99

Chapter 25

Positive evolution, clocks and
holonomy
A surface order specifies algebraic paths, relations and gluing. A quantum model additionally
specifies a complex Hilbert space, an adjoint, a state, a Hamiltonian and a measurement rule.
Calling a path a clock does not make those choices. Keeping them explicit turns the physical
interpretation into a sequence of calculable questions.
This part retains finite clocks, infinite observable algebras, quantum field theory, learned graph
networks and molecular hardware. We distinguish a finite identity, a theorem with hypotheses, a
reported experiment and a proposed design. A conditional arithmetic spectral criterion belongs to
the second category: its missing determinant comparison cannot be supplied by a hardware analogy.

25.1

States and two different logarithms

A finite system has Hilbert space H = Cd , inner product v ∗ w, and density matrix ρ = ρ∗ ⪰ 0,
Tr ρ = 1. An observable is Hermitian and has expectation Tr(ρO). A pure state is vv ∗ for a unit
vector v; its global phase is invisible. A self-adjoint energy operator gives two different evolutions:
U (t) = e−itH/ℏ ,

T (a) = e−aH/ℏ ,

H = H ∗.

(25.1)

Here t and a have time units. The first is unitary, the second positive. The formal substitution
t = −ia is Wick rotation. A continuum field theory needs additional reconstruction and convergence
hypotheses.
Proposition 25.1 (Unitary and positive logarithms). For any unitary U and ∆t > 0 there is
a bounded self-adjoint H with U = e−iH∆t/ℏ . For a positive-definite finite matrix T , the matrix
H = −(ℏ/a) log T is Hermitian. These are distinct operations.
Proof. Choose a bounded real Borel argument ϑ on the circle. Functional calculus gives H =
−(ℏ/∆t)ϑ(U ). For the second assertion, unitary diagonalization of T gives positive eigenvalues λj
and log T = V diag(log λj )V ∗ .
The Borel argument need not be continuous or lie in the original C ∗ -algebra. The coordinate z
on S 1 has no continuous logarithm because of its winding. A singular nilpotent matrix has no matrix
logarithm: every exponential is invertible. One must model such a truncation by a contraction or
channel instead. For a unitary clock, different phase branches change energies by integer multiples
of 2πℏ/∆t; identical ticks can thus conceal different dynamics between ticks.
100

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

25

For positive T , top-eigenvalue normalization has the precise sign
Te = T /λmax ,

e = H + (ℏ/a) log λmax I ⪰ 0.
H

In contrast, i log T is skew-Hermitian, not this Hamiltonian. If ρ > 0 is a density matrix, K = − log ρ
is a dimensionless, nonnegative modular Hamiltonian. Its interpretation as physical energy requires
a chosen scale and a state-dynamics comparison.

25.2

Total positivity, wiring and physical positivity

A real matrix is totally nonnegative when all minors are nonnegative, and totally positive when all
are positive. These are ordered-basis conditions. Hilbert-space positivity means Hermitian positive
semidefiniteness. The matrix ( 13 27 ) is totally positive but not Hermitian. Its positive real eigenvalues
do not make its logarithm self-adjoint in the given inner product.
The oscillation theorem and planar-network parametrizations remain valuable [112]. In an
acyclic directed planar network, the Lindström–Gessel–Viennot cancellation expresses a minor of
its path matrix as a sum over disjoint path families. With compatible boundary orderings the
surviving signs are positive. Missing families can give zero minors, so positive edge weights do not
guarantee strict positivity. Concatenation preserves total nonnegativity, but not symmetry. Positive
Grassmannians organize these minors; they do not alone identify a physical scattering amplitude or
reconstruct a quantum field theory.
Proposition 25.2 (A finite positive-energy certificate). A real symmetric positive-definite transfer
matrix has a self-adjoint logarithmic Hamiltonian. Normalization by its largest eigenvalue makes
the energy nonnegative. Symmetric total positivity is sufficient, not necessary.
Proof. Use Proposition 25.1 and the normalization formula. Only positivity of the eigenvalues is
needed.

Transfer normalization, Gibbs normalization and energy extraction answer different questions.
For a row-stochastic P , detailed balance πi Pij = πj Pji makes S = D1/2 P D−1/2 symmetric,
1/4 3/4
where D = diag π. Its positivity is extra: P = 3/4 1/4 is reversible but has eigenvalues 1, −1/2.
The lazy transition (I + P )/2 has eigenvalues 1, 1/4 and a positive logarithmic interpretation.
Laziness changes the process; it is not a proof of positivity for the original process.
In Euclidean QFT, reflection positivity is a condition on reflected correlations. Together with
the other reconstruction axioms it produces a Hilbert space and positive-energy translations [113].
Entrywise nonnegative Boltzmann weights are not that condition. A family of finite positive matrices
does not alone prove locality, covariance or a continuum limit.

25.4

Infinite clocks: rotations and odometers are distinct limits

On ℓ2 (Z) let U |n⟩ = |n + 1⟩ and V |n⟩ = e2πiθn |n⟩. Then V U = e2πiθ U V . Under Fourier transformation, U becomes multiplication by eit and V becomes angle translation. They are not two names for
the same multiplication operator. The universal algebra on this relation is the rotation algebra Aθ .
Proposition 25.3 (Finite dimension detects a rational phase). If q-dimensional unitaries satisfy
this relation, then qθ ∈ Z. For θ = p/q, the pair Xq , Zqp gives a finite representation, but not a
faithful representation of the entire universal Ap/q .
Proof. Take determinants and cancel the nonzero determinants of U, V . At a rational phase U q , V q
are central; the finite model fixes them to I, whereas other central circle values are possible.
The irrational algebra has a canonical trace selecting the coefficient of U 0 V 0 in a Fourier
polynomial. This is not the ordinary trace of the identity on an infinite Hilbert space. Abstract
algebraic states, normal states in a representation and trace-class densities are distinct notions.
Continued fractions provide rational approximations |θ − pj /qj | < qj−2 , but the denominators
need not divide one another: successive Fibonacci numbers already refute that assertion. Exact
isometries intertwining both Weyl generators at different phases would imply (e2πiθj − e2πiθj+1 )J = 0,
which is impossible for a nonzero J. A limiting construction must specify approximate embeddings
or a continuous field. Elliott–Evans approximations use matrix algebras over the circle, not a naive
AF limit of finite matrix algebras [114].
A separate infinite clock chooses nj | nj+1 and forms X = lim Z/nj Z. Addition by one is an
←−
odometer with Haar invariant measure. Compatible local extensions C((u1/nj ))/C((u)) have these
finite cyclic groups. Their inverse Galois limit and the direct union of fields point in opposite
b a selected tower gives the corresponding procyclic
directions. The full set of denominators gives Z;
quotient. This preserves the ramification interpretation without identifying it with one irrational
rotation algebra.

Holonomy, DG compatibility and persistence

Assign Hilbert fibers to graph vertices and unitary transports to oriented edges, with inverse on
reversed edges. Their based loop product is holonomy; changing vertex bases conjugates it. For a
triangular loop of complex lines with phases eiϕj it is ei(ϕ1 +ϕ2 +ϕ3 ) . This is a gauge-invariant phase
defect, not yet a gravitational redshift.

Operators on different tensor-product registers instead define many-body observables. Their product
is not automatically transport on one fiber. The distinction must be fixed before designing a
holonomy measurement.
Chapter 24 gives a corrected DG model of the actual infinite node, its clock lift and the endpointsensitive theta Ext calculation. A DG enhancement has d2 = 0 and a graded Leibniz rule. A clock

periodic syzygies are not themselves a Hamiltonian or a time period.
A state-derived bifiltration can use increasing spatial radius and a decreasing correlation threshold.
The order must give actual inclusions. For example, four edges around a square give an H1
class; adding two triangles fills it. Multiparameter rank invariants, Betti tables and jump loci
are meaningful diagnostics, but there is generally no canonical complete two-parameter barcode.
Stability needs bounds on the actual filtration functions, including the statistical error of a nonlinear
correlation-to-distance conversion. It does not identify a spectral or arithmetic determinant.

Entanglement, channels and RG
requirements
A renormalization channel needs an objective: which observables, states or phase labels should its
coarse variables retain? Training selects parameters from data. This chapter supplies a solvable
optimization task and a graph extension, while distinguishing an analytic optimum from an executed
training run.

26.1

An entangled pair and the finite first law

Entropy is S(ρ) = − Tr(ρ log ρ), with 0 log 0 = 0. Reduced states use partial trace; mutual
information is I(A : B) = S(ρA ) + S(ρB ) − S(ρAB ). We use natural logarithms. For
|ψp ⟩ =

√

p|00⟩ +

p

1 − p|11⟩,

0 < p < 1,

the reductions are diag(p, 1−p), so S(ρA ) = h(p) = −p log p−(1−p) log(1−p) and I(A : B) = 2h(p).
The modular Hamiltonian is KA = diag(− log p, − log(1 − p)). At p = 1/2 its flow is trivial although
the pair is maximally entangled. Entanglement amount therefore does not determine a nontrivial
local modular clock rate.
Proposition 26.1 (Finite first law). If σ > 0 is a density matrix and ρ(ϵ) is differentiable, ρ(0) = σ,
then
d
S(ρ(ϵ)) = Tr(ρ′ (0)(− log σ)).

Proof. In a diagonal basis, the derivative under the trace is d Tr(X log X)[Y ] = Tr(Y (log X + I));
off-diagonal divided-difference terms have zero trace. Use Tr ρ′ (0) = 0.
For the pair above, h′ (p) = log((1 − p)/p) is exactly Tr(diag(1, −1)KA ). The reference modular
Hamiltonian stays fixed in this variation. A rank-deficient reference needs support control or a
declared regularization such as (1 − η)ρ + ηI/d.
Relative entropy D(ρ∥σ) = Tr ρ(log ρ − log σ) is nonnegative, with equality only at equal states;
incompatible supports give infinity. This is a strict matrix-convexity result, not the assertion
that equality in a limit forces equality in an intermediate Hölder inequality. For the Gibbs state
σ = e−βH /Z one obtains K = βH + log Z. A general reduced state has no automatically identified
physical energy or physical time parameter.

Correlation geometry and the gravitational research question

On a finite graph assign symmetric regularized lengths
ℓe = ℓ∗ max{ε, − log(max{Ie , δ}/I∗ )},

ℓ∗ , ε, δ, I∗ > 0.

They define a shortest-path metric. Zero lengths would yield a pseudometric; infinite lengths could
disconnect the graph. If two length assignments differ by at most η on a graph with N vertices,
shortest simple paths have at most N − 1 edges, hence their distances differ by at most (N − 1)η.
A patchwise bound can be smaller. The logarithm is ill conditioned near zero correlation, which
makes the regularization and its error propagation part of the model.
A regular tree and H2 are both hyperbolic metric spaces but have different boundaries, Cantor
and circle, and are not quasi-isometric. Negative curvature does not alone give logarithmic diameter
for every finite quotient. Expansion or a controlled family is needed. Arbitrary edge lengths need
not form valid triangles for angle-defect curvature; metric-only curvature surrogates also require
their neighborhood measures.
One can postulate a rate ratio rv /r∗ = e−αεv for an entanglement proxy. This is a falsifiable
ansatz. A bounded cyclic number expectation cannot increase forever, so an operational rate should
use an unwrapped phase, counted transitions or calibrated oscillations. Matched noise-only controls
must distinguish frequency changes from lost contrast. The entanglement-equilibrium route to
Einstein equations has extra geometric, area, energy and continuum assumptions [115]. The finite
first law does not supply them. Persistence can test stability across thresholds; it cannot convert
this ansatz into Einstein dynamics.

26.4

Trained QGCN and QGAT on ribbon graphs

Give the graph its cyclic half-edge ordering and a proper edge-coloring. Shared two-site gates can
run in parallel on each color class. A simple bounded-degree graph requires at most ∆ + 1 colors, not
universally ∆. Matching-based pooling specifies disjoint retained/discarded subsystems and a ribbon
contraction. Independently applying channels to overlapping marginals does not automatically
produce a consistent global coarse state.
Quantum convolutional and graph-network proposals offer concrete ansatz families [117, 118, 119].
A trained RG task may target phase classification, selected correlators, a reconstructed state, or an
effective Hamiltonian. Success on one does not prove success on the others. A possible loss combines
observable errors and symmetry penalties:

The penalty encourages cyclic weight sharing; exact equivariance requires intertwining by the
architecture. Geodesic CNN coordinates and ribbon orderings are related design inputs, not
identical constructions. Random quanvolutional circuits provide a useful untrained baseline. They
must be compared with trained circuits and a classical model on the same data rather than credited
with an automatic quantum advantage.
For a gate e−iθP/2 with P 2 = I, parameter-shift differentiation gives
∂θ f = 12 (f (θ + π/2) − f (θ − π/2)).
Indeed the surrounding expectation has only constant, cosine and sine terms in this parameter.
Other generators require a different rule. Finite-shot gradients and exact-state-vector gradients
have different noise and optimization costs. Clifford layers can create entangled graph and GHZ
states; non-Clifford gates are needed for universality, not for entanglement itself.
QGAT scores can be obtained from edge-register query/key circuits followed by classical softmax.

input encoding and classical processing are where these feature maps can become nonlinear in
classical data.

Associative memory with a correct measurement model

A normalized readout uses positive effects Ea summing to I, with pa = Tr(Ea U ρU ∗ ). An incomplete
readout has sum at most I and a failure effect. For orthogonal rank-one projectors and pure input,
Ea = |ξa ⟩⟨ξa |,

pa = |⟨U ∗ ξa , ψ⟩|2 .

This is exact. A common unitary preserves the templates’ inner products; it does not independently
prescribe arbitrary nonorthogonal templates. Arbitrary projectors are not a POVM: two identical
|0⟩⟨0| projectors give probability sum two at |0⟩. A general Hermitian observable also need not be
positive and cannot be interpreted as one rank-one pattern effect through a generalized square root.
For a two-pattern test, measure the computational basis on Ry (x)|0⟩. Then p0 = cos2 (x/2) and
p1 = sin2 (x/2). A larger-probability rule retrieves zero for |x| < π/2 modulo 2π. Sampling makes
queries near the boundary difficult. This is a concrete retrieval task, not an exponential-capacity
theorem.
Classical modern Hopfield networks have capacity and retrieval guarantees under specified
separation, normalization and temperature assumptions [120]. Substituting dnq for their associative
dimension does not prove a QGAT memory theorem. A neighborhood with δ keys contains δ explicit
candidates per head. Capacity requires a precision criterion, a dataset and a resource model. A
D-dimensional system perfectly distinguishes at most D orthogonal states in one use; nonorthogonal
packing, classical parameter storage and repeated queries are different resources.
Holographic reduced representations bind classical vectors by circular convolution. For the
bb
unnormalized discrete transform, ad
∗b=a
b. Correlation is an exact inverse only under additional
P
nonvanishing/normalization conditions. A qudit’s ⟨Zdm ⟩ = j ρjj ω mj determines diagonal populations when all modes are known, but no off-diagonal phases. Additional bases distinguish |+⟩ and
|−⟩, which have equal populations.
A channel hierarchy can be drawn as a tensor network with coarse bulk variables and fine
boundary variables. Holographic-code models use a specified isometric encoding and reconstruction
property [121]. Arbitrary discarding does not have it: a channel sending both |0⟩ and |1⟩ to one
state destroys the label. A pooling tree alone proves neither boundary-area scaling of an actual
state, error-correcting reconstruction nor AdS/CFT. These remain separate questions that a trained
graph model can investigate.

26.6

Evidence categories for learned coarse-graining

An executable example must record graph, encoding, task, ansatz, loss, train/validation split, seed,
optimizer and stopping rule. It should save parameters, predictions and loss history, compare
classical and untrained baselines, and distinguish exact, finite-shot and hardware expectations.
A noise study must name the channel and where it is applied. A successful finite toy task does
not certify a thermodynamic RG limit, quantum speedup, Einstein dynamics or an arithmetic
determinant.
The solvable pooling objective supplies a precise acceptance test for the separate executable
examples. A training result is reported only when its checkpoint and verification outputs exist. The
mathematical model and its analytic optimum remain meaningful independently of such a run.

Chapter 27

Noise, molecular spins and
hyperbolic-code architectures
An architecture needs controls, interactions, preparation, measurement and a noise model. A
coupling graph supplies none of these calibrations by itself. Exact small operations provide useful
acceptance tests before considering larger processors or molecular assemblies.

27.1

Ancillas, noise and measurement resources

For a contraction A, the map
v 7−→ Av ⊗ |0⟩ + (I − A∗ A)1/2 v ⊗ |1⟩
is an isometry: its squared norm is ∥v∥2 . It extends to a unitary with an environment. Postselecting
zero realizes A with probability Tr(AρA∗ ). Tracing out the ancilla includes both branches and does
not produce just AρA∗ as a trace-preserving map. An operator of norm above one must first be
scaled, with its success probability and amplitude normalization accounted for.
Amplitude damping has Kraus matrices

Direct multiplication gives K0∗ K0 + K1∗ K1 = I and multiplies the excited population by 1 − γ. Pure

model, 1/T2 = 1/(2T1 ) + 1/Tϕ ; these relaxation times alone do not determine pulse calibration,
leakage or gate fidelity.
A Hadamard test estimates Re Tr(ρU ); a changed ancilla basis estimates the imaginary part.
Repeated preparations are required. Local randomized measurements and classical shadows can
reuse settings for many observables, with locality-dependent costs [122]. Entropy is nonlinear, so a
linear-observable sample bound does not automatically estimate mutual information. Reconstructing
small marginals or using dedicated purity/entropy estimators is necessary.
At N = 104 sites and maximum degree ∆, at most ∆N/2 edge marginals are needed. This
defines a proposed workload, not an available processor specification. Routing, state preparation,
correlated readout errors and reset must enter a resource estimate. Likewise 105 qudits of dimension
eight require at least 3 × 105 binary qubits, before ancillas and coding. A generic encoded qudit
entangler does not cost merely log2 d two-qubit gates. Its actual compilation and unused basis states

The peptide-spin evidence and its limits

Lanthanide-binding peptides offer chemically programmable spin organization. Rosaleny and
collaborators reported ensemble pulsed X-band EPR in frozen solutions at 4 K, with representative
relaxation values [123]:

Driven nutations motivate a molecular-spin platform, but are not randomized-benchmark gate

interfaces must be distinguished from a demonstrated controlled multi-qubit processor. Bindingaffinity improvements are not measurements of increased coherence.
PDB 1TJB is a 2.00 Å Tb-bound structure [124]. Its motif has seventeen amino acids,
YIDTNNDGWYEGDELLA, with a terminal amide entry in the deposited entity. Eight nearby
oxygen donors come from Asp3, Asn5, Asp7, the Trp9 backbone carbonyl, and bidentate Glu11 and
Glu14. Deposited residue identifiers and sequence indices need reconciliation in atomic constraints.
Replacing the metal or scaffold requires new coordination and magnetic validation; a Tb structural
model is not already a calibrated Gd or Nd control model.
An effective spin Hamiltonian is
Hspin = HCF + µB B · gJ + I · AJ + Hdrive .
Selecting a qubit requires a named transition, selection rules and leakage bounds. In a positive-field

is not the lowest doublet. Spectroscopic selection can still use it, but must not be justified by a
false energy ordering.

A proposed molecular-design program

RFdiffusion3 supports atomically conditioned protein design and structural benchmarks [125].
Proteína-style backbone generation and BoltzDesign-style inverse-predictor optimization are complementary candidate methods. None guarantees a target spin coherence time, interaction graph
or gate. A reproducible calculation pins a release, checkpoint, input schema, atomic identifiers
and constraints; generated structures become preserved inputs for later evaluation. Current RFD3
documentation uses structured input specifications and its model-specific design interface, not an
assumed legacy RFdiffusion invocation.
A conservative motif workflow fixes a resolved coordination cage, specifies metal/donor atoms
and the intended state, generates surrounding scaffolds, assigns sequences with fixed motif positions,
and tests structure and metal-aware energetics before synthesis. Hydrogen-bond conditioning is not
a lanthanide coordination validator. RMSD and prediction confidence are filters, not physical yields
or gate measurements. A small-motif benchmark does not guarantee a pass rate for a seven-motif,
1000-residue assembly.
ThermoGFN-IF can be formulated as a proposed multi-fidelity optimization [126]. SPURS
supplies mutation stability predictions, BioEmu-v1 protein backbone ensembles, and UMA learned
atomistic energies and forces in evaluated domains [127, 128, 129]. Model domains matter: BioEmuv1 does not explicitly represent metal ions and their donor-oxygen geometry, so it cannot directly
certify an intact Ln cage. All-atom reconstruction and a separately validated metal-aware calculation
would be extra stages. Atom-count throughput is not a certificate of spin-vibronic accuracy.
P
For declared dimensionless scores define R(x) = exp( j wj sj (x)). If positive ∆∆G denotes
destabilization, its stability reward contribution should have a negative sign. A GFlowNet aims to
sample terminal candidates proportionally to this specified reward. Trajectory balance compares full
forward/backward path probabilities to the terminal reward; subtrajectory balance additionally uses
weighted subpath constraints. A unique construction path restricts the legal backward policy. These
definitions can be implemented and tested without claiming a synthesized or operating processor.
110

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

27

Increasing melting temperature need not monotonically raise a Debye scale, suppress a particular
spin-phonon channel or improve T2 . At a fixed transition frequency, Bose occupation depends
on that frequency and the temperature, not directly on bulk rigidity. A dephasing power law
needs its spectral density, coupling operator and mechanism, including direct or Raman processes
when relevant. One nanosecond of dynamics does not alone estimate a converged folded/unfolded
partition-function ratio. Proposed tables mapping ∆Tm to numerical coherence, gate fidelity and
fault tolerance are therefore hypotheses, not measured outcomes.

27.5

Hyperbolic kagome geometry and the assembly problem

A regular {p, q} tiling has p sides per face and q faces at a vertex. At curvature −1, a face has
angle 2π/q and area π(p − 2 − 2p/q). In a closed genus-g quotient, pF = 2E, qV = 2E and
V − E + F = 2 − 2g. For {7, 3},
E = 42(g − 1),

V = 28(g − 1),

F = 12(g − 1),

Af = π/3.

(27.2)

Then F Af = 4π(g − 1) as Gauss–Bonnet requires. The orientation-preserving (2, 3, 7) triangle
group has coarea π/21; a torsion-free subgroup has index 84(g − 1). Its reflection triangle has
half this area. The Klein quartic, with usual model x3 y + y 3 z + z 3 x = 0, has genus three and
(V, E, F ) = (56, 84, 24). It is not a construction of every proposed quotient.
For a simple cubic tiling graph, its line graph places a qubit at each edge and joins pairs
that share a vertex. Each qubit has four neighbors; the line graph has E vertices and 2E edges.
Quotient degeneracies such as loops or repeated incidences need separate counting. Equal hyperbolic
edge lengths do not automatically become equal Euclidean separations or physical couplings in an
assembly.
A finite graph can be embedded and routed in three-dimensional space, but this does not create
a complete smooth isometric H2 in R3 . A gyroid has varying intrinsic curvature. Schwarz–Pick
concerns holomorphic metric contraction, not an SE(3) protein-placement algorithm. A finite
assembly needs target Euclidean coordinates, distortion and steric bounds, mechanical constraints
and explicit chemical ownership of shared sites. Kabsch alignment is a least-squares rigid fit; it
does not remove inconsistent global distances. Two independently generated chunks cannot both
contain one supposedly shared physical peptide without an actual chemical construction realizing
that sharing.

27.6

CSS codes from boundary maps

Let a finite connected closed orientable cellulation have
∂

∂

2
1
C2 (Σ; F2 ) −→
C1 (Σ; F2 ) −→
C0 (Σ; F2 ),

∂1 ∂2 = 0.

This is a chain complex, not a short exact sequence. Its first homology is precisely the code’s logical
information. Put qubits on edges and set HX = ∂1 , HZ = ∂2T . A binary row x defines an X product
and a row z a Z product. Their commutation sign is (−1)x·z , so the boundary identity proves
commutation. The code is the common +1 eigenspace in (C2 )⊗E , not a binary kernel intersection
regarded as that Hilbert space.
Proposition 27.1 (Surface-code dimension and distance). For this genus-g cellulation,
k = E − rank HX − rank HZ = 2g.
111

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

27

Distance is the smaller weight of a nontrivial primal homology class and a nontrivial dual homology
class.
Proof. Connectedness gives rank ∂1 = V − 1, and the one-dimensional top homology gives rank ∂2 =
F − 1. Thus k = E − V − F + 2 = 2g. A commuting Z error is a cycle; multiplication by Z
stabilizers changes it by a boundary. A nontrivial logical Z is therefore a nonzero homology class.
The dual argument treats X errors.
A noncontractible separating curve can be null homologous. Searching one geometric systole
therefore does not certify a code’s distance. In a {7, 3} code, vertex X checks have weight three and
face Z checks weight seven; a global swap of X, Z conventions also swaps the logical descriptions.
At genus thirteen, (27.2) gives 504 data qubits and 26 logical qubits. If E = 1008, the genus is
25 and k = 50. The asymptotic rate is 1/21. A {5, 4} cellulation gives E = 20(g − 1) and rate 1/10.
Syndrome ancillas add physical resources. Suitable hyperbolic quotient families have logarithmic
distance at constant rate [130, 131]; topology alone gives no such lower bound for every quotient, or
distance eight for an unspecified finite surface. A finite certificate needs its incidence matrices and
primal/dual logical-operator search. Logical qubit count grows linearly, not exponentially, with the
number of physical qubits.
A finite acceptance test. An L × L periodic square cellulation, L ≥ 3, has V = F = L2 ,
E = 2L2 . Thus k = 2 and d = L: a straight winding row or column gives an L-edge logical cycle in
either lattice, and a lifted winding cycle must traverse at least L edges. This yields [[2L2 , 2, L]] with
weight-four checks. The ranks L2 − 1, L2 − 1 verify k directly. This is a useful exact decoder test
before claiming a larger unprovided hyperbolic instance.
A storage code is not yet a fault-tolerant computer. Generic exchange and Zeeman terms do
not commute with every stabilizer. An XX interaction meeting a Z check on exactly one site
anticommutes with it, giving a commutator of order the coupling. Syndrome extraction needs
ancillas, controlled operations, reset and measured error models; three exchange pulses alone do not
prove nondemolition parity measurement. A threshold is conditional on code family, decoder and
noise model. Code-capacity noise with ideal checks and circuit-level noisy checks are different. A
gate estimate from another model cannot be compared to one threshold and declared fault tolerance.

27.7

The arithmetic comparison is still an additional task

A physical graph, a complex Belyi dessin and a rational arithmetic model are different data. A
passport need not determine a Galois orbit, and one finite dessin cannot support a faithful action
of the entire absolute Galois group on its finite sheet set. Frobenius requires descent and good
reduction. Measured couplings do not automatically recover an Euler factor. The earlier finite-field
theta benchmark is a concrete comparison test with its already specified scope, not a global GRH
proof.
There is a useful circuit sanity check. XY exchange annihilates |00⟩, so its exponential fixes it. A
network of these gates, on an all-zero register preceded only by diagonal Z rotations, has a vacuum
overlap that is just a phase. It cannot generate L-function zeros. Also e−izG for Hermitian G is not
Q
generally unitary at complex z. Finite products p (1 − χ(p)p−s ) are exponential polynomials in s,
not degree-N polynomials; their unramified factor zeros have Re s = 0, not the critical line.
A Hilbert–Pólya proposal still has to identify its determinant with the same completed arithmetic
function, including gamma and conductor factors. The transformation ρ 7→ 1 − ρ fixes a zero on the
critical line; it does not reverse the ordinate. Conjugate twists matter for complex characters. Chiral
112

symmetry is extra structure. An unbounded ±-paired self-adjoint
spectrum has no Gibbs trace

is a different diagnostic. Similarly ∆ − 1/4 needs a spectral restriction
away from negative shifted modes and a treatment of continuous spectrum.
These chapters give finite calculations, trainable channels and concrete experiments. They
preserve the physical research program while leaving its arithmetic determinant, purity, convergence
and positive-realization obligations explicit. They establish no complete proof of GRH.

Chapter 28

Actual renormalization: effective
actions and scale
Renormalization changes the description while preserving a specified set of physical predictions. To
make that claim testable, we carry the effective action, its constant, observables and scale convention.
Neural state compression and principal-axis alignment can be useful auxiliary techniques, but their
training losses do not replace these objects.

28.1

Actual renormalization retains the effective action

A compression score does not specify an RG transformation. Following the real-space formulation
of Wilson and Kogut [81], we specify the eliminated variables, effective action, normalization and
scale map. Our exact finite decimation is distinct from an approximate tensor-network truncation
such as Levin–Nave TRG [82].
Definition 28.1 (Finite classical RG step). Let the fine configuration be (s, t) ∈ S × E, with finite
nonempty sets S, E, and dimensionless log Boltzmann weight V (s, t) = −βH(s, t). Define
(RV )(s) = log

The full action includes its additive constant. A truncation or a learned approximation is a further
operation with its own error, not this identity.
Proposition 28.2 (Partition, observables and composition). One has Z(RV ) = Z(V ), and the
normalized expectation of every observable depending only on s agrees in the two descriptions.
Eliminating E1 and then E2 equals eliminating E1 × E2 at once, with the same tensor labels and
inverse temperature.
Proof. Exponentiating the definition gives eRV (s) = t eV (s,t) . Sum over s, optionally multiplying
by an observable O(s), and divide both sides by the same strictly positive Z. Reordering finite
sums proves the composition statement. No projection onto pair interactions is used.

28.1.1

Genuine dessin incidence models

For odd m, the clean dessin y 2 = xm − 1, β = xm has two black vertices, m white vertices and one

B+ , B− , s0 , . . . , sm−1 on the incidence vertices; this is a specified finite spin model, not a faithful
representation of that infinite algebra or order. The abstract incidence graph alone does not recover
the ribbon embedding or genus.

28.1.2

Quantum thermal effective Hamiltonians

For a finite Hermitian Hamiltonian H on nonzero finite-dimensional HS ⊗ HE and β > 0, define
W ′ = TrE e−βH ,

Heff (β) = −β −1 log W ′ .

The matrix W ′ is strictly positive: for v ̸= 0, sum the strictly positive quadratic forms of v ⊗ ej over
an orthonormal basis of HE . Its Hermitian spectral logarithm is therefore defined. The identity
e−βHeff = W ′ proves partition and retained equal-time observable preservation by the partial-trace
property. Repeated traces compose. The full Pauli expansion, including its identity coefficient,
is retained. It is generally nonlocal and depends on β; this statement does not preserve all fine
real-time dynamics or supply a local zero-temperature/thermodynamic RG fixed point.
FiniteRG.lean checks nine actual finite scalar marginal, exponential-action, partition, observable, composition, error-sandwich and transfer-trace statements. The quantum spectral-log and
complete thermodynamic/field-theory interfaces are not formalized by these lemmas. These physics
constructions give no arithmetic determinant comparison or new GRH conclusion.

28.2

Controlled errors and a trained full-action RG approximation

Proposition 28.3 (Classical action-error propagation). If |Vb (s, t) − V (s, t)| ≤ ε at every finite
configuration, then |RVb (s) − RV (s)| ≤ ε. The partition ratio lies in [e−ε , eε ]. Every further exact
marginalization preserves the same action bound.
Proof. The pointwise inequalities e−ε eV ≤ eVb ≤ eε eV survive finite summation. Taking logarithms
proves the first assertion; summing also over the retained variables proves the second. Iterate
the same argument. For normalized measures the likelihood ratio lies between e−2ε and e2ε , so a
bounded observable error is at most ∥O∥∞ min(2, e2ε − 1). Per-step learned errors would add; a
single initial error does not grow under subsequent exact elimination. This scalar proof is not an
assertion that the matrix exponential is operator-monotone.

28.2.1

The implementation checks full enumeration against the closed black-spin formula, Walsh reconstruction, raw partition equality, retained-spin magnetizations and two-stage versus direct elimination.
At the quantum Γ = 0, β = 1 limit, every Pauli coefficient agrees with the classical effective action,

A quantum-feature model trained on actual RG

The learner targets all 32 unnormalized logweights of the 7 → 5 spin m = 5 decimation. Its three
inputs are (J+ , J− , b), with ranges [0.15, 1.1]2 × [−0.6, 0.6]. Fixed fields are hj = 0.045 cos(1.7j) +
0.02j/5. Seed 20261103 fixes 160 input samples, a 120/40 train/test split, and 40 OOD samples
with J± ∈ [1.15, 1.5], b ∈ [−0.8, 0.8]. The three feature qubits encode the couplings by Ry (1.5J+ ) ⊗
Ry (1.5J− ) ⊗ Ry (2b)|000⟩. Two local Ry , Rz layers, with a CZ triangle after each, contain 12 nominal
optimizer angles. The final three Rz phases commute with the measured Z products and are
structurally inactive for this loss; they still change the displayed state phases. We count them
as nominal parameters, not additional effective expressive directions. Seven nonempty Pauli-Z
products feed an affine 8 × 32 readout. There are 268 parameters. Feature registers are not the
seven physical spin sites or five quiver vertices.
The training objective is full-action MSE, including the constant; KL alone would ignore that
constant. A training-only ridge fit initializes the head, then 120 Adam updates optimize both
circuit and head. Actual PennyLane analytic expectation calls implement the feature circuit.
Parameter-shift derivatives of each feature are combined with the classical head gradient; full-loss
finite differences agree to 6.46 × 10−11 . All data, parameters, intermediate checkpoints and complete
loss curves are preserved.
The held-out full-action MSE is approximately 0.00985, mean DKL (pexact ∥plearned ) = 0.00300,
mean TV 0.0290, and mean absolute log-partition error 0.0610. A classical quadratic-feature baseline
with the same 256 readout parameters obtains 0.00613,0.00240,0.0233 and 0.0138, respectively.
OOD action MSE increases to 0.267 for the quantum hybrid, versus 0.0555 for the baseline. The
quantum-feature constant MAE is 0.0444 and higher-body coefficient RMSE is 0.00900, versus
0.0124 and 0.0130 for the classical baseline; different metrics favor different aspects. The aggregate
results show no quantum advantage. They expose genuine full-action approximation errors rather
than substituting state-compression success for RG.
Each saved example computes its maximum error over all 32 coarse states; the proposition
then applies to its subsequent exact marginalizations. These per-example finite bounds are not
uniform over the input parameter box. The synchronized architecture inspector shows literal gate
outputs, Pauli features, head, logweights, generated interactions and partition value, linked to
the exact genus-two dessin/order context. No full order representation, continuum universality,
material-specific accuracy or arithmetic zero-location bridge is inferred.

28.2.3

Quantum TokenGT and the geometric error-correction boundary

A further executed node/edge-token hybrid uses the same exact RG teacher, with frozen three-qubit
features, trained Q/K/V and a full-action head. Softmax, componentwise max-plus and hybrid
variants are compared with a matched classical feature model; the latter has lower held-out action
error. An explicit intervention-code/change variational MI objective learns concept directions; a
61-candidate latent forest and a held-out analogy transfer are scored on exact physical observables.
Rips H1 is actually reduced over F2 , and a continuous Gaussian GFlowNet trains a trajectorybalance objective with specified root/Lebesgue measures. Empirical residuals do not prove exact
terminal sampling, semantic reasoning or categorical equivalence.
The physical bit-flip repetition channel is separately executed and has pL = 3p2 − 2p3 under
ideal recovery. A genuine cellular CSS calculation on the genus-two one-face dessin gives [[10, 4, 1]]:
vertex-incidence rank 6, zero face boundary, X-distance 1 and Z-distance 4. Thus this cellulation
provides no useful protection merely from its genus. These finite layers, full formulas, proofs,
parameters and scope are developed in the research book and interactive laboratories; the physical
117

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

models are not faithful representations of the infinite completed order and supply no GRH bridge.

28.2.4

An actual finite gentle cut for the categorical interface

On the genuine m = 5 dessin quiver write ai , bi : i → i − 1 with indices modulo 5; cross-branch
consecutive pairs are forbidden. Remove both arrows with source 0. The surviving algebra B has
vertices 0 through 4, arrows ai , bi for i = 1, . . . , 4, and relations ai bi−1 = bi ai−1 = 0 for i = 2, . . . , 4.
It is an explicit finite gentle quotient, not the completed order itself.
Every surviving positive path stays on one branch and has length at most 4. For length
ℓ = 1, . . . , 4 there are 2(5 − ℓ) paths, while length 0 contributes five idempotents. Therefore
dimk B = 5 + 2(4 + 3 + 2 + 1) = 25. The forbidden-chain resolution already proved for monomial
gentle algebras has two alternating chains of length n from vertex i to i − n when 1 ≤ n ≤ i,
and none for n > i. Applying Hom to simples kills its arrow differentials. Hence pdB Si = i and
Ext4B (S4 , S0 ) ∼
= k 2 . The split finite-dimensional simple-module criterion gives gldim B = 4.
Every full branch cycle crosses a removed arrow, so the common completed order parameter u
maps to zero. Thus B is a finite u-torsion quotient, not a DVR-free surface order. The difference is
visible in derived Hom: S0 is projective over B, whereas in the original completed order the two
forbidden arms of length 5 return to vertex 0 and give Ext5Λ (S0 , S0 ) ∼
= k 2 . The exact restriction along
Λ ↠ B is consequently not derived fully faithful. This supplies a concrete cut on which to investigate
graded gentle/Fukaya models, while proving why its equivalence cannot simply be transported to
the original completed order. Boundary/stops/line-field data and an actual module-valued neural
decoder would still have to be supplied. Exact path and endpoint enumeration accompanies the
prose proof; this cut/category comparison is not claimed wholly Lean formalized.

Higher-genus dessins and reproducible
finite carriers
The companion separates three objects: an exact combinatorial dessin, a chosen visualization of
its surface, and a finite operator model. Agreement of their dimensions is not an equivalence of
categories. We give constructions for which these distinctions can be checked directly.

29.1

A hyperelliptic family beyond the torus

For odd m ≥ 3, let Cm be the smooth compactification of
y 2 = xm − 1,

β = xm : Cm −→ P1 .

The affine curve is smooth: simultaneous vanishing of 2y and mxm−1 would force x = y = 0, which
does not lie on the curve in characteristic zero. Its double cover of the x-line branches over the m
roots of unity and infinity. Riemann–Hurwitz gives g = (m − 1)/2.
Proposition 29.1 (Clean passports and infinite orders). The degree-2m Belyi map β has passport
(m, m),

(2, . . . , 2) (m entries),

(2m).

Its contracted dessin has two vertices, m edges and one face. With darts 0, . . . , 2m − 1, one may
take
σ = (0 1 · · · m − 1)(m m + 1 · · · 2m − 1),

α=

m−1
Y

(j j + m).

j=0

The associated completed order has k[[u]]-rank 2m2 , and the polynomial locally gentle algebra is
infinite dimensional.
Proof. Over zero, x is a local coordinate at the two points y = ±i, and xm has index m. At a root ξ
of unity, y is a local coordinate and β − 1 = y 2 , giving index two. At the unique point at infinity, x
has pole order two, so β has pole order 2m. Away from these points, differentiating xm introduces
no further branching. This proves the passport and the Belyi property. The two cycles describe
the two lifts of the radial segments in the x-plane. The involution exchanges their lifts at the clean
branch points. For odd m, ϕ = ασ −1 is a single 2m-cycle: its square advances two steps in each
m-cycle, and two is invertible modulo m. Hence 2 − 2g = 2 − m + 1. Each permitted m-cycle
repeats without meeting a forbidden pair. This proves infinite dimension; the earlier split-order
theorem gives rank m2 + m2 .

The number of vertices of the algebra is m, while its coefficient-order rank is 2m2 ; they should not
be interchanged in a quantum register count.

29.2

Larger genera and infinite hyperbolic windows

The same exact family gives genus five (m = 11, rank242), genus nine (m = 19, rank722) and genus
sixteen (m = 33, rank2178). These ranks refer to the infinite completed orders over k[[u]], not finite
vector-space dimensions. The browser includes these examples and a four-dimensional rotation
control: write (x, y) ∈ C2 in real coordinates (x1 , x2 , y1 , y2 ), rotate the (x1 , y1 ) plane, then project
to three coordinates. The omitted coordinate and projected intersections are explicitly identified.
This is a view of the affine double-cover sheets, not a claimed injective embedding of a compact
higher-genus surface.
A separate example is the infinite regular hyperbolic {7, 3} tiling. Its heptagons have angles
2π/3, so the defect formula gives area 5π − 7(2π/3) = π/3 at curvature −1. For a regular {p, q}
tiling, its central polygon’s Poincaré circumradius is
s

r=

To derive this, bisect the polygon into right triangles with acute angles π/p and π/q. The hyperbolic
right-triangle identity is cosh R = cot(π/p) cot(π/q), while the disk radius is r = tanh(R/2).
Substituting and applying elementary sum-to-product identities gives the formula. The condition
1/p + 1/q < 1/2 ensures positive real radius.

If a side is a diameter, the circle formula is replaced by reflection in the line through the origin; the

It never calls a truncated window the whole infinite lattice. An optional Lorentz-hyperboloid view

lattice patch supplies none of them.

29.3

What a three-dimensional view can claim

The genus-one examples use a standard torus parameterization
((R + r cos v) cos u, (R + r cos v) sin u, r sin v),

R > r > 0.

The theta paths lift from (0, 0) to (1/3, 1/3) with translation vectors (0, 0), (−1, 0), (0, −1) in the
universal covering plane. Their interiors are disjoint on the quotient, and their complement is a disk;
the induced rotations at the two black vertices give the one-face theta dessin. The elliptic square
uses the two fundamental loops. These are topological realizations; the induced Euclidean torus
metric is not asserted to be the constant-curvature or uniformizing metric of the algebraic curve.
For m ≥ 5, the companion instead plots a three-real-coordinate projection of the two sheets
of y 2 = xm − 1. Branch cuts, changes of sheet and the compact point at infinity remain part of
the mathematical construction. Projected sheets can meet. No injective embedding or numerical
uniformization follows from a screen image. The Sage source gives exact passports, quivers, forbidden
pairs and determinant calculations. The installed sage launcher on this machine points to a missing
application, so those Sage scripts are supplied but have not been executed in Sage in this milestone.
Exact stdlib Python checks independently verify the displayed permutations, genus and ramification
counts.

29.4

Finite clocks and cross-backend agreement

For d ≥ 2, let U ej = ζ j ej , V ej = ej+1 , with ζ = e2πi/d . Then
U V = ζV U,

U d = V d = I.

Applying the two sides to ej proves the first identity, including the wraparound because ζ d = 1.
The discrete Fourier matrix prepares the uniform superposition. The executable comparison applies
F , then U , then V , on a four-level carrier; its relative phases are nonconstant. Cirq, Qiskit and
PennyLane statevectors are compared to the reference vector. Strawberry Fields realizes the same
U (4) matrices on the single-photon subspace of four optical modes and compares the entire density
block, including its off-diagonal phases. Preparing a Fock state need not leave the simulator’s
internal representation marked pure, so reading only a ket would be incorrect.
122

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

29

The photonic cutoff two is exact for this passive, single-photon computation: photon number
is preserved and no mode ever contains more than one photon. This justification does not extend
to arbitrary squeezing or multiphoton simulations. Separate NVIDIA cuQuantum and CUDA-Q
adapters and pinned upstream forks are provided. GPU execution is not performed on this Mac;
source availability is distinct from a successful accelerator run.

These preserved experiments have distinct objectives. Local Ising regressors approximate selected

targets, held-out samples, recorded seeds, classical optimizer and feature readouts are retained
without calling these different objectives a complete RG flow.

Its Kraus maps are Kb = (I ⊗ ⟨b|)Qθ , so b Kb∗ Kb = I. Thus it is completely positive and trace
preserving. For ρ ≥ 0 and any v, each summand v ∗ Kb ρKb∗ v is nonnegative; cyclicity of the finite
trace proves trace preservation. The Lean interface verifies the finite-matrix positivity and trace
statements with explicit completeness hypotheses. It does not formalize the optimizer or claim
convergence of an infinite renormalization flow.
The compression loss is the weight discarded by the chosen vacuum projection. Its successful
decrease measures improved concentration for this finite input family. It does not imply exact
recovery of arbitrary four-qubit states, an isometry from dimension sixteen to four, or a critical
fixed-point construction. The held-out loss remains appreciable, and is reported without calling the
model lossless.

30.4

A larger genus-four nine-qubit example

The m = 9 hyperelliptic dessin has genus four and completed-order rank162. Its nine edge labels
index a permitted quiver cycle. A separate finite transverse-field Ising model is placed on this
nine-cycle, with Hilbert-space dimension512. This uses the dessin’s combinatorics, without claiming
a faithful representation of its infinite order.
The larger experiment uses twelve ground states over fields h ∈ [0.2, 1.9], with nine training
and three held-out states. These are numerical lowest-eigenstate approximations. The residual
alone is not a rigorous lowest-energy certificate. A sparse Hermitian eigensolver has maximum
recorded eigenpair residual 1.32 × 10−13 . The circuit has two layers of local Ry , Rz gates and
nearest-neighbor CZ around the cycle, with thirty-six parameters. Four CNOTs connect retained to
discarded qubits, and tracing out four leaves a five-qubit output. Actual PennyLane gate simulation
and parameter-shift gradients train for fifty updates. Final training and held-out discard losses
are approximately 0.548 and 0.409. These substantial residuals are reported: this architecture is
not an accurate lossless coarse graining. It is a reproducible larger testbed, with saved parameters,
held-out examples and numerical ground-state residuals. The local page permits comparison with
the smaller four-qubit model, while retaining their different data families and capacities.

30.7

Reproduction and limits of the evidence

The scripts in examples/quantum specify each circuit, data generator, random seed and optimizer.
The approved isolated environment is recorded in requirements-cpu.lock; existing environments
and all pinned Lean toolchains remain unchanged. NumPy reference and actual PennyLane runs
are saved separately. Cross-framework tests exercise a nontrivial Fourier/clock/shift state, not just
127

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

30

a vacuum on which every diagonal clock is invisible. The Strawberry Fields run uses its Gaussian
backend for training and its Fock backend for the separate one-photon comparison.
The companion’s checkpoint slider selects a saved loss checkpoint. Its held-out examples use
final weights; no intermediate predictions are implied. There are no hardware shots, cloud jobs
or accelerator benchmarks. The training/test splits are deterministic interpolation tests on small
synthetic families, not out-of-distribution or broad physical-validity tests. A model can help rank
a proposed construction; it cannot discharge arithmetic holomorphy, determinant convergence,
positivity or zero-location obligations merely by achieving low loss.

128

Chapter 31

Reading an architecture from its
tensors
The trained models of Chapter 29 and the ToricGT references have a common computational interface:
a finite directed acyclic graph whose vertices contain actual tensors and whose edges record the
inputs to each operation. The local inspector at http://127.0.0.1:8765/architecture.html
makes this graph navigable. Its scene is a computation graph, not a hardware layout or a physical
embedding of the infinite surface order. The source code and numerical records are part of the
project.

31.1

Three distinct kinds of evidence

The twenty-two saved cases cover six trained CPU model families and six fixed-weight reference
variants. The QCNN4 and QCNN9 weights, shared two-qubit convolution, quantum-feature attention,
Gaussian photonic pooling and finite Harper hybrid are replayed without further optimizer steps.
The classical TokenGT-style, tropical, block-ring, attention-hybrid and two expert-routing variants
use explicit seeded weights. They are executable miniatures, not upstream trained language-model
checkpoints. The adjustable forward clock is a separately computed finite carrier. GPU and
hardware execution remain unavailable, and optional upstream heads remain proposals unless their
own numerical evidence is supplied.
The record contains the source/checkpoint hashes, seed, conventions, formulas, shapes, axes,
parameter values and checked errors. Each saved record is immutable and content addressed; its
SHA-256 is verified before the interface accepts it. The inspector never changes the trained weights.
Selecting a forward-operation step is not selecting a training epoch.

31.2

Shapes explain the operation

Two different attention mechanisms illustrate why the axes matter. The trained quantum-feature
model produces three feature vectors,
F ∈ R3×3 ,

ℓ = F q ∈ R3 ,

α = softmax(ℓ),

c = α T F ∈ R3 .

Softmax here runs over three patches. The classical reference has seven token positions, including
one padded node, model width six and two heads. After projection and splitting, its Q, K, V have
shape [1, 2, 7, 3]. These heads are different from the three observables of the quantum circuit. Its
Q, K also undergo the source’s maximum shift and Euclidean normalization before dot scores.

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

The shape labels are literal exported dimensions. The two columns are different finite models; no
equivalence of their learned functions is asserted.
For tropical aggregation, a scalar output is
Cbhic = max Sbhij + Vbhjc ,


j legal

S = QK T / dhead .
p

The inspector shows every maximizing key and the gap between the largest two legal values. A tie
means equality of computed floating-point values, not a symbolic equality certificate. A query with
no legal key has zero context by an explicit guard. Invalid block entries are stored as zero with a
validity mask; including these zeros in a maximum would be incorrect. Softmax weights displayed
in tropical records are comparison diagnostics rather than the tropical aggregation rule.
Soft-MoE dispatch normalizes across valid tokens for each expert-slot; combination normalizes
across expert-slots for each valid token. These are different axes. Strict-prefix slots exclude the
current and later token indices, while equal-rank attention can permit different tokens with the
same reveal rank. The two rules must not be conflated into a stronger causality assertion. Likewise
padding can acquire projection bias in a residual, so its mask must remain in later routing and
pooling.

31.3

Quantum and Gaussian inspection

The stored StatePrep input precedes the displayed circuit. The variational gates have their exact
wire order and angle metadata. Qubit zero is most significant. For QCNN9, the final CNOT targets
start at wire five; the retained density matrix has shape [32, 32], not a five-qubit pure state obtained
by discarding amplitudes. The partial trace follows the unitary gates and is distinct from their
dimension-preserving action.
The photonic model uses covariance order (x0 , x1 , p0 , p1 ) and ℏ = 2. Its ellipse is a one-standarddeviation contour of a selected two-coordinate Gaussian marginal, with the marginal covariance
taken from the actual intermediate record. It is not a plot of a trained interacting field theory. The
Harper page separately displays the learned proposal and the fifteen exact rational principal-minor
certificates. Rejected proposals are labelled as repaired. The carrier uses backward V , so V U = iU V ;
the live forward-clock example instead has U V = ωV U .

31.4

Spatial calculations and guided navigation

The interaction reference is Brendan Bycroft’s LLM Visualization, reviewed at the pinned source
revision recorded in planning/reference-inspector/source-manifest.json. Its model overview
and self-attention walkthrough place the narrative beside dimensioned tensor planes, parallel
query/key/value projections, attention heads and residual paths. The source’s picking code maps a
130

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

31

selected output cell to input rows or columns and dot-product terms; its camera code interpolates
toward walkthrough targets. These specific mechanisms motivate the local redesign. Reference
hover inspection was limited by a renderer crash; source inspection is distinguished from successful
live observation.
The local implementation uses original browser code and the project’s own immutable records.
Its spatial scene follows dependency ranks and semantic lanes, rather than putting every operation in
an identical card. A tensor’s dimensions determine its grid; head planes, selected literal coordinates
and level-of-detail windows remain labelled. Overview retains the whole recorded computation,
while a focused step moves toward its neighbourhood. The narrative gives the actual operation
and evidence type. Supported cell expansions show numerical input terms and their computed
result; other operations retain graph dependencies without inventing a scalar derivation. Surface
and circuit views explain a separate geometric or physical interpretation of the finite carrier.
The rendering is a lightweight axonometric projection rather than a copy of the reference’s
WebGL engine. Neither perspective effects nor a quantum-state array make an attention model a
coherent quantum transformer. Checkpoint replay, fixed-weight reference calculations and separately
proposed extensions keep their different status throughout the interface.

31.5

A reproducible way to explore

Choose an evidence category and example, then a step. Clicking an input follows its recorded
dependency. Head/token/coordinate controls select literal array slices; paging exposes large states
without pretending that a small viewport is the entire tensor. Color is a display convention and the
selected coordinate gives its numerical real and imaginary parts. A zero amplitude has undefined
phase and is labelled accordingly. Playback visits the forward pass, and stops when cases change or
the tab becomes hidden. Orbit and zoom change the view, not the state or physical model.
The shared numerical records have independent quantum-backend and scalar reference checks.
Browser checks exercise all saved cases, coordinate selection, masks, playback, view controls,
circuits, covariance and certificate panels. These computational checks do not imply complete Lean
formalization, generalization beyond the samples, quantum advantage or the arithmetic positivedeterminant comparison. The general path/category and arithmetic interfaces remain the research
obligations described in the final chapters.

31.6

ConvexTok: certified path vocabularies

The words “token” and “graph” have two different roles here. A TokenGT position represents a
node or edge with features and incidence metadata. A tokenisation position instead represents
a boundary in a word; an edge jumps across a contiguous substring. ConvexTok, introduced
by Tempus, Whittington, Schmidt, Komm and Pimentel, Tokenisation via Convex Relaxations,
arXiv:2605.22821v1, optimises the second representation. We derive its small finite formulation,
reproduce its six-word example, and give new certified examples for finite arrow-word corpora of
our infinite surface algebras. This supplies an auditable compression layer; it does not identify the
two kinds of token or change a trained graph model.

31.6.1

A vocabulary controls many occurrences

Let D = (w1 , . . . , wN ) be a finite multiset of nonempty words over a mandatory alphabet Σ. For
each word introduce boundary vertices 0, . . . , |wn |. Include an edge (i, j) for each allowed contiguous
131

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

31

token wn [i : j]. All single-symbol edges are free in the vocabulary budget, but cost one each in the
segmentation objective. Longer candidates form a finite set C. Equal strings have the same colour
even when they occur in different words or positions. Candidates never cross word boundaries.
Write xe for occurrence flow and ct for selection of token type t. Use incidence entries −1 at an
edge’s source and +1 at its target. For each word let d be −1 at its initial boundary and +1 at its
final boundary. Separate unit-flow equations are equivalent to joining adjacent word terminals as
in the paper: each word boundary must carry one unit, and there are no edges across words. The
finite programme is
LK = min

Requiring xe , ct ∈ {0, 1} gives the integer problem. On the acyclic position graph its flow is one
path per word, hence one segmentation. Conversely any segmentation with at most K extra token
types gives such an integer point. Consequently the LP optimum is a lower bound for the integer
optimum IK . The constraint is per occurrence: selecting ab once permits both occurrences in abab,
P
whose cost is two. Replacing these constraints by e: t(e)=t xe ≤ ct would incorrectly prohibit that
reuse.
For a fixed discrete vocabulary V ⊇ Σ, the exact segmenter is the backward recurrence
D(n) = 0,

min

t∈V : w[i:i+|t|]=t

D(i + |t|).

It returns a shortest path and a decoding by concatenation. Longest-first greedy need not do so: for
w = abcd and extra vocabulary {ab, bcd} it returns ab|c|d, whereas the optimum is a|bcd. This
segmentation issue is separate from BPE’s greedy construction of the vocabulary itself.

31.6.2

An exact lower bound needs a feasible certificate

A floating solver’s proposed objective alone is insufficient for an exact lower-bound claim. Our
original CPU checker writes the LP in the form
min q T v,

Av = b,

Gv ≤ h,

v ≥ 0,

where v = (x, c) and G includes every upper-bound row vi ≤ 1. It uses SciPy/HiGHS from the
already approved environment to propose primal and dual solutions, reconstructs rational entries,
and then checks every condition with exact rational arithmetic:
Av = b,

Gv ≤ h,

v ≥ 0,

z ≤ 0,

AT y + GT z ≤ q,

q T v = bT y + hT z.

These are executable rational witnesses, not a claim that floating-point optimisation itself is a proof
assistant.
Proposition 31.1 (Finite rational certificate). Every feasible primal v ′ obeys q T v ′ ≥ bT y + hT z for
any dual pair satisfying the displayed inequalities. Equality with one feasible v certifies the exact LP
optimum.

132

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

31

Proof. Since v ′ ≥ 0, the componentwise dual inequality gives
q T v ′ ≥ y T Av ′ + z T Gv ′ ≥ y T b + z T h.
The second inequality uses z ≤ 0 and Gv ′ ≤ h. Equality therefore matches a lower and an upper
bound.
Independently, our checker exhausts every size-K subset of C and runs the shortest-path
segmenter on each. This covers the at-most-K problem, because adding candidate tokens cannot
increase a shortest-path cost. For K > |C|, all candidates suffice. Every stored witness is checked;
deliberately perturbed primal and dual witnesses are rejected.

31.6.3

Three rounding rules and worked numerical gaps

The paper’s Det rule chooses the K largest ct ; Bias chooses the K largest ct /|t|; Int retains only
values essentially equal to one. Our certificates are rational, so Int uses exact equality to one and
Det/Bias use lexicographic tie-breaking. After choosing tokens we recompute a discrete shortest
path; a fractional flow is not itself a tokenisation. An integral colour in one LP solution need not be
present in every optimum. For example, with word abc and candidates ab, bc, either single-token
choice is optimal.
The paper’s example is
D0 = (abc, abd, abe, bc, bd, be),

K = 3.

The mandatory-symbol count is fifteen. BPE first creates ab, saving three positions, then two merges
save only one each: its cost is ten. Choosing bc, bd, be instead gives nine. Our exact primal/dual
pair has value nine, and all 73 = 35 candidate vocabularies confirm L3 = I3 = 9. All three rounding
rules give nine on the saved LP solution.
Our first new example is
D1 = (cbcbc, cbca, cbabac, caaab),

K = 2.

One optimal fractional vocabulary has
ccbc = 21 ,

ccaaab = 21 ,

ccbabac = 1,

with all other values zero. Its per-word costs are 3, 3, 1, 3, totalling ten; the rational dual certifies
optimality. The integer optimum is eleven, witnessed by vocabulary {caaab, cbabac} with costs
5, 4, 1, 1 and confirmed by all 30
2 = 435 vocabularies. Det and Bias also yield eleven, while Int
yields fifteen. The LP is therefore genuinely fractional even though its objective is an integer.
A second original corpus,
(aaaab, baaccb, aac, babaab, cbbbca),

K = 2,

has L2 = 31/2 < I2 = 16. Its nonzero colour weights are caa = cbabaab

approximation ratio for rounding.

Application to permitted paths on actual higher-genus dessins

Set m = 2g + 1 for g = 2, 4, and retain the existing clean dessin of y 2 = xm − 1, β = xm . On
H = {0, . . . , 2m − 1} put
σ = (0 · · · m − 1)(m · · · 2m − 1),

τ = σ −1 ,

α(h) = h + m (mod 2m),

There are two vertex cycles, m edges and one face, so 2 − 2g = 2 − m + 1. Its polynomial algebra is
the infinite locally gentle algebra
Ag = kQ/(ah aϕh : h ∈ H),

ah : [h] −→ [τ h].

Here products are written in travel order: ab traverses a first and then b. A left-operator matrix
convention reverses these products. Define the nonzero permitted word P (h, r) = ah aτ h · · · aτ r−1 h .
Our finite multiset is
P (0, m + 2), P (0, m + 2), P (m − 1, 4), P (m, m + 2), P (2m − 1, 4) .

Each arrow label is one alphabet symbol, even when its printed label has several characters. The

as an extra candidate. The LP bound consequently concerns this explicitly restricted candidate set,

The exact witnesses attain these values, and Det/Bias/Int all attain them on the saved LP solutions.
A token names a finite permitted subpath; concatenation recovers every original arrow, hence its
quiver endpoints and original path length. This is finite corpus compression, not a finite-dimensional
quotient of Ag . Arbitrary token concatenations may fail composability and need their own endpoint
checks. Neither an algebra homomorphism on a freely generated token algebra nor a representation
of arbitrary completed formal sums is asserted. The completed order still has rank 2m2 over k[[u]];
its rank, coefficients, multiplication, relations and modules are not reduced by counting tokens.
The companion laboratory at http://127.0.0.1:8765/convextok.html exposes the position
DAG, literal fractional flows, vocabularies and discrete segmentations. Its five corpora and sixteen
budget cases have sixteen exact primal/dual certificates and 2,312 exhausted integer vocabularies
in total. The browser independently recomputes segmentation costs from local arrays; it does not
solve a new LP when a user changes a vocabulary. The primary paper’s NLP training and large
GPU experiments are not reproduced. Compression of a finite path corpus proves no module or
Fukaya equivalence, arithmetic determinant comparison, or GRH statement.

Chapter 32

Quantum TokenGT, concept
navigation and error correction
This chapter connects executed finite methods through the same physical RG teacher. The completed
order, ribbon dessin, physical Hamiltonian, token sequence and auxiliary quantum circuit retain
their different dimensions. The surface is not reconstructed from a neural embedding, and the
network is not silently declared a module over its completed order.

32.1

Node and edge tokens with a quantum feature map

The genus-two clean dessin of Chapter 28 has five quiver vertices and DVR-rank 50. Its physical
K2,5 incidence model has seven spin nodes and ten incidence edges. Our TokenGT-style input has
these 17 valid node/edge tokens and one masked padding position. Each token carries its type
and endpoint metadata through an explicit three-angle encoding. The normalized auxiliary state
uses Ry data gates, fixed Rz rotations, CNOT 01 , CNOT 12 and fixed Rx rotations. The measured
features are
(⟨Z0 ⟩, ⟨Z1 ⟩, ⟨Z2 ⟩, ⟨Z0 Z1 ⟩, ⟨Z1 Z2 ⟩, ⟨Z0 Z2 ⟩).
These are three feature qubits per token, not the seven physical spin sites or a faithful representation
of the infinite order. The quantum feature map is frozen; the Q/K/V matrices and nonlinear readout
are trained. A separate literal replay of the pinned elliptic-theta fixture is retained with its own
input hash and category boundary. The physical RG training input is the genus-two incidence
graph, not that different theta fixture.
√

Padding is masked in both softmax and max-plus operations and in pooling. The tropical output is
a componentwise max-plus aggregation, with actual maximizing-key witnesses. It is not merely an
argmax-key copy. A tanh context/residual pool and classical head predict all 32 coarse logweights,
including the constant. These are quantum–classical transformers on measured features, not a
coherent softmax unitary. TokenGT [83] motivates the node/edge organization; no general upstream
expressivity or benchmark theorem is asserted for this compressed feature map. Numerical token-row
permutation transport is checked, separately from arbitrary graph-relabel invariance and information
lost by the encoding.

and the same hybrid head. The teacher is exactly RV from the seven-spin physical model, so
partition, constant and retained-observable errors have physical meanings. The controlled input has
three tied parameters, not 31 freely varied output couplings.

The classical baseline has lower held-out full-action error. Quantum OOD RMSE ranges from.555

contexts and readout, and separately exposes the trained concept critic and continuous-GFlowNet
MLP/density computations, with synchronized physical and algebraic support labels.

32.3

A defined mutual-information objective for concept directions

Let z ∈ R6 be a pooled embedding. We learn three normalized directions B1 , B2 , B3 and a critic;
their span need not be the whole embedding space. Choose C uniformly among the six direction/sign
codes and define
∆ = tanh(0.3z + 0.7 sign(C)Bdir(C) ) − tanh(0.3z).
A fixed vector is not a random variable: the random objects here are the intervention code and its
induced embedding change. The GraphCG-inspired [84] objective is cross entropy for predicting C
from ∆, plus 0.08∥BB T − I∥2F . For any normalized critic q,
I(C; ∆) ≥ log 6 − E[− log q(C | ∆)].
Indeed the conditional cross entropy is conditional entropy plus an average nonnegative KL divergence.
The held-out empirical functional is approximately 1.78844 nats. Its sampling estimate is not a
certified lower confidence bound on an unknown population MI, nor proof of semantic identification:
deliberately injected codes can already be distinguishable. The orthogonality penalty concerns
the learned directions; it does not replace this probability model by ill-defined “MI between basis
vectors.” A training-only ridge decoder maps steered embeddings back to physical couplings, whose
effects are evaluated with the exact RG teacher.

32.4

Latent forests, analogy and persistent homology

The latent forest branches along both signs of each learned direction, keeps a width-three beam
and searches depthfour. All 61 candidates are scored by exact coarse log Z and the five retained
magnetizations. Its best saved reward changes from −1.60383 to −0.70865; the residual is substantial.
This is finite branching optimization in embedding space, without a claim of human or general
language-model reasoning.
136

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

32

A distinct analogy test estimates a mean displacement from transformed training pairs for
(J+ , J− , b) 7→ (J+ + 0.07, J− − 0.04, b + 0.06), clipped to the training domain, and transfers it to
40 held-out embeddings. Decoded couplings go back through the exact physical teacher. Log Z
RMSE is.09578 versus.15417 without transfer; magnetization RMSE is.02332 versus.04012. These
are measured transformation-transfer results, not unrestricted “emergent analogical reasoning.”
For an explicit finite point cloud with chosen metric, the Rips filtration contains each simplex at
the maximum distance between its vertices. The implementation reduces the full boundary matrices
of vertices, edges and triangles over F2 , with faces ordered before cofaces at ties. A zero reduced edge
column creates a one-cycle; a later triangle pivot kills its class. Triangles suffice to compute H1 . The
12-point learned embedding cloud has one positive-length interval approximately [2.50049, 2.97133).
The target and transferred physical-observable clouds, scaled as (log Z/3, m0 /.25, . . . , m4 /.25), both
have empty positive-length H1 barcodes and pair-distance RMSE.29095. Empty matching barcodes
alone are not faithful analogy. The 67-radius independent checks test this finite calculation, not a
physical topological-order invariant or a mirror theorem.

A genuine error-correction layer and a surface-code obstruction

For a logical qubit, use the repetition isometry |0⟩ 7→ |000⟩, |1⟩ 7→ |111⟩. Measure Z0 Z1 and Z1 Z2 ,
and apply the X correction specified by the two syndrome bits. Under independent X noise of
probability p on each physical qubit and ideal encoding/recovery, the weight-zero/one errors recover
the state. Weight-two/three errors leave a logical X. Hence
pL = 3p2 (1 − p) + p3 = 3p2 − 2p3 ,

NL (ρ) = (1 − pL )ρ + pL XρX.

The same channel identity holds with an entangled reference. The executed four-qubit Bell-reference
density calculation compares actual BitFlip noise, syndrome projection and corrections with an
independent eight-pattern sum. At p = .1 its entanglement fidelity is.972, versus.9 without coding.
It corrects one X error under the stated ideal assumptions, with no phase protection or universal
fault-tolerance claim.
Applied independently to each auxiliary token-feature qubit, this is an explicit physical featurememory layer. For a measured Z product of weight r, the unencoded X channel multiplies its
expectation by (1 − 2p)r ; the recovered channel replaces p by pL . This follows by conjugating Z by
X and tensoring the single-qubit channel identity, even for entangled feature states. It distinguishes
actual syndrome recovery from embedding regularization or persistence diagnostics. The resulting
attention/readout is evaluated on all 40 held-out physical RG actions. At p = .1, hybrid fullaction RMSE is.89766 without coding,.31589 after one ideal recovery level and.10608 after two,
compared with clean.10125. Tropical RMSE changes from.32676 to.11810 and.06733, compared
with clean.06530. Seventy-five explicit logical-X density-mixture comparisons on actual entangled
token states agree with the attenuation rule within 4.45 × 10−16 . The separately checksummed
evaluation preserves every trained checkpoint. The three auxiliary feature qubits require 3,9 and 27
total physical qubits at zero, one and two recovery levels. The reported evaluations use the proved
logical-channel identity, not a full noisy 9/27-qubit gate simulation; encoding and recovery gates are
ideal throughout. Concatenating ideal blocks gives the noise recursion p 7→ 3p2 − 2p3 , improving
p < 1/2; it is not an RG theorem about the physical Hamiltonian.
Proposition 32.1 (The one-face dessin is not a useful quantum memory). The CSS construction
with qubits on the 10 clean edges of the genus-two K2,5 dessin, vertex X checks and its single face Z
check has parameters [[10, 4, 1]], with X-distance 1 and Z-distance 4.
Proof. The connected graph incidence matrix over F2 has rank 7 − 1 = 6. Every edge occurs
twice in its single face boundary, so the face boundary is zero. The chain relation holds. For
Q
independent commuting Pauli checks that do not generate −I, the projector is j (I + Sj )/2. Only
its identity term has nonzero trace, so its rank is 2n−rX −rZ . Thus the CSS logical dimension is
10 − 6 − 0 = 4 = 2g. All single-edge X operators commute with the zero face check. No singleton
edge set is a cut/coboundary, since each edge lies on a four-cycle; thus it is not an X stabilizer, and
the X-distance is 1. A Z logical operator is a nonzero cycle because the face-boundary subspace is
zero. The simple bipartite graph has girth 4, proving Z-distance 4. Exact enumeration independently
checks the ranks, commuting checks and shortest logical witnesses.
Genus and logical dimension therefore do not imply noise protection. Useful surface-code families
require controlled cellulations, local checks, growing primal and dual systoles, a stated noise model
and verified decoders.
