Tropical, categorical and physical applications

For real numbers aj and τ > 0,
max aj ≤ τ log
j

X

eaj /τ ≤ max aj + τ log N.
j

j

Factor out the maximum; the remaining sum lies between 1 and N . This is a precise zero-temperature
max-plus limit. Replacing the finite-temperature RG sum by a maximum discards entropy, including
its normalization. An attention semiring alone supplies neither the physical energy calibration nor
the valuations and curve/disk counts needed for tropical geometry and mirror symmetry [86].
For a categorical connection, Section 28.2.4 gives an actual 25-dimensional finite gentle cut
with global dimension 4, kills u, and proves that restriction to the original completed order is not
derived fully faithful. It is a constructive starting point, with a proved obstruction to transporting
comparisons indiscriminately. Choose its grading and verify the relevant homological smoothness
before invoking a geometric model. The partially wrapped Fukaya comparison for smooth graded
gentle algebras requires boundary, stops and a line field [66]; it is not a blanket statement for
the complete order on the original closed surface. One could then compute specified Hom/Ext or
arc-intersection data and use them in a categorical learning objective. No such equivalence or learned
categorical functor is produced by the three-qubit feature circuit. Physical RG marginalization is
not automatically a derived equivalence either.
For materials models, one must supply a physical Hamiltonian/data family, symmetries, locality
constraints and validated observables. The executed finite spin problem is a reproducible statisticalmechanics testbed, not a materials-discovery result. An executed five-qubit generative example
P p
uses a saved learned coarse action to compute p(s) = eVb (s) /Z and prepares |ψ⟩ = s p(s)|s⟩.
Born probabilities reproduce p to 2.78 × 10−17 . This coherent pure state is not the mixed Gibbs
state. Applying the explicit local projector-Kraus dephasing channel gives diag(p) with the same
residual. From 4096 seeded quantum Born samples, the maximum five-magnetization sampling error
is.04377; the simultaneous 95% Hoeffding radius is.05086. The union bound uses 10 exp(−N ϵ2 /2)
for five observables in [−1, 1]. This bound controls shot error relative to the learned distribution,
separately from its physical teacher bias. All 32 amplitudes are computed classically, with no
efficient many-body preparation or quantum advantage claim. Sampling latent coupling controls,
predicting a full action and generating physical configurations is therefore a concrete finite hybrid
route, with its costs and errors exposed. Quantum-information uses of the physical feature-memory
code require the stated noise/recovery model. None of these numerical, categorical or physical
interfaces establishes the missing arithmetic Frobenius/determinant comparison for GRH.

32.8

Verification boundary

All optimizer histories, source hashes, parameters, held-out/OOD data, barcodes, candidate rewards
and density checks are preserved. The six-panel laboratory and four trained architecture passes
display literal arrays and gate states; the original 22 architecture records remain unchanged. Exact
finite CSS enumeration and the bit-flip channel proof are distinguished from floating-point learned
diagnostics. The finite classical RG identities are kernel checked in FiniteRG.lean; general neural
optimization, continuous GFlowNet sampling, quantum recovery and categorical interfaces are not
thereby wholly Lean formalized.

Chapter 33

Learned generative fields, width limits
and quantum geometry
This chapter connects explicit learned functions to physical RG targets, categorical path calculations
and checked quantum circuits. Each width, coefficient field and category is specified before
comparisons are made.

33.1

Learned fields, width limits and quantum geometry

Learned functions enter this program as candidate generators, physical surrogates and control laws.
A network may suggest an arithmetic metric, operator or gluing datum; a successful fit does not
establish its positivity, analytic domain or identification with a completed L-function. Those are
independent mathematical obligations. This section gives explicit interfaces and executed examples
rather than treating “quantum”, “tropical” or “infinite width” as a substitute for that comparison.

Proposition 33.2 (Tropical limit and a cancellation boundary). For real s1 , . . . , sn and τ > 0,

An attention maximum therefore has a controlled log-sum-exp approximation. It does not by itself

Proof. After subtracting M = max sj , the exponential sum lies between one and n. For the second
statement, both 1 + T and −1 + T have valuation zero in Q((T )), whereas their sum 2T has valuation
one. In characteristic two that sum is zero. Thus replacing every addition by its tropical minimum
without tracking leading coefficients loses information.
Kontsevich–Soibelman constructions require valued analytic and integral-affine gluing data;
appropriate gentle/Fukaya comparisons require specified categories, gradings, stops and smoothness
hypotheses [92, 91, 93]. Learned coefficients can propose such data, but the compatibility equations
and arithmetic comparison still need proofs.
Executed physical examples. On the actual clean genus-two dessin, put seven quantum spins
on its K2,5 incidence graph and compute a normalized Gibbs state. Exact white-spin partial trace
gives a two-spin quantum RG marginal. Twelve RTX4090 runs train four-qubit-feature diffusion
and flow-matching models for its smoothed Z-measurement law: two objectives, entangled/product
circuit controls, and three seeds. They have 5,810 nominal parameters and 2,000 steps each. A
separate four-qubit variational purification flow learns the full known density interpolation, with
48 controls. Its minimum fidelity on a 33-point grid is 0.999903; this is not a uniform theorem or
universal noise inverse. The matched controls show no consistent entanglement advantage.
Actual cuStateVec/cuTensorNet execution also covers 7-, 11- and 23-qubit finite circuits on
genus-two, genus-four and genus-ten dessin incidence graphs. The 23-qubit case has 8,388,608
amplitudes and 447 gates. These larger circuits are untrained; the small cases have independent
NumPy comparisons, while the largest has normalization and inverse-circuit checks. The exact
environment and all failed/preliminary records are preserved.
Geometric and hardware interfaces. For the contracted m-edge dessin, the graph fundamental
group is Fm−1 , and its universal graph cover is the m-regular tree. The theta case m = 3 has a
three-regular cover, distinct from the four-regular Cayley graph of F2 . Neither is the coefficient field
F2 or the universal cover of the filled compact surface. Explicit path modules over the infinite genustwo algebra give Hom, Ext and Tor computations; Frobenius pants tensors give exact TQFT gluing,
with a heralded physical implementation. CHSH gives a finite game benchmark, distinct from the
unrestricted correlation separation in MIP*=RE [99]. Three executable tropical hardware references
and a source-pinned chip-design survey complement the circuits. They are arithmetic/control
designs, not synthesized chips, validated CFT/string dualities or a proof of GRH.

33.2.4

Architecture, matched controls and actual GPU results

The neural input is [x0 , x1 , t, sin 2πt, cos 2πt, sin 4πt, cos 4πt]. An affine encoder maps its seven
channels to four angles through π tanh. A two-layer four-qubit circuit uses angle-reuploading
RY , RZ gates and, for the entangled architecture, a CNOT ring. Fifteen commuting Z-string
expectations are concatenated with the seven input channels. A 22 → 64 → 64 → 2 SiLU head
predicts noise or velocity. The product control uses the same nominal parameters and removes the
entanglers. Final diagonal phase rotations are invisible to the all-Z readout; equality of nominal
parameter counts does not establish equality of effective Fisher capacity.
Every run has 5,810 nominal parameters, batch size 512 and 2,000 Adam steps. There are 16,384
training draws and 4,096 disjoint held-out draws, with seeds 20261104, 20261105 and 20261106.
The four architectures/objectives were rerun at every seed. The following figures are mean and
sample standard deviation across those three runs, not confidence intervals or asymptotic theorems.

33.2.5

A coherent learned purification flow

We separately implement quantum evolution, without identifying it with the classical samplers.
Exact depolarizing diffusion is
Dt (ρR ) = e−t ρR + (1 − e−t )I4 /4.
It preserves trace and positivity and satisfies Ds Dt = Ds+t by direct substitution. Its linear inverse
on all matrices is not positive: Dt−1 (P ) = et P − (et − 1)I4 /4 has a negative eigenvalue on the
orthogonal complement of any rank-one projection P when t > 0. Thus a learned inverse cannot be
a universal deterministic physical channel.
For a constructive state-preparation benchmark,√take the family Rt = (1 − t)I4 /4 + tρR ,
0√≤ t ≤ 1, and its canonical purification |Ψt ⟩ = vec Rt in C4 ⊗ C4 . It is normalized because
∥ Rt ∥2F = tr Rt = 1. The initial purification is two Bell pairs. We train a six-block four-qubit
144

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

33

circuit: each block conjugates local RY rotations by a fixed CNOT network and its inverse; angles
are tθ + t2 ω. All blocks are identity at the control time zero. The 48 learned controls are trained
against full known purification states, using the fidelity loss. This ideal supervision is stronger than
access to measured samples and is accounted for explicitly.
An RTX4090 run of 2,000 steps, batch 64, gives minimum fidelity 0.999903 on a 33-point grid
and maximum reduced-density Frobenius error 0.009642. The reduced state is obtained by an actual
ancilla partial trace and passes trace/positivity checks. These are grid diagnostics, not a uniform or
out-of-family guarantee. The construction is a learned coherent control path for known targets, not
a universally learned inverse to depolarization. Its full 76-gate replay and intermediate complex
states are exported to the same numerical architecture inspector as the other models.

33.2.6

Width, fields, RG and arithmetic obligations

Proposition 33.1 fixes which width is being increased: the number of independent one-qubit features,
with only their readout trained. At initialization let ar ∼ N (0, 1) be independent standard centered
readout coefficients, independent of the frozen features. Conditional finite-dimensional outputs
are then exactly centered Gaussian with covariance Km . The almost-sure kernel convergence
implies convergence of these finite-dimensional characteristic functions to the Gaussian law with
covariance K. This proves the specified finite-dimensional GP limit. It does not prove tightness
in a function-space norm, parameter-feature learning, a many-qubit limit or a continuum QFT.
For frozen Km , squared-loss gradient flow gives ṙ = −Km r, whose eigencomponents decay at their
kernel eigenvalues. A zero eigenvalue leaves a nonzero residual unchanged.
The executable width experiment fits the existing exact 32-configuration classical black-spindecimated action on genus two, including its constant. This is a second true RG teacher, distinct
from the quantum white-spin partial trace above. With 96 parameter triples for training, 32 for
testing, eight feature seeds and ridge 10−5 , widths 16 and 4,096 give mean relative kernel errors
0.2990 and 0.01726 and mean full-action test MSE 0.02005 and 0.000743. The limiting RBF readout
is also evaluated. Finite numerical convergence supports the implementation; the proposition
supplies the limit proof. Quantum NTK literature requires its own parameterization, ensemble and
lazy/large-width hypotheses [91, 90].

For complex embeddings the Hermitian Gram form, realification A + iB 7→ B
A
conjugation must be respected. An arbitrary real network on twice as many channels need not be
complex linear. In characteristic two, 1+1 = 0; an embedding into real vectors cannot be a unital field
homomorphism preserving that addition. Finite-field path algebras and cellular/CSS computations
are still meaningful, but Gaussian integration, ordered Gibbs weights and real backpropagation do
not transfer by renaming the field. An explicit discrete learner over Fp , p = 2, 3, 5, uses features
1, x, y, xy and coefficients searched in the finite field to learn f (x, y) = xy. All p2 inputs are verified;
an affine-only learner has nonzero minimum error. The trained map compiles to the permutation
unitary Uf |x, y, z⟩ = |x, y, z + f (x, y) mod p⟩ on a complex Hilbert space of dimension p3 . Retaining
x, y makes its inverse subtraction explicit. This is reversible encoding of a field-valued polynomial
learner, not a field embedding into complex amplitudes or backpropagation in Fp . Its training is
exact finite coefficient search. A tropical semiring is not a field; coefficient cancellation, valuation
signs and the underlying valued algebra must be specified.
For GRH, each architecture offers an experimentally testable role: quantum features can
parameterize candidate metrics or probe observables; graph tokens can encode actual local incidence,
masks and monodromy; tropical attention can search extremal valuations or active constraints;
flow/diffusion can propose continuous operator/control parameters; RG can organize a genuine
hierarchy of coarse actions. None of these roles proves that its Gram matrix is the arithmetic positive
metric. One must still construct the arithmetic object with correct conductor, gamma factors, zero
divisor and domain, prove compact-uniform control and zero location, and keep Dirichlet, Hecke,
Dedekind and Artin hypotheses separate. A learned candidate is accepted only after its exact
defining equations and analytic estimates are supplied.

33.3

Covers, paths, pants and quantum games

33.3.1

Which free group and which universal cover?

Write F2 = ⟨x, y⟩, F2 and Fb2 for the free group, coefficient field and profinite completion, respectively.
For a theta graph with vertices A, B and edges a, b, c, an explicit cover has vertices (g, A), (g, B),
g ∈ F2 , and edges
a : (g, A) − (g, B),

b : (g, A) − (gx, B),

c : (g, A) − (gy, B).

Left multiplication is the deck action. Contracting all a-edges produces the usual four-regular
Cayley tree of F2 ; before contraction the graph is three-regular. Equivalently, vertices are reduced
nonbacktracking edge paths from A, with an immediately reversed edge cancelled. This gives a
P
k
tree and unique path lifting in every length. Its radius-r patch has 1 + 3 r−1
k=0 2 vertices, hence
766 at radius eight. With a planar ribbon structure the abstract graph is a spine of a topological
pair of pants. The actual m = 3 dessin instead has the ribbon structure of a once-punctured torus.
Forgetting the cyclic orders identifies the abstract graphs, not these thickenings. Graph distance is
not automatically hyperbolic geodesic length.
For the contracted clean m-edge dessin there are two vertices and m parallel edges, so π1 is
Fm−1 and the analogous universal tree is m-regular. The new examples include m = 5, 9, of genus
two and four. Reinserting clean white bivalent vertices subdivides its edges. Attaching the face to
recover the compact surface introduces a relation; the closed genus-two surface has its surface group
and a disk universal cover, not one of these graph trees. Separately, P1 \ {0, 1, ∞} has fundamental
group F2 . The unbranched cover induced by a clean dessin removes all preimages of those three
values. For our degree-2m map there are m + 3 punctures; its Euler characteristic is −2m, not that
146

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

33

of the dessin’s one-skeleton. Thus the occurrences of F2 in monodromy, graph covers and arithmetic
profinite theory must not be silently identified.

For finite-supported representations of a suitably graded bound quiver, pushdown maps split into
deck-homogeneous blocks. The arrow equations preserve the degree, and monomial zero relations
are homogeneous. The resulting Hom direct sum controls all deck pieces, not just degree one. For
an explicit graph-only diagnostic two lifted edge intervals have upstairs endomorphism dimension
two, but downstairs endomorphism dimension four: the deck pieces have dimensions 2 + 1 + 1. This
graph-quiver calculation is not substituted for a dessin-algebra module example. The actual infinite
polynomial module examples below supply that part. A Hom-controlled functor needs its specified
exceptional/source/target subcategory and control statement; exactness, a deck-sum formula or
equal dimensions alone is not an equivalence or an AR universality theorem. A derived comparison
must control all Hom(X, Y [n]) and composition, together with an actual derived/enhanced functor.

33.3.2

Actual infinite-algebra path modules and unbounded resolutions

To fix conventions, let Acol be the earlier rightmost-arrow-first polynomial matrix algebra acting
on left columns. Here we use travel-order path multiplication and write Atrav = Aop
col for the
prose-identified bound-quiver algebra. Right Atrav representations therefore encode the earlier
covariant left-column representations. There are vertices i ∈ Z/5 and arrows ai , bi : i → i − 1, with
ai bi−1 = bi ai−1 = 0.
Both pure branch paths survive at every length. No arrow-truncated finite algebra is being used.
We work with ordinary polynomial modules, not with all completed lattices or all infinite strings.
Put Pi = ei Atrav and let Si be its residue simple. The augmentation kernel is ai A ⊕ bi A.
Multiplication by an arrow kills exactly the opposite-branch positive paths at its target, while each
surviving path has a distinct normal image. Its kernel is therefore the right ideal generated by its
unique forbidden successor, without cancellation. Iteration yields a projective resolution with
P0 = Pi ,

Pn = Pi−n ⊕ Pi−n

(n ≥ 1).

The two arms are indexed by alternating-branch relation words. The differential entry is the last
arrow, not the product of the zero relation word. This kernel description proves exactness in every
147

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

33

degree. Applying Hom to a simple kills every arrow entry, hence
dim Hom(Si , Sj ) = 1i=j ,

dim Extn (Si , Sj ) = 21j=i−n

(n ≥ 1).

Tensoring with the left residue simple gives the same Tor dimensions. A graded version needs its
internal degree shifts. In particular the residue simples have infinite projective dimension in this
ambient algebra.
Let B be the one-arrow string supported at vertices zero and four, with a0 = 1 and every other
arrow zero. Its presentation is
(b0 , a0 a4 )

P4 ⊕ P3 −−−−−−→ P0 −→ B −→ 0.
The two images have disjoint normal-path support and span the kernel, because B retains exactly
e0 , a0 . The next entries are a4 on the first arm and b3 on the second; continue by their unique
forbidden successors. This gives an unbounded exact resolution by the same normal-path argument.
Full arrow-commuting equations give
dim Hom(B, B) = 1,

dim Hom(S0 , B) = 0,

dim Hom(B, S0 ) = 1,

dim Hom(S4 , B) = 1.

In the Hom complex for Ext1 (B, B) both adjacent differentials vanish, so Ext1 (B, B) ≃ Q. A
concrete nonsplit self-extension has Q2 at zero and four, a0 = I2 , b0 = ( 00 10 ), all other arrows
zero. The first-coordinate submodule and quotient are B. A section at vertex zero would lift the
quotient basis to a vector with second coordinate one; b0 sends it to a nonzero first-coordinate
vector, contradicting commutation with the zero b0 action on B. Thus B is not rigid in this actual
ambient category. No almost-split universality is inferred from this nonsplitting.
For finite-dimensional N let DN = HomQ (N, Q). On each finite idempotent projective the
evaluation pairing identifies P ⊗A DN with D HomA (P, N ). Dualization is exact on these finiten
dimensional terms, so the resulting complexes give TorA
n (M, DN ) ≃ D ExtA (M, N ) for the stated
A
finitely generated resolutions. In particular Tor1 (B, DB) ≃ Q. The exact program checks six
source/target pairs through degree twelve, all five simple-target formulas and 250 normal-kernel
samples. The unbounded resolution assertions rest on the normal-form proof, not on the finite
cutoff. These new homological proofs are prose/exact calculations; full corresponding resolution
objects are not yet Lean packaged.
A geometric path may label a string after specifying the ribbon structure, relations and category.
Turning it into a geodesic needs a metric and appropriate boundary/stop data. Intersections compute
morphisms only under the hypotheses of an actual graded/Fukaya comparison. The homologically
smooth graded-gentle perfect-category theorems do not identify our entire ordinary or completed
module category with the Fukaya category by inspection [93]. In particular the residue resolutions
warn against silently imposing smoothness or finite projective dimension.

33.3.3

Pants tensors, physical dilation and sewing boundaries

For C = Q[ϵ]/ϵ2 , take ε(1) = 0, ε(ϵ) = 1. In the ordered bases (1, ϵ) and (1 ⊗ 1, 1 ⊗ ϵ, ϵ ⊗ 1, ϵ ⊗ ϵ),

The pairing is ( 01 10 ), with determinant −1. Associativity follows from ϵ2 = 0; coassociativity sends
one to the three placements of a single one among two ϵ’s and sends ϵ to ϵ⊗3 . On the four tensor
basis vectors, all three maps in
∆µ = (µ ⊗ I)(I ⊗ ∆) = (I ⊗ µ)(∆ ⊗ I)
give, respectively, 1 ⊗ ϵ + ϵ ⊗ 1, ϵ ⊗ ϵ, ϵ ⊗ ϵ, and zero. This proves the Frobenius identity. The unit
and counit identities follow directly on the same basis. These are exact oriented 2D TQFT gluing
data [95], not a presumed positive Hilbert structure.
The handle map H = µ∆ sends 1 7→ 2ϵ and ϵ 7→ 0. Closed genus-g contractions εH g η are
0, 2, 0, 0, . . .. The genus-two value really vanishes in this example. In characteristic two the torus
value also vanishes, illustrating a meaningful coefficient change rather than an embedding-space
relabelling. After extending to C with the Euclidean metric, ∆∗ ∆√ = diag(2, 1) and ∆ ̸= µ∗ ;
µ : C4 → C2 is not an isometry. The exact code constructs K = µ/ 2 and a 4 × 4 failure block
L with K ∗ K + L∗ L = I4 . Stacking them gives an isometry into C2 ⊕ C4 , extendable to a unitary
with ancillas. Its success probabilities on the four basis states are 1/2, 1/2, 1/2, 0. Postselection
implements K with state-dependent success; normalizing the selected outcome is not the original
linear map µ.
For a closed genus-g ≥ 2 surface a pants decomposition has 2g − 2 pieces and 3g − 3 sewn
curves. Conformal sewing adds moduli-dependent propagators and analytic consistency [96]; string
amplitudes need worldsheet, ghost and integration data beyond this finite tensor. A pants network
or a hyperbolic graph is likewise not an AdS/CFT duality or a Ryu–Takayanagi derivation [97].
Such interpretations remain specified research targets until their extra structure is constructed.
Arithmetic/anabelian reconstruction additionally requires arithmetic profinite fundamental-group
data and its Galois structure under the proper field/curve hypotheses. Neither a discrete F2 graph
nor a coefficient F2 module supplies that arithmetic extension [98].

33.3.4

Quantum games and the Connes-embedding distinction

For uniform input bits x, y, CHSH accepts outputs u, v when u ⊕ v = xy. With ±1 observables its
success probability is 1/2 + ⟨T ⟩/8, where
T = A0 ⊗ (B0 + B1 ) + A1 ⊗ (B0 − B1 ).
For a deterministic classical assignment one bracket vanishes and the other is ±2, giving success

MIP*=RE, in its pinned v4, gives an external unrestricted correlation separation and a negative
answer to Connes embedding [99]. We do not rerun its external formalization or claim a new proof
of it. CHSH has its optimum on two qubits and the same commuting optimum by the preceding
bound; it does not exhibit the general separation. A learned game strategy is useful for finite witness
search and checked bounds, but unrestricted computability and dimension quantifiers cannot be
settled by a finite game simulation.

33.3.5

Tropical accelerator reference designs and reproducibility

The chip-design campaign pins twenty public repositories, twenty-four focused document ranges
and thirteen primary papers. It covers AlphaChip/Circuit Training, independent macro-placement
149

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

33

benchmarks, OpenROAD-family flows, quantum layout and low-level control. The source ledger
records what was actually read, not a full audit or reproduction of every model. An exact public
identity for the phrase “bare-metal Qiskit” was not established; no repository was invented.
Three original executable references implement a masked signed max-plus tile, an ordered mixed
MAC/max-plus scheduler and a fail-closed synthetic IQ steering controller. Wide addition precedes
winner selection and final saturation; earliest-index tie handling is explicit. Empty masks remain
invalid, not an extra candidate of value zero. The controller requires a current calibration epoch,
enough samples and a valid margin/deadline before allowlisting a steering action. Checks include all
65,536 signed eight-bit additions, 262,144 masked two-lane cases, reordered completions and twelve
negative controls. These are digital reference models, not synthesized RTL, routed chips, measured
RF feedback, power/area estimates or universal hardware speedups. A tropical context block can
avoid multiplications inside that primitive while its projections, scores and other layers still require
their own arithmetic.
The Linux snapshot preserves the Mac project, source clones, histories, checkpoints and environment archives. Linux GPU execution uses the existing Torch CUDA environment and a separately
approved isolated cuQuantum prefix; macOS binaries are not treated as a Linux installation. The
conda lock, setup instructions, gate/head replay records and all failures are retained. The on-page
book contains one canonical laboratory for this chapter and links all five new checkpoint architectures into the existing tensor inspector. New physics, width and homological results have their
explicit prose/numerical/external status; selected earlier Lean components do not constitute a full
formalization of this extension or a proof of GRH.

34.1

Complementary physical datasets and checked dynamics

The learned program now has two additional, separately constructed corpora: 16,384 effectivematerial response records and22,951 molecular conformer/force records in their default configurations.
Time samples are not counted as independent systems. Source-linked and dynamical configurations
are additional. The active HolonForge/Aster and Mora-3 repositories were read only; no data,
vocabulary, checkpoint, source enrollment or training process was changed.
The material corpus covers SSH, Kitaev, QWZ, Rice–Mele, Harper and actual clean-dessin
classical RG models. The molecular corpus includes designed amides and peptides, with explicit-H
MMFF94 calculations, geometric persistence, and method-labelled conformations. Forty-eight
existing Aster MP20 structures, 32 existing Mora PubChem identities and eight public PDB structures supply separately labelled anchors. Their new computed responses are not new experimental
observations, DFT labels, binding affinities or CISS measurements.
All conformer, gauge, intervention and task descendants share a partition. Specified physical/chemical similarity edges are closed transitively before component-level stratification. Large
components are kept intact even when 80/10/10 proportions or rare-class coverage become impossible. This gives a finite, threshold-relative separation statement; it cannot guarantee every kind of
statistical independence or prevent overfitting. Source anchors are train only. Molecular anchors
are also checked against every default heldout using the same chemical and applicable geometric
thresholds; conflicting parent groups are excluded. Global exposure to the models’ previous corpora
is a separate evaluation gate.
For a number-conserving one-particle Hamiltonian H, write Pij = ⟨c†j ci ⟩ and U (t) = e−itH . The
data use P (t) = U (t)P (0)U (t)∗ , occupations ni = Pii and outgoing currents Jij = −2 Im(Hij Pji ).
P
Then ṅi = − j Jij , directly from iṖ = [H, P ]. Paired BdG evolution includes its independently
computed condensate source; it is not falsely treated as electron-number-conserving normal transport.
An exact interacting Hubbard dimer supplements Gaussian exchange correlations. Molecular nuclear
dynamics uses MMFF94, with a separately labelled, uncalibrated effective π-orbital model for charge
modulation.
Parquet configurations retain condition, target and evaluation roles, source versions/hashes,
units, failures and audit evidence. The local dataset explorer shows actual structures, currents,
time evolution, graph wavelets and checked circuit compilations. These computations support
candidate discovery and model validation; the independent arithmetic determinant/zero-location
151

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

34

bridge remains open. No new whole-chapter Lean formalization is asserted.

34.2

Construction, curation and interpretation

34.2.1

A record is an evidence-bearing physical task

Each default Parquet row has a persistent record identity, parent/similarity group, partition,
evidence class, lossless scientific payload and typed numeric columns. Forward inputs have the
prefix condition_; supervision uses target_; evaluation_ fields are excluded from inputs. The
full payload, source hash, record identifier and audit text are not automatically fed into a model.
Otherwise an apparent condition/target mask could still leak the target through metadata or a
duplicated representation. An inverse task needs an explicit desired-condition contract and role
change.
The origin fields distinguish a new simulation from a reused planned-source identity or geometry
with new labels. The source-anchor configuration preserves existing train assignments. New public
PDB coordinates retain their own source identity rather than being called members of PDBbind
or newly acquired experimental quantum measurements. All anchors are train only: they do not
establish blind performance on a model that may have encountered the parent.
The actual native interfaces are tested without changing either model. Aster accepts conditiononly Hamiltonian records through its existing tokenizer. Changing a hidden target leaves those
input tokens unchanged. Mora-3’s scalar graphs preserve computed/predicted evidence. Its existing
scalar/array supervision interfaces reject those targets under their observed-only policy. That
rejection is recorded, not bypassed by changing the evidence label. Source construction is therefore
distinct from the owning model’s future admission, scaling, objective and evaluation decisions.

34.2.2

Effective material families and gap guards

The two-band QWZ model uses
H(kx , ky ) = sin kx σx + sin ky σy + (m + cos kx + cos ky )σz .
Its continuum band gap is 2 min{|m + 2|, |m|, |m − 2|}. To see this, put u = cos kx , v = cos ky .
The squared positive energy is 2 + m2 + 2m(u + v) + 2uv, a bilinear function on [−1, 1]2 ; its
minimum occurs at a corner. Thus an odd mesh that misses π cannot certify a gap near m = 0
or2. The corrected release uses this analytic guard, even32/64 meshes, random gauge checks and an
unavailable label near closure. The occupied-band integer uses the oriented link-product convention
of Fukui–Hatsugai–Suzuki [100]; the sign convention is retained in the row.
For the Rice–Mele pump, take t1 = 1 + δ cos θ, t2 = 1 − δ cos θ and ∆ = r sin θ. With positive
t1 , t2 , minimization over k first gives (t1 − t2 )2 + ∆2 at k = π; minimization over θ gives the exact gap
2 min(2δ, r). Harper’s direct gap has a conservative mesh/Lipschitz guard, explicitly distinguished
from interval certification. The displayed bulk slice has ky = 0; an open strip is evaluated separately
at ky = .37. A single strip is not a proof of its entire edge dispersion.
The SSH winding, Kitaev sign invariant and classical dessin RG retain their own hypotheses.
Surface genus is not an electronic phase index. In the Kitaev row, a physical onsite defect changes
particle and hole diagonals with opposite signs. Its gauge change has the form diag(G, Ḡ). These
preserve the recorded particle–hole symmetry; arbitrary independent diagonal perturbations would
not. A quarantined draft revealed this error before release. No failed draft replaces a verified
artifact.
152

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

34.2.3

34

Molecular geometry and forces

The molecular set contains explicit chemical graphs, atom order, hydrogens, stereochemistry, methodlabelled optimized references and bounded coordinate perturbations. MMFF94 supplies empirical
molecular-mechanics energies, analytic forces and partial charges [101]. It supplies neither ab-initio
electron density nor experimental affinity, ADMET, redox or biological efficacy. An optimization
convergence code is not a theorem of a unique global minimum. Absolute MMFF energies are not
cross-molecule atomization energies.
The checker reconstructs the stored bond graph in its actual atom order. A canonical SMILES
string determines chemical identity but need not give the coordinate-array order. Geometry-assigned
stereochemistry is checked against that identity; mirror controls fail. Energies and forces are
recalculated on every accepted record. Central energy differences check force signs/units, and proper
rotations/translations check the expected scalar/vector behavior. Missing or unsupported labels are
not imputed.
For the heavy-atom point cloud, the complete finite Vietoris–Rips2-skeleton gives H0 , H1 over
F2 . All edges and triangles, including ties, are ordered explicitly. Independent boundary-rank
calculations verify selected Betti numbers against the barcodes. These are geometric distancefiltration features; a molecular loop or persistence bar is not an electronic topological phase. MMFF
and the idealized π-orbital Hamiltonian retain separate labels.

34.2.4

What the split certificate proves

Let V be the finite parent set and connect two parents whenever a declared similarity test passes.
Add edges for all shared source/variant lineages. Assign a partition to each connected component
and inherit it for every row. If an accepted similarity edge joined two partitions, its endpoints would
lie in one component with two assignments, a contradiction. This proves the exact finite no-crossing
property for the declared relation.
For materials, normalized parameter and relative finite-spectrum thresholds are family/sizespecific. SSH hopping swaps, Kitaev chemical-potential sign and the classical RG global-sign
symmetry are folded conservatively. For molecules, stereo-agnostic constitution, nonempty Murcko
scaffold and radius-two Morgan Tanimoto threshold .65 form chemical components. A second
all-conformer pass adds edges for equal heavy-element multisets with sorted pair-distance RMS
at most .10Å. This descriptor is rotation/permutation invariant but is not a complete congruence
invariant or a certified RMSD optimizer; conservative false-positive grouping is possible.
Whole-component allocation balances row counts and declared family/topology or molecularsize/persistence strata. It never breaks a component to manufacture an80/10/10 result. The large
peptide component makes the molecular training partition much larger than80%; rare/held-out
peptide coverage can consequently be absent. Exact distributions and cross-partition nearest
witnesses accompany the release. Protein TM-score, homology separation and global overlap with
the models’ complete historical corpora are not certified by small-molecule fingerprints. Threshold
separation cannot itself guarantee freedom from all leakage or overfitting.

follows by adjoints, trace follows by cyclicity, and P (t) = U P (0)U −1 is a similarity. Thus 0 ≤ P ≤ I
and projector occupancy remain valid. Computing the diagonal of −i[H, P ] gives

34.2.6

Source-backed peptides and genuine boundaries

Public wwPDB coordinates are supplied under CC0 [102]. The source packet includes Trp-cage1L2Y,
villin1VII, historical Trp-zipper1HRW (superseded by 1LE0, with the actual file HEADER identity
retained), pancreatic polypeptide1PPT, azurin1AZU, crambin1CRN, ubiquitin1UBQ and engrailed
homeodomain1ENH. The full source file, checksum, DOI, first-model/chain selection and bounded
residue-subsystem mapping remain explicit. An NMR ensemble’s model order is not physical time.
Azurin’s electron-transport classification does not calibrate our coarse contact Hamiltonian or turn
it into an electronic-structure result.
The CISS review and peptide-bridge charge-transfer preprint motivate future spin, environment
and lead benchmarks [103, 104]; the source review is focused, not an independent reproduction of
their experiments. A spinless contact graph cannot supply their spin polarization, electron-transfer
rates or measured quantum-biological mechanism. Synthetic strain, vacancy, mirror, coupling and
conformer variants have their own provenance and share the parent group. They are additional
training candidates, not newly observed systems.

Chapter 35

ToricGT: quantum features, tropical
certificates and mirror interfaces
ToricGT organizes graph tokens, trajectories and auxiliary algebraic data; its companion research
program separates architecture, dynamics, memory, oracle labels and toric training objectives
[132, 133]. This chapter extends that organization to the present surface-order problem. A quantum
feature map can replace a specified finite feature block, and exact algebra can check a proposed
relation or parameter. Neither operation supplies the arithmetic identification needed by Hilbert–
Pólya.
The inspected ToricGT revision is a5199bc989a6fb170e21220e5e87bf95bc2fc0b4; the program revision is f91ba1811b5a4f70d53c7120acf8ca2a0049f85b. The files toric_algebra.py,
tropical_toric_certificates.py, cas_certificates.py and the graph-token schemas are the
concrete interfaces used here. Their mathematical objects are not inferred from repository names.
In particular, we do not claim to have reproduced the upstream language-model training campaigns
or their compression scores.

35.1

A toric chart inside an actual dessin representation stratum

Let A be the infinite polynomial elliptic-square dessin algebra of the preceding chapters. It has two
quiver vertices, two arrows in each direction, and the specified locally gentle zero relations. Retain
one arrow X : V0 → V1 , put the other arrows equal to zero, and frame V0 = V1 = k 2 . Every path
of length two now acts by zero, so these are genuine continuous finite-dimensional modules of the
completed order as well. Impose the additional closed condition rank X ≤ 1. In the chosen frames

The coordinate ring is B = k[z00 , z01 , z10 , z11 ]/(f ). This is a representation stratum with a rank
condition; it is not an assertion that the full order or its invariant ring is a toric variety.
Proposition 35.1 (The Segre semigroup and its full relation). The homomorphism

Proof. The displayed binomial maps to zero. Replace any factor z00 z11 in a monomial by z10 z01
until no such factor remains. The integer kernel of the exponent matrix is generated by (1, −1, −1, 1)
in the order (00, 10, 01, 11). Two distinct reduced monomials cannot have the same image: their
exponent difference would be a nonzero multiple of this vector, and the monomial at the positive end
would contain both z00 and z11 . Distinct reduced monomials therefore have linearly independent
images. Reducing a polynomial proves ker π = (f ). Multiplication by f is injective in the polynomial

monomials.
This supplies a small complete certificate rather than an unspecified “syzygy loss.” A learned
output zb can be penalized by |f (zb)|2 . After rationalization, checking f (z) = 0 exactly certifies the
rank-one relation. It does not certify the learned point’s interpretation as a module with other
nonzero arrows, an arithmetic endomorphism, or a positive spectral family. The multiplicative
parametrization zij = ai bj enforces this one relation identically, including degenerate rank-zero
points.
The projectivization of this cone is the Segre surface P1 × P1 . The full framed representation
space, the rank-one cone, its projectivization and a quotient by the vertex group are different
objects. Under the full left-right action on X, its individual entries are not all semi-invariants. The
determinant has its usual determinant character, but is identically zero on this stratum. Framing is
thus part of the example, not an invisible choice of gauge.

35.2

Tropical charts and exactly what their certificates prove

The same square of exponents defines the max-plus function
ψ(u, v) = max{0, u, v, u + v} = max{0, u} + max{0, v}.
Its corner locus is the union of the coordinate axes. There are four one-dimensional rays of weight
one, with primitive outgoing directions (1, 0), (−1, 0), (0, 1), (0, −1). Their sum is zero. This is an
exact local balancing calculation. The Newton polygon is the unit square.
v

35

There are two associated tropical constructions, and they should not be conflated. The hypersurface of a four-term polynomial with this support lives in R2 . The tropicalization of the two-term
Segre ideal lives in R4 and satisfies
w00 + w11 = w10 + w01 .
The latter follows because the two terms of the binomial must have equal valuation. Over a valued
field, tropicalization records valuations and initial degenerations; it is not a literal embedding of the
tropical set as an ordinary algebraic subvariety of the complex toric variety. A fan specifies a toric
compactification, while compatibility with that fan and the valued-field data specifies the relevant
tropical geometry.
The inspected helper in tropical_toric_certificates.py uses integer supports, rational wall
intersections and polygon edge normals. At this revision its input conversion applies int to floats.
Consequently the exact claim here is restricted to inputs already verified to be integral; a nonintegral
learned exponent cannot be silently rounded into an exact certificate. Pairwise ties also need their
dimension checked. In the square example the pair (00, 11) ties maximally only at the origin, as does
(10, 01). These zero-dimensional sets are not extra facets. A balancing sum for one dual polygon is
not by itself a certified Chow class on an unspecified complete fan, nor an exact description of a
higher-dimensional tropical variety. The stronger boolean names in the upstream record are not
used as additional mathematical premises here.
A useful hybrid pipeline learns affine scores with real parameters, exports integer/rational
candidates with an explicit approximation rule, and then recomputes active cells, dimensions,
weights and balancing exactly. The approximation error belongs to the learned model; the certificate
belongs to the exported data. Recording both prevents a small floating residual from becoming an
alleged exact identity.

35.3

The quantum phase block and its cocycle

The ToricGT shift convention differs from the forward shift used in some other chapters. At θ = p/q,
with ω = e2πiθ , its untwisted matrices act by
U ej = ω j ej ,

V ej = ej−1 mod q ,

V U = ωU V.

If p, q are coprime they are a finite irreducible carrier of a central quotient. They do not faithfully
represent the full rational rotation algebra. A boundary twist has to have modulus one if V is to be
unitary. Rational approximants to an irrational θ produce a sequence of finite models, but not an
automatically nested sequence of matrix-algebra inclusions.
Proposition 35.2 (Ordered and symmetric Weyl multipliers). For integer pairs x = (a, b) and
y = (c, d) set W (x) = U a V b . Then
W (x)W (y) = ω bc W (x + y).
Choose a real lift of θ and set D(a, b) = eπiθab U a V b . Then
D(x)D(y) = eπiθ(bc−ad) D(x + y),

D(x)D(y) = e2πiθ(bc−ad) D(y)D(x).

These formulas are on Z2 ; descent to exponents modulo q requires retaining the resulting central
phases.

formula. Associativity also follows directly from bilinearity: bc + (b + d)e = de + b(c + e) for a third
pair (e, f ).

avoids that operation.
For a density matrix ρ, one physically measurable feature is
fx (ρ) = Tr(ρD(x)).
Its real and imaginary parts are expectations of the Hermitian operators (D(x) + D(x)∗ )/2 and
(D(x) − D(x)∗ )/(2i). They lie in [−1, 1]. Thus a classical graph-token block can receive measured
finite-dimensional phase features; a variational circuit can learn the state preparation or bounded
observables; and a classical head can combine these with exact combinatorial records. No claim of
quantum speedup follows from replacing an already inexpensive matrix calculation by a circuit.
Proposition 35.3 (A genuine positive feature kernel). For any finite list xi and any density matrix

The latter comparison is an additional theorem, not a consequence of positivity of ρ.

35.4

A quantum–classical ToricGT extension with auditable interfaces

A typed record for a surface example should contain dart permutations, arrow endpoints, zero
relations, central parameters, coefficient fields, module dimensions and category labels. Neural
embeddings can be added to these records; they should not replace them. A candidate hybrid block
is

where α is a normalized classical attention rule, ρv is prepared by a specified finite circuit, and cv is

simulated classically, as in the finite training examples of the preceding computational chapter.
159

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

Dessin and

Verified result

Exact algebra
and proof checker

There are three distinct uses. First, supervised regression can learn a coarse-graining map
against exact Ising or Gaussian targets, with a held-out parameter set. Second, a proposal model
can search for a small module map or a polynomial identity, checked by exact substitution and
stated presentation relations. Third, a proof-search policy can select candidate lemmas or tactics,
whose accepted output must pass the proof assistant. Only the last two have the potential to yield
new exact mathematical results, and only when the checker verifies the full exported statement. A
classifier predicting that a proof is valid is not such a checker.
The ToricGT program’s certificate distinction is particularly useful here: store exact CAS data,
validated surrogates and unavailable backends as different provenance classes. When comparing
a quantum feature head with a classical one, hold the data split, parameter budget and objective
fixed; report sample or simulation cost as well as error. A network with extra features need not
improve the objective. None of the finite experiments in this book is an upstream full-transformer
language-model ablation.
An exact candidate metric G for a finite matrix M illustrates the proposal/check distinction.
For rational real entries, symmetry and all principal-minor nonnegativity can certify G ⪰ 0. Positive
definiteness and GM = M ∗ G then imply similarity to a Hermitian matrix: G1/2 M G−1/2 is Hermitian.
Checking a few sampled minors or a small floating residual does not prove these assertions, and
applying the result to an arithmetic family still requires uniform positivity and the exact arithmetic
determinant comparison.

35.5

Mirror symmetry supplies category comparisons, not a spectral shortcut

Kontsevich–Soibelman develop torus-fibration and homological-perturbation methods, including an
A∞ comparison for specified subcategories of abelian mirrors [134, Sections 6–8, Theorem 4]. A
lattice of phase labels is useful computational data for such geometry, but a finite Weyl pair alone
does not construct either Fukaya objects or an A∞ functor. The latter must carry complexes of
morphisms and all higher composition maps with their signs and convergence rules.
A concrete toric theorem provides a better model for the proposed link. For a smooth projective
toric X, Abouzaid constructs a full A∞ sub-pre-category of the relative Fukaya category of its
Landau–Ginzburg mirror that is quasi-equivalent to the DG category of line bundles on X [135,
Theorem 1.2 and Corollary 1.4]. The theorem gives an embedding after the indicated derived/split
closure; it does not assert an unrestricted equivalence for every non-Fano toric variety or for our
singular completed order.
For intuition, the customary toric mirror potential for the Segre surface is
W (z, w) = z + z −1 + w + w−1 ,

(z, w) ∈ (C∗ )2 .

Solving z∂z W = w∂w W = 0 gives (z, w) = (±1, ±1), with critical values 4, 0, 0, −4. The logarithmic
Hessian is diagonal with entries z + z −1 and w + w−1 and is nonsingular at those four points. This
160

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

35

is a direct calculation
of a mirror potential. By contrast the two-level clock matrix 2Z + 2X has
√
eigenvalues ±2 2, since XZ + ZX = 0. The critical values and matrix eigenvalues are plainly
different data. A proposal to relate them must specify a functor or quantization and its normalization;
visual resemblance of their torus coordinates is not that comparison.
For gentle surfaces, the precise positive input already used in this book is the equivalence for a
homologically smooth graded locally gentle algebra with its prescribed stopped surface and line
field [66, Theorem 3.2.2]. Our nonempty closed dessin algebras have forbidden cycles and fail
that smoothness hypothesis. A stopped quotient or categorical resolution can be a meaningful
intermediate object, but it requires its own functor. Ordinary exact quotient inflation does not
automatically preserve derived Hom spaces, AR triangles or determinants. In particular, the
polynomial node branch k[x] has a valid stopped-annulus interpretation, whereas inflation into
k[x, y]/(xy) is not derived fully faithful, as proved earlier.
A finite training loss for m21 = 0 or a chain-map residual checks only the displayed truncation. A
genuine category construction additionally needs the remaining A∞ equations, admissible geometric
objects, grading, compactness or convergence, and the claimed generation result. Tropical gradient
trees can guide computations of compositions under appropriate geometric hypotheses; they do
not remove those obligations. This is a constructive research interface: learn a small candidate,
calculate its maps exactly, and then prove the specified comparison.

35.6

What would make the learned component relevant to GRH?

For the present Hilbert–Pólya program the useful learned outputs are candidates for arithmetic
correspondences, controlled positive metrics, coefficient kernels outside the classes already refuted, or
estimates with an exact symbolic witness. Every candidate must be checked against local Frobenius
factors, central and deck actions, determinant multiplicities, conductor and gamma normalizations,
and compact-uniform convergence on the stated complex domain. A Dirichlet comparison is not a
proof for Hecke or general Artin L-functions.
An exact finite witness can disprove a proposed universal construction, and that is valuable
progress. A successful finite fit only proposes its next theorem. Neither a toric binomial certificate
nor a positive quantum feature kernel proves that all arithmetic approximants have their zeros
on the critical line. The small-gcd analytic estimate and the independent positive arithmetic
determinant comparison therefore remain separate load-bearing research tasks. The role of the
hybrid architecture is to make their search more explicit, testable and reproducible.

35.7

An executed hybrid spectral experiment

The executable example examples/quantum/toricgt_hybrid.py imports the pinned ToricGT
rotation-algebra module. For θ = 1/4 it obtains four-dimensional Weyl generators and forms the
Hermitian Harper family
Hλ = U + U ∗ + λ(V + V ∗ ),

0.1 ≤ λ ≤ 1.8.

A two-qubit PennyLane feature circuit and affine head, with twelve parameters, learn the smallest
eigenvalue divided by six. Seed 20261104 specifies one hundred sampled couplings, with seventy-five
training and twenty-five held-out samples. After 160 Adam updates the held-out mean-square loss
is 4.01905 × 10−5 , compared with 0.220518 at initialization. These are finite simulated spectra, not
L-function zeros.
161

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

35

For six rational test couplings, the program forms the exact rational matrix
Hλ = diag(2, 0, −2, 0) + λ Adj(C4 ).
It rounds a learned proposal to a rational number E and checks all fifteen nonempty principal minors
of Hλ − EI. A real symmetric matrix is positive semidefinite exactly when every principal minor
is nonnegative, so an accepted proposal is an exact lower eigenvalue bound. A rejected proposal
is repaired by rational bisection, starting from the independent Gershgorin lower bound −2 − 2λ
and the Rayleigh upper bound −2. The saved rational minors certify the resulting lower bounds.
Thus learning proposes; an independent exact criterion accepts or repairs. The certificate does
not depend on the neural prediction being accurate. No arithmetic operator or completed-order
equivalence is claimed by this experiment.
This √
benchmark has an exact closed-form solution: det(tI − Hλ ) = t2 (t2 − 4(1 + λ2 )), so
E0 = −2 1 + λ2 . For λ > 0, rows one and three of Hλ agree, and the difference of rows zero and
two is 2/λ times row one. Thus its rank is at most two. Its trace is zero and tr(Hλ2 ) = 8(1 + λ2 ),
giving the stated eigenvalues (and hence characteristic polynomial). The experiment deliberately
has an exact oracle: it tests learning, certificate acceptance and repair on a known problem rather
than presenting a numerical fit as a new spectral theorem.

162

Chapter 36

Two-parameter persistence, Schur
syzygies and surface-order coefficients
A persistence diagram records how objects and maps change under an ordered family of inclusions.
A projective resolution records the generators, relations and relations among relations of one module.
These descriptions meet naturally over a polynomial parameter ring. They must still be distinguished
from the infinite locally gentle algebra attached to the surface: the parameter ring describes the
filtration, not a replacement surface algebra.
The three supplied papers motivate these interfaces [136, 137, 138]. We use their surface-order,
lattice and finite-presentation ideas with the corrections already proved in this book. In particular, a
matrix satisfying consecutive-product zero is a complex, not automatically a resolution; Fitting base
change is not exact base change; and the existence of a generic resolution is not a finite terminating
algorithm. The worked example below proves its own exactness and exhibits an actual topological
realization.

36.1

The polynomial ring encodes the two commuting directions

Let k be a field and give N2 its coordinatewise order. A persistence module is a covariant functor
P : N2 → Vectk . Writing Pa,b = P (a, b), put
R = k[x, y],

deg x = (1, 0),

deg y = (0, 1),

P=

M

Pa,b .

a,b≥0

Multiplication by x and y is the map to the next coordinate in the corresponding direction.
Functoriality makes their square commute. Conversely the graded pieces and multiplication maps
of an N2 -graded R-module define such a functor. General transition maps are multiplication by
xc−a y d−b when (a, b) ≤ (c, d). The exponents are nonnegative in this direction, a useful sign check.
This gives an equivalence for the stated discrete diagrams. Finite generation is an additional
condition, not a consequence of having finite-dimensional pieces. A module can have infinitely
many independent births. Finite cellular input with finitely many birth grades gives the finite
presentations used here. A real-parameter filtration needs an appropriate discretization or a different
coefficient algebra before being treated as a module over k[x, y].
We write R(−a, −b) for a free module whose generator has grade (a, b). Over a field, a finite
graded module has a minimal graded free resolution of length at most two. Minimality means that
every differential entry belongs to (x, y); tensoring with k = R/(x, y) then kills the differentials.
The numbers and grades of the surviving generators are the multigraded Betti numbers. The field
163

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

36

hypothesis matters: Z[x, y] has homological dimension three, and the regular sequence (2, x, y)
supplies a length-three example. The particular resolution below also works integrally, but that
does not make every integral resolution length two.

36.3

A bifiltered CW realization and the syzygies it creates

Start with one vertex and an oriented loop e, both born at grade (0, 0). Attach three two-cells by
degree-one maps to that loop, at grades
D20 : (2, 0),

D11 : (1, 1),

D02 : (0, 2).

At grade (a, b) include precisely the cells whose birth grades are at most (a, b). Every boundary
is already present, so these are actual subcomplex inclusions and their squares commute. The
persistence cellular chain modules are
C2 = F1 ,

C1 = Re,

C0 = R,

∂2 = (x2 xy y 2 ),

∂1 = 0.

Therefore H1 = M exactly. No assertion that every abstract persistence module has this particular
geometric form is needed.
The loop survives before any disk is present and dies when the first disk appears. Two disks
attached along their common boundary form a sphere. The first two persistent H2 generators are
the compatible cell cycles
−yD20 + xD11

at (2, 1),

−yD11 + xD02

at (1, 2).

Theorem 36.1 proves H2 ∼

all three disks give two independent sphere classes. Thus the same matrix d2 is a relation among
the killing cells and a positive persistent homology class in the larger CW complex. This auxiliary
CW space need not be a Riemann surface: three disks along one circle are not a manifold. It is a
topological realization of the filtration module, not another claimed cellular dessin.

Schur modules organize the generators and the first syzygies

Assume now char k = 0, put V = kx ⊕ ky, and let GL(V ) act on R = Sym V by linear substitution.
The ideal (x, y)2 is invariant. Its degree-two generators form Sym2 V , with basis x2 , xy, y 2 . The
linear syzygies form the kernel of multiplication
V ⊗ Sym2 V −→ Sym3 V.
There is a natural equivariant map
δ:

2
^

V ⊗ V −→ V ⊗ Sym2 V,

(v ∧ w) ⊗ z 7−→ v ⊗ wz − w ⊗ vz.

Multiplication kills its image. On the two basis vectors its values are exactly the two columns of d2 :
(x ∧ y) ⊗ x 7→ x ⊗ xy − y ⊗ x2 ,

(x ∧ y) ⊗ y 7→ x ⊗ y 2 − y ⊗ xy.

They are independent, and the kernel has dimension 6 − 4 = 2, so δ identifies it with det V ⊗ V =
S(2,1) V . The Littlewood–Richardson rule gives
V ⊗ Sym2 V ∼
= Sym3 V ⊕ S(2,1) V.
Here the partition convention uses symmetric rows. The equivariant total- graded resolution has
multiplicity spaces k, Sym2 V, S(2,1) V in generator degrees 0, 2, 3. The diagonal torus recovers their
bigraded weights (2, 0), (1, 1), (0, 2) and (2, 1), (1, 2).
The grading distinction is essential. General GL2 substitutions mix x and y, so they preserve
total degree, not the two original filtration coordinates. Only the diagonal torus preserves each
bigrading. In positive characteristic the displayed maps and our exact resolution remain valid, but
semisimple direct-sum representation theory must not be assumed. Nor does applying a Schur
functor termwise to an arbitrary exact sequence preserve exactness, as the earlier counterexamples
show.

36.5

Fitting data, exactness and specialization
D

For a finite presentation Rp −
→ Rq → N → 0 define
Fittj (N ) = Iq−j (D),

I0 (D) = R,

where Ir is the ideal of r-minors, Ir = R for r ≤ 0, and Ir = 0 for r > min(p, q). The ideal is
independent of the chosen finite presentation. Its formation commutes with arbitrary base change
because minors are polynomials in the presentation entries. For our cyclic module,
Fitt0 (M ) = (x, y)2 ,

Fittj (M ) = R

(j ≥ 1).

The support is the origin, but its Fitting scheme retains a length-three thickening. This is not the
same datum as the rank invariant of the ordered graded pieces.
The 2-minors of d2 are y 2 , −xy, x2 , so their ideal is also (x, y)2 . Expected ranks are 1, 2 and this
ideal has grade two; x2 , y 2 is a regular sequence. These facts agree with the finite-free acyclicity
criterion of Buchsbaum–Eisenbud [139], independently of our direct proof. Over a general Noetherian
ring, a height count cannot silently replace the criterion’s grade/regular-sequence hypotheses. The
166

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

36

criterion concerns a finite complex over a commutative ring; it does not certify every infinite
path-algebra resolution.
Arbitrary specialization does not preserve that exactness. At x = y = 0 both matrices vanish,
leaving positive homology k 3 , k 2 in degrees one and two. More informatively, after setting only
y = 0, the complex has
H0 = k[x]/(x2 ),

H1 = (k[x]/(x))2 ,

H2 = 0.

These follow immediately from d1 = (x2 , 0, 0) and the two surviving entries x of d2 . They are the
derived-base-change Tor groups. Flat base change, suitable Tor vanishing, or a new exactness proof
is needed to obtain an ordinary specialized resolution. A generic parameter space and a small
differential residual do not remove those conditions.

36.6

A correct finite space of complexes and its incidence model

For variety language in this section take k algebraically closed. Let Vi have dimensions ni for
i = 0, 1, 2. Fix feasible ranks a, b with
a ≤ min(n0 , n1 ),

b ≤ min(n1 , n2 ),

a + b ≤ n1 .

The reduced variety
B

A

Ca,b = {(A, B) : V2 −
→ V1 −
→ V0 , AB = 0, rank A ≤ a, rank B ≤ b}
parametrizes based complexes with these upper bounds, not isomorphism classes of resolutions.
Consider flags L ⊂ K ⊂ V1 with dim L = b, dim K = n1 − a, and impose
im B ⊂ L ⊂ K ⊂ ker A.
Over the flag variety the allowed maps form the vector bundle
Hom(V1 /K, V0 ) ⊕ Hom(V2 , L).
Its total space is smooth and irreducible. Projection is proper onto Ca,b : for any complex with
the rank bounds, first extend im B to a b-plane inside ker A, then extend it to an (n1 − a)-plane
there. On the full-rank locus the choices are uniquely L = im B, K = ker A. Full ranks exist by
feasibility, so this projection is birational. This proves the asserted incidence resolution of the
reduced rank-bound variety.
For (n0 , n1 , n2 ) = (1, 3, 2) and (a, b) = (1, 2), the two flag spaces coincide in a plane, and the
base is Gr(2, k 3 ). The vector bundle has fiber dimension 1 + 4 and base dimension two, giving
total dimension seven. Full-rank points are exact in positive degrees because im B = ker A and B is
injective. The zero pair is also a point of the complex variety and is not exact. Our polynomial
matrices map the punctured plane to the exact locus; the origin maps to that nonexact boundary.
A specialization of the universal matrices therefore still needs its own homological check.
Bruns’ generic-resolution construction uses a directed limit of rings which kills positive homology
in the limit [140, Theorem 1, pp.35–36]. It does not assert that some finite stage always becomes
exact, nor that every specialization of the resulting matrices preserves exactness. The finite example
in this chapter makes its stronger local claims by explicit calculation rather than by invoking an
unspecified generic object.

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

Coefficients in a genuine infinite dessin order

Return to the actual nodal interval dessin and distinguish its variables:
A = k[ξ, η]/(ξη),

Λ = k[[ξ, η]]/(ξη),

u = ξ + η.

Both A and Λ are infinite dimensional over k. The completed order is free of rank two over k[[u]], on
1, ξ, with ξ 2 = uξ and η = u − ξ. Multiplication by u is injective, as is clear in its two normalization
branches. Set E = Λ/u2 Λ. It has basis 1, ξ, u, uξ over k, nontrivial arrow actions, and
u2

0 −→ Λ −→ Λ −→ E −→ 0
is a genuine free resolution. The module E is finite length, not a k[[u]]-free lattice. Its finite
dimension does not turn its parent order into a finite-dimensional substitute.
The family E ⊗k M is a persistence module valued in continuous Λ-modules: each nonzero piece
is E, and its structure maps are the identity or zero as in Figure 36.1. The actions of x, y commute
with the fixed action of u, ξ, η. There is no identification of either persistence direction with the
order uniformizer.
More precisely put B = Λ ⊗k k[x, y]. Tensor the length-one Λ-resolution just displayed with
Theorem 36.1 over k. Its finite total complex has free B-ranks
(rank F0 , rank F1 , rank F2 , rank F3 ) = (1, 4, 5, 2)
and resolves E ⊗k M . If Qi denotes the order resolution and Pj the persistence resolution, the total
differential on Qi ⊗ Pj is
D = ∂Q ⊗ 1 + (−1)i 1 ⊗ dP .

The cross terms cancel, so consecutive products are zero. For exactness, write a positive-degree
cycle as (a, b), with da + u2 b = 0 and db = 0. Modulo u2 the first component is a cycle in E ⊗k P• .
That complex is exact in positive degrees, since tensoring vector spaces over k is exact. Hence
ā = dc̄. Lift c̄ to c in the free term and write a − dc = u2 e. The original equation becomes
u2 (de + b) = 0. Multiplication by u2 is injective on every free term, so b = −de. Consequently
(a, b) = D(c, e), as required. The same argument includes the top degree by putting missing
terms equal to zero. Degree-zero homology is B/(u2 , x2 , xy, y 2 ) = E ⊗k M . This proves exactness
without assuming that an arbitrary tensor product of resolutions stays exact. The ranks follow from
(1 + z)(1 + 3z + 2z 2 ) = 1 + 4z + 5z 2 + 2z 3 .
This construction is compatible with the original center action. It does not shorten the infinite
resolution of the nodal residue simple, make E a lattice, or construct Frobenius. Restriction to the
commutative coefficient ring k[[u]][x, y] doubles these free ranks. Determinant functors apply to the
specified finite perfect complex, with virtual rank 1 − 4 + 5 − 2 = 0, but determinant multiplicity
and arithmetic comparison remain additional questions.

36.8

Quantum data, monotonicity and the next usable interface

An arbitrary time-varying interaction graph is not a bifiltration: an edge can disappear at a later
time. One valid alternative fixes finite birth pairs (ae , be ) for edges and includes an edge exactly
168

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

36

when both coordinates are reached, then takes the flag complex. Another uses a cumulative union
over times up to the first coordinate and decreasing interaction thresholds in the second. Both give
inclusions; the second records cumulative history rather than individual snapshots. Vertices and
higher cells must have compatible birth grades. Zigzag persistence is a different appropriate tool
when deletions must be retained.
Quantum channels are linear on density matrices, but two unrelated channels need not commute.
Assigning them to the two directions does not define a persistence functor unless the required
squares commute. One may instead extract a declared classical correlation graph, choose a monotone
filtration, and compute its homology with the conventions above. The data-to-distance rule and its
statistical uncertainty still need justification.
For the research program this yields concrete trainable and checkable targets: propose birth
grades, presentation entries or a low-degree syzygy; test inclusions, gradings, the matrix identities
and actual exactness; record Betti and Fitting data before interpreting them. A loss penalizing D2
checks only the complex equation. The exact module example, the flag-incidence model and the
surface-order coefficient resolution show how to add the missing checks. None identifies those finite
invariants with a completed arithmetic L-function, provides a positive arithmetic spectral limit, or
proves GRH.

36.9

The executable staircase and quantum laboratory

The canonical on-page tool accompanies this chapter; other chapters link here. Proposition 2.3
generalizes the worked square-ideal resolution to every strict Artinian staircase. Four displayed ideals
have lengths 3, 6, 13, 16, with custom natural exponents also allowed. The tool shows generator and
adjacent-syzygy degrees, standard monomials, exact comparable-grade maps and cellular incidence
ranks. Coefficients in E multiply field dimensions by four; the displayed free ranks over Λ[x, y]
instead come from the u2 cone. These are different rings.
Specializing the free resolution at a point over F2 computes its fiber-complex homology. At the
origin its dimensions are (1, m, m − 1); at each of the three nonzero F2 points they vanish, since
a pure-power generator becomes a unit. This is derived fiber data, not the CW homology at a
filtration grade. The signed maximal minors and Hilbert numerator are checked independently with
symbolic polynomials.
The quantum tab uses the single saved trained four-qubit QCNN unitary U and the channel
T (ρ) = Tr2,3 (U ρU ∗ ). The designed inputs have their
√ last three qubits in |000⟩. Their first qubit
runs over eight equatorial states (|0⟩ + e2πij/8 |1⟩)/ 2 and both poles. This is an out-of-trainingdistribution illustrative family, not new training or a held-out performance claim. Birth labels are
fixed from the input population (I + Z0 )/2: south 0, equator 500, north 1000. They are retained at
the output; recomputing them from output energy would not justify the same identity inclusion.
Floating-point input/output trace distances are scaled by 1000 and rounded upward. Their
frozen integer graph labels are saved with a SHA-256 checksum and checkpoint provenance. The
inequality of output and input labels is verified directly, never imposed by taking their minimum.
Proposition 2.4 explains the exact-channel contractivity; the numerical records are not interval
certificates. For the recorded labels, all finite boundary matrices and induced ranks are computed
exactly over F2 . At grade (400, 500) the input contains an equatorial 8-cycle and an isolated south
pole, with (β0 , β1 ) = (2, 1). Its H1 map to (600, 500) has rank one, whereas its map to (800, 1000)
has rank zero. The plotted two-parameter rank table fixes the target and varies comparable source
grades; it is not a complete multiparameter barcode. Rotating the drawing changes the input-qubit
Bloch layout only. The same layout is used at output and does not embed its two-qubit state space.
169

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

36

Independent row elimination and the browser’s bit-column implementation agree on both Betti
numbers and inclusion ranks for the saved cases. These executable finite examples complement the
proofs and the cited persistence theory. The worked square-ideal module now has a kernel-checked
ungraded free resolution, described below. General staircase exactness, graded and categorical
interfaces, the coefficient cone and channel norm contractivity remain separate formalization tasks.
No learned component or finite homology calculation supplies the unresolved positive arithmetic
determinant bridge.

36.10

What the polynomial Lean resolution actually verifies

The file PersistenceResolution.lean uses R = MvPolynomial(Fin 2, k) for an arbitrary field. Its
free coordinate modules are F1 = R × R × R and F2 = R × R, with the matrices of Theorem 36.1.
The augmentation is the actual R-linear quotient map to R/(x2 , xy, y 2 ). The proof of d1_kernel
quantifies over all polynomial coefficient vectors; it is not a sampled scalar-rank test.
The reusable lemma relation_decomposition starts with a commutative domain and a pair
x, y such that x =
̸ 0 and x | yz implies x | z. From x2 a + xyb + y 2 c = 0 it constructs p, q with
(a, b, c) = (−yp, xp−yq, xq). For the actual polynomial variables, the regularity premise is discharged
by viewing R as polynomials in x with coefficients in k[y]. A polynomial is divisible by x exactly
when its constant coefficient vanishes; multiplication by the nonzero y cannot kill such a coefficient.
Thus the final kernel theorem has no unproved regularity hypothesis. The first and last coordinates
then prove d2_injective; d1_range identifies the image with the specified ideal. Augmentation
exactness and surjectivity complete the sequence, and the free terms are proved projective.
The field k carries the residue action r · a = constantCoeff(r)a. The free modules retain
ordinary polynomial multiplication. Both actions are explicit in the source, with action identities
checked in the kernel. For every R-linear map from the appropriate free term to this residue field,
precomposition with d1 or d2 is zero, since all displayed entries contain x or y. These are actual
Hom statements with their module actions, not merely entrywise evaluations. Abstract Ext-group
identifications and the graded minimal-resolution interface are not yet packaged as formal theorems.
All theorem types and axiom dependencies are printed. Only the standard Lean foundations
occur; no project axioms or incomplete proofs are used. This establishes the entire underlying
ungraded worked resolution and its residue Hom differential calculation. It does not formalize
every staircase, the CW model, Schur equivariance, the E-coefficient tensor cone or any arithmetic
determinant comparison. Those distinctions remain visible on the local verification page beside the
executable laboratory.

The remaining arithmetic theorem and
a verifiable research loop
37.1

A precise positive spectral target

For a primitive nonprincipal Dirichlet character χ, choose the completed L-function with its parity,
conductor and gamma factor fixed. A sufficient spectral program requires holomorphic functions FN
on a connected domain containing the critical strip, compact-uniform convergence FN → Λ(s, χ), and
a proved zero-location property for every FN . A self-adjoint determinant representation must include
its domain, determinant regularization, nowhere-zero normalization and coefficient comparison.
Self-adjointness of an unrelated finite matrix does not establish any of these identifications.
If every FN is zero-free off the critical line, Hurwitz on each connected off-line component shows
that the nonzero holomorphic limit is zero-free there. The limit cannot be identically zero on an
open component if it is a nonzero holomorphic function on the connected containing domain. This
last connected-domain hypothesis matters: componentwise convergence alone does not rule out
an identically zero limit on a disconnected component. The conclusion is conditional until the
arithmetic approximants and positive representation are constructed. General Artin functions also
need their own holomorphy statement; Brauer quotients do not supply it. Hecke, Dedekind and
elliptic-curve normalization and critical centers must remain distinct.
The original modular-geodesic argument fails at its prime identification. Integer hyperbolic
√
√
traces and eℓ/2 + e−ℓ/2 = p + 1/ p cannot match all rational primes. An asymptotic similarity
between prime and geodesic counts does not give a termwise equality of Euler factors. Nor does
finite-field purity for a family of curves prove number-field GRH. The local Jacobi theta construction
is a positive arithmetic benchmark with its own coefficient and symmetry obstructions, treated in
the earlier chapters.

37.4.1

Exact factorization, retained zeros and the failed Cauchy route

Proof of the regrouping with original coefficients
The monoid consists of nonzero integral ideals prime to the fixed excluded set S. Every divisor of a
good ideal is good, so the identity µ ∗ ζ0 = δ holds on this same monoid. Expanding convolution
in (37.1) gives finite divisor sums. Multiply the identity by µ(n)2 ν(n)χn (u)W (N n/D) and sum.
The extra squarefree indicator does not change µ(n), since µ(n)3 = µ(n). On its support n = abc
forces each factor to be squarefree and any two to be coprime. Sextic multiplicativity, including
zeros, gives χn (u) = χa (u)χb (u)χc (u); a collision with any factor makes both sides zero. The fixed
character also satisfies ν(n) = ν(a)ν(b)ν(c) on these good ideals.
In the small-factor piece group d = ab. The remaining variable c is squarefree and coprime to d;
N d ≤ U 2 is necessary, but not sufficient for a nonzero βU (d): its specified truncated factorizations
must still exist. For each such factorization on squarefree d, µ(a)µ(b) = µ(d), proving the coefficient
formula. In the large-factor piece group s = bc. The condition N b > U implies N s > U , and
summing that divisor gives exactly gU (s). Since N s > U ≥ 1, s is not the unit ideal; its complete
Möbius divisor sum vanishes, proving the alternative formula for gU . This regrouping never replaces
the two-variable weight by W (N s/D), never deletes (a, s) = 1, and never replaces the resulting
coefficients by a favorable arbitrary family.
One can instead keep the unrestricted convolution identity on nonsquarefree columns, but then
both individual pieces and their local conductor description change. For U = 1 and a good prime p,
the column p2 contributes one to each of aU ∗ aU ∗ ζ0 and bU ∗ bU ∗ ζ0 , even though µ(p2 ) = 0; these
cancel only after the pieces are recombined. Its squared sextic symbol has a cubic local component.
Thus dropping total squarefreeness is not a harmless piecewise simplification, although the full
unrestricted algebraic identity still holds.
175

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

37

The two conductor scales are unchanged
For a squarefree n = as with (a, s) = 1, every prime of n has one nontrivial sextic local component
in the row variable. Hence its primitive finite conductor is the ideal as; N aN s ≍ D on the column
support. On multiplying a column by the conjugate of another, the local exponent is zero at common
primes, 1 at a prime of n/h, and −1 at a prime of n′ /h. The common prime still imposes its original
zero on rows. Writing n = hr, n′ = ht therefore gives exactly
χn (u)χn′ (u) = 1(u,h)=1 χr (u)χt (u),

q = rt,

l = hrt.

Here q is the primitive oscillatory conductor and l a period of the full zero extension. These are
ideals, not necessarily squarefree rational norms. In particular, conjugate primes over one split
rational prime are distinct coprime ideal factors. Full defining moduli of ν and primary conventions
remain fixed in S; this row-conductor calculation does not assert a new uniform reciprocity theorem
in the column variable. In the coprime bulk N q ≍ D2 remains large.

(H + B) N s≍B |gU (s)|2 Dε , the ordinary divisor estimate |gU (s)| ≤ τ (s) and the fixed-field ideal
