A(H + B)Dε ≍ (AH + D)Dε .
That hypothetical slice estimate is not established here. This is an insufficiency of the generic
upper-bound calculation, not a lower bound on the signed energy. At balanced A, B ≍ D1/2 and
H = D3/4 , the AH term loses D1/2 relative to the target; the D term also exceeds H. A sharper
use of the actual gU , cross-factor constraints or covariance between the original pieces is required.
This calculation does not refute all bilinear, moving-cutoff or dispersion methods.
The fixed-U = 1 lower bounds in Proposition 37.2 are different: they are genuine energy lower
bounds for the separate pieces. If W ≥ w0 > 0 on [c1 , c2 ] ⊂ (1, 2), count primes in [c1 D, c2 D] for
I1 and in [c1 D/N q0 , c2 D/N q0 ] for II1 . Removing S and q0 removes finitely many primes. The
prime-ideal theorem gives positive constants times D/ log D in both cases; the latter contribution
has coefficient two. Because every coefficient of II1 is nonnegative, keeping these products is valid.
This is unlike isolating the prime part of the full signed Möbius energy, where uncontrolled cross
terms remain. For the exact requested bound H(HD)ε at H = D3/4 , the competing power is
D3/4+7ε/4 , giving the threshold ε < 1/7. Replacing (HD)ε by Dε reparametrizes the loss and gives
the equivalent 1/4 formulation. The positivity argument is specific to U = 1. For a concrete control,
the primary element 7 − 21ω has norm 637 = 7 · 7 · 13 and three distinct ideal prime factors. At U = 7
its type-I coefficient is −1, its type-II coefficient is −2, and its Möbius value is −1. Thus the separate
pieces need not have nonnegative coefficients at another cutoff; no moving-cutoff obstruction follows
from the preceding positivity proof.

Surface Orders and Cyclic Time

37

Exact finite checks and formal boundaries
The deterministic checker represents every good ideal of norm at most 1024 once by its primary
generator, including inert prime ideals and squarefree products of three distinct prime ideals. It
checks 1535 coefficient/regrouping cases at five cutoffs, retains 19680 encountered original zeros in
actual sextic sums, and verifies 900 pair-conductor exponent ledgers. It evaluates principal ν and
the fixed Kummer character ν(n) = χn (2) on the same good ideals. The finite diagnostic weight is
the rational profile ((N n − D)(2D − N n))2 /D4 on [D, 2D], zero elsewhere; it is not claimed to be
the smooth analytic weight of the theorem. Every identity is algebraic for arbitrary finite weights;
these samples do not prove seminorm or scale-supremum estimates.
For example, at D = 128, H = 16, principal ν and U = 1, the unit-row normalized sums have
the following exact common scaling:
D9/2 M1 −122721831
D9/2 I1
314726661
9/2
D II1
192004830
Their exact difference is II1 − I1 = M1 . The lower-bound theorem uses the prime-ideal proof, not
extrapolation from these small numbers. An independent no-production-import oracle also checks
convolution, squarefree regrouping and weighted sextic sums.
MobiusSplit.lean proves eight actual statements: the inverse-ring identity, natural-number
arithmetic truncation/tail, the Möbius Dirichlet identity, the unit cutoff, its exact tail convolution
and coefficient, and nonnegativity. The natural-number Dirichlet ring is an actual arithmetic object;
it is not the ideal convolution ring. Ideal arithmetic, sextic/Hecke character construction, analytic
prime counting and the unproved signed mean square remain prose or named classical inputs. No
new zero-free region, full GRH proof or positive determinant bridge has been obtained.

37.5

Formal coverage and the next research step

The new Lean interface checks actual Plücker polynomials, nodal matrix relations and determinants,
finite clock/shift identities and Kraus positivity/trace preservation. The short-row module checks
finite-character masks, original finite Gram expansion, matched-row lower bounds and actual
Eisenstein norm/evaluation identities. Full planar Poisson summation, ideal-character interfaces,
the AR classifications, the derived functors and the complete physics program are not all formalized
by these components. Their theorem types and axiom dependencies are printed in the verification
logs.
The next load-bearing research task is an estimate for the remaining low-gcd original arithmetic
kernel, or an independently constructed positive arithmetic determinant family escaping the precisely
stated finite-kernel obstructions. A learned ToricGT-style component may propose a family, choose
a search ordering or discover a candidate identity. Acceptance still requires exact algebra, analytic
estimates, Lean interfaces where available and independent critical review. A fitted zero picture or
a small training loss is an observation, not the desired proof.

37.7

Primitive conductors, deleted factors and the scalar contour

37.7.1

Constructing the column character without losing its zeros

Let α6 = u. Since K contains µ6 , the extension K(α)/K is abelian, with character g 7→ g(α)/α. At
p ∤ 6u, arithmetic Frobenius gives the residue u(N p−1)/6 , exactly the source’s sextic symbol. Abelian
reciprocity makes it a finite-order Hecke character; multiply by ν and take its primitive inducing
character. The classical reciprocity input is [89, Theorem 0.8 and Chapter I,§1]. The finite-order
archimedean character is trivial, since C× is connected.
At p ∤ 6, write u = π e v with v a local unit. A sixth root of v lies in an unramified extension:
its residue root is separable and lifts by Hensel’s lemma. Inertia acts on a sixth root of π through
µ6 , so on α the inertia character has order 6/ gcd(6, e). It is trivial exactly when 6 | e; its square is
trivial exactly when 3 | e. A nontrivial tame one-dimensional character has conductor exponent
one. Since ν is unramified outside S, it cannot cancel this inertia there. At primes above 6, the
groups Kp× /(Kp× )6 are finite; their finitely many characters have a uniform maximum conductor.
Consequently
Y

Qu ≪ν N u.
p|u, p∤6

The fixed ideal cν includes the wild and fixed ν factors. No assertion identifies fu with the larger
full zero modulus.

/ T put z = ψu (p)(N p)−s . Its squarefree factor 1 + z = (1 − z 2 )/(1 − z) proves the
ratio using the same deleted set in numerator and denominator. Restoring the primitive Euler
factors yields (37.3). At a prime where the primitive square becomes unramified its value cannot be
obtained by squaring the sentinel zero ψu (p) = 0. For principal ν, u = π 3 has a quadratic Kummer
character whose primitive square is globally principal. Its local value in that square is one, not zero.
For u = π 6 both primitive characters are principal, although the full family still deletes p. These
two cases prove the local table directly.

The fixed-field ideal count is OK (X); dyadic summation proves the last inequality for sufficiently

up to the arbitrarily small losses. It remains positive for every 0 < δ < 1/2. Thus this scalar
convexity argument alone supplies no short-row saving. This is a limitation of the estimate just
derived, not an obstruction to averaging the numerators over Kummer rows, retaining signed
coefficients, or exploiting covariance with type II.
The seminorm uniformity is not a proof of the original scale supremum. For each independently
chosen scale D′ the displayed outer argument requires U 2 ≤ D′ . If a fixed cutoff is retained while
D′ shrinks, then eventually even D′ ≤ U : the short 2aU term in the Vaughan identity can meet the
support and must be restored. Neither that term nor the covariance with type II may be omitted in
a supremum or reassembly.

37.7.4

Verification boundaries

HeckeTypeI.lean checks seven actual complex local/finite-product statements, including independent primitive coefficient arrays and their full deleted-factor restoration. It does not identify
an ideal Hecke series with an abstract finite product or formalize a Mellin contour. The exact
checker includes split and inert prime ideals, actual sextic phases, conductor drops, sixth-power
rows and original mask zeros. The analytic theorem above uses classical abelian reciprocity, Hecke
continuation/functional equation and standard complex-analytic tools as external inputs. Full
ideal-character/formal analytic objects, the original signed low-gcd mean square and every GRH
completion obligation remain open.
